### Three more rules that fall out of the live walkthrough  
  
**The target does not always average.** *"Sometimes the target will not average  
properly"* — because the leg above may be large and the one below small, or the  
reverse. When it does not average at the 50, **look at the next line above the  
50** and trade to that instead. This is the first time he admits the 0.25/0.5  
pair can fail to produce a usable target, and gives the fallback.  
  
**No repeat trade at a level already traded.** *"Do not take repeated trades at  
this same place again"* — his reason is mechanical: each time the market buys up  
through a level and then breaks back down through it, so the second attempt at  
the same price is not the same trade. **Wait for the NEXT boundary.**  
  
**If the new low sits above the previous low, do not start.** The reference has  
to have been broken, not approached.  
  
**And the drawing discipline, repeated:** draw the boundary first, then the  
fibonacci on the V pattern; only **0, 1, 2** on the boundary fib — *"25, 50, 75  
must not be placed"* (Day27p1's rule, said again); and the chart must end up  
looking **clean**, which he treats as a test of whether you have drawn it right.  
  
The Bitcoin worked example needs **three** candles to count the pattern  
(*"three must come"*), not two — worth noting against Day21's two-V rule, which  
this may be restating with the head counted.  
  
### What Day29 part 1 adds  
  
| Rule | Testable as stated? |  
|---|---|  
| **T41** | The distance from entry to target must be **above 1%**, because the exit takes a QUARTER of it and costs are ~0.2%. 1% leaves 5 paise in a rupee; 2% leaves 30 | **Yes, and it is a hard filter** — reject any campaign whose 0.25 target is under 1% of price |  
| **T42** | Enter only if the market has **actually corrected by ~1%** from the reference — a drawn level with no real correction behind it is not a trade | Yes |  
| **T43** | **No repeat trade at a level already traded.** Wait for the next boundary | Yes |  
| **T44** | When the target does not average sensibly, take the **next line above the 50**, not the 0.25/0.5 pair | Partly — "does not average sensibly" needs a number |  
  
**T41 IS THE ANSWER TO WHY OUR LADDER EARNS SO LITTLE.** Measured on 26-Sep-2026:  
the median 0.25 target in the NIFTY run was **0.221% of price** — and his floor is  
1%. We have been taking, by his arithmetic, the trade he tells the class not to  
take. The mother candles we mark are four to five times too small.  
  
And this reframes the min-unit sweep run the same day. Raising the mother's  
minimum range cut the sample to almost nothing (20 campaigns at 80 points, 6 at  
150, 2 at 400) — which now looks less like a dead end and more like the evidence  
that **NIFTY's 5-minute structure is simply too small for this method.** His own  
instruments are gold and Bitcoin, where a pattern spans percents. T41 says the  
instrument has to move in percents or the method cannot pay for itself.  
  
## Day29 part 2 (LIVE) — why there is no stop loss, and it is not a maths claim  
  
The risk lesson, and it contains **the most important concession in the course**  
alongside the plainest statement of why his system is built the way it is.  
  
### The real reason for no stop loss: CONFIDENCE ARITHMETIC  
  
> *"Do you know what the big plus is in a trade with no stop loss? You keep  
> getting psychologically stronger, because your confidence keeps increasing. If  
> you cut four trades and take the fifth as a winner — your overall confidence is  
> four times DOWN and only one time UP."*  
  
That is the whole argument. T11 ("no stop loss") has been sitting in this  
playbook as though it were a claim about expectancy. **It is not. It is a claim  
about the trader's psychology**, and he states it as one. A ladder that never  
books a loss never damages the operator; a stop-loss system that wins 20% of the  
time damages him four times for every repair.  
  
**And he concedes the other side, explicitly:** *"even trading with a stop loss  
there are plenty of beneficial things in it — I am not saying there aren't."*  
  
**This matters enormously for what we can and cannot test.** A backtest cannot  
measure operator confidence. So the central rule of the system is, by his own  
framing, **outside the reach of any backtest we run** — which explains why every  
control we ran on the ladder came back indistinguishable. We have been testing  
the P\&L of a rule whose stated purpose is psychological.  
  
### Debt is the thing he is actually warning about  
  
The strongest language in the whole course, and he says it is from experience:  
  
> *"The price you are going to pay for debt — take it as your life."*  
> *"It will not let go until it has taken your life, because it is that  
> dangerous."*  
  
The mechanic he draws is the doubling ladder: *"it is only two rupees"* → 4 → 8 →  
16 → 32, and *"within six months you are standing there a big debtor"*, eight or  
ten lakh. And the trap: everyone around you looks fine, *"but you are the only  
one who knows what danger you are in."*  
  
**Start small, deliberately.** On a ₹1 lakh salary, do not begin with ₹2 lakh —  
begin with **₹10,000**. *"How much you start with does not matter. How much  
EXECUTION PERFECTION there is, that is what matters."* Three years of that beats  
the commitment you were trying to shortcut.  
  
**Brokers as the counterparty to your impatience.** You will hunt the one who  
gives 10x, then 20, 50, 100, 1000. He explains why the broker allows it: the  
₹1,000 account vanishing is his business model — the SIM-card operator earns  
₹200 crore from tiny amounts across fifty lakh people, and *"from you he will  
take twenty or thirty thousand crore."*  
  
**And the structural claim, said flatly:** *"the stock market is a casino, a  
gambling club — its very structure is like that... the most terrible gambling  
there is, is the stock market"* — unless you do it correctly, and *"99% cannot."*  
  
**Do not go live while still in demo.** *"Going to the original while you are  
still in the demo period"* — at most six months and you will fail, and *"I will  
write it down for you."*  
  
**What options and futures are actually FOR:** *"What is an option for? So that  
the loss does not come to an asset you own. What is a future for? To TAKE  
DELIVERY."* The wedding analogy: a futures contract is something you are  
obliged to take. Using either to speculate is using the wrong tool.  
  
### What Day29 part 2 adds  
  
| Rule | Testable as stated? |  
|---|---|  
| **T45** | The reason for no stop loss is **psychological, not mathematical** — cut 4 and win 1 and confidence is 4x down, 1x up. He also concedes stop-loss trading has real benefits | **NO — and that is the finding.** It is outside any backtest |  
| **T46** | Trade **your own capital only**. Measure what your capital yields and trade that. Debt compounds 2-4-8-16 and reaches lakhs in six months | Not a backtest rule; it is the constraint every result must respect |  
| **T47** | Options exist to protect an asset you hold; futures exist to take delivery | Consistent with T40 — both are the wrong instrument for a ladder |  
  
**T45 changes what we should be claiming.** Every ladder control we have run  
(26-Sep-2026) asked "does the no-stop ladder beat its alternative on P\&L" and got  
"indistinguishable". By his own account that was the wrong question.  
  
## Day29 part 3 (LIVE) — time buys you money: the higher the clock, the LESS capital  
  
Short, and it contains the one rule in the course that our own backtest  
discovered independently the same day.  
  
### The trade-off, in his words  
  
> *"If you avoid the 5-minute, the trade only comes at 15 minutes. Avoid that,  
> and it only comes at 1 hour. So as the TIME increases, THIS comes down"* — and  
> he names it: the investment *"falls to 50, to 30."*  
  
**As the timeframe rises, the CAPITAL REQUIRED FALLS.** Time is substitutable for  
money, and that is the mechanism behind Part 4's whole "7 to 10x" claim.  
  
**Measured the same day, before this file was read**, on `--scale swing` versus  
`--scale intraday`: net +10,640 on **222,000 deployed** against +11,809 on  
**1,167,000 deployed** — the same money earned on a fifth of the capital. He  
states as a rule the thing the run had just shown. That is the strongest  
independent corroboration anything in this course has received from our data.  
  
### The 30/70 rule of movement — and the money is in the 70  
  
> *"The actual fast movement in the market is only 30 percent. Seventy percent it  
> is inside a RANGE... and where it settles, that is where our money is."*  
  
So the ladder is **a range-harvesting method**, not a trend method, and he says  
the profit lives in the quiet 70%. That reframes every "the campaign held 126  
days" complaint: the hold IS the trade.  
  
### The rest  
  
**One trade a week is enough.** *"You will only get one trade a week. That is  
enough"* — and it beats everything you would have done sitting at the screen all  
day, because of *"the tax that comes with it, the brokerage that comes with it."*  
  
**Look once every one or two hours.** Not every five minutes.  
  
**The negative-mindset test.** Two students ask about the same range: one asks  
whether to *avoid* two trades and take less, the other whether to *add a lot*.  
*"That is a negative mindset... your question itself shows who you are."* He  
says he lets two people talk for two minutes and knows.  
  
**Two rabbits.** A dog chasing two rabbits catches neither. One target.  
**Patience like a stone dropped in water** — *"do not keep peeping up."*  
  
**And the gold warning against assuming a fall must recover:** from 1980 to 1998  
it went nowhere, *"70 percent"* — said to a student who was sure a drop would  
come back.  
  
### What Day29 part 3 adds  
  
| Rule | Testable as stated? |  
|---|---|  
| **T48** | **Higher timeframe, LESS capital.** Avoiding the 5m pushes the trade to 15m then 1H, and the investment falls "to 50, to 30" | **Yes — and it is CONFIRMED.** Measured 26-Sep-2026: same net on a fifth of the capital |  
| **T49** | Only **~30% of the time does the market travel; ~70% it ranges**, and the money is made where it settles | Yes — measure the fraction of bars inside the prior range |  
| **T50** | **One trade a week is enough**, and it beats many small ones once tax and brokerage are counted | Yes, and it agrees with T41 |  
  
**T48 is the first rule of his we have confirmed rather than merely failed to  
refute.** T49 is the next thing worth measuring, and it is cheap arithmetic.  
  
## Day31 (parts 1–3) — PROFESSIONAL FUND MANAGEMENT: the chapter the ladder actually is  
  
Three files, and together they are the most mechanically precise thing in the  
course. **This is the chapter our ladder has been trying to model all along, and  
it corrects two things we implemented on 26-Sep-2026.**  
  
### The frame: two systems, one difference  
  
> *"Everything you do in this, you must do in that too. The difference is: THERE  
> you put a stop loss, HERE you don't. That is the whole matter."*  
  
The intraday chapter is **active income** with a stop. This chapter is **passive  
income** without one — *"earning money while lying down"*, and *"you will sit only  
about fifteen minutes a day; the rest is all advance orders."* So T11 is not a  
universal rule of his: it is what distinguishes the passive book from the active  
one.  
  
**Candle / pattern / trend are three HORIZONS, not three methods:** *"trend is  
like long term, pattern is mid term, candle is short term"* — and all three live  
inside the same drawing: *"inside this there is a trend, inside this a candle,  
inside this a pattern, so all three trades come from here."* You do not draw the  
V separately.  
  
### THE THREE FEARS — and your entry goes where each one lives  
  
He asks the class where a retailer is afraid, and builds the ladder on the answer:  
  
| | The retailer's fear | Where your money goes |  
|---|---|---|  
| 1 | *"It's going up — should I buy, should I buy"* (missing out) | the **first** tranche |  
| 2 | *"I bought too much, the stop loss is about to be hit"* | the **second** |  
| 3 | *"The market is breaking down fast"* | the **third** |  
  
> *"Wherever they are afraid — that is where your entry must be."*  
  
And the reassurance that the ladder is supposed to hurt: *"when the big rich  
accumulate, their hand gets burnt two times, four times."* Being wrong two to  
four times inside one campaign is the design, not a failure.  
  
### THE GEOMETRY, and it is NOT our 0/1/2/4/8  
  
This is stated twice, in parts 1 and 3, with numbers:  
  
| Leg | Where | Size |  
|---|---|---|  
| 1 | at the level itself | **20%** |  
| 2 | **where your stop loss would have been** | **30%** |  
| 3 | at **twice that distance** | **50%** |  
  
*"Your stop loss — which place would it be at? At that place put the second, 30  
percent... then at twice that distance, 50 percent."* And in part 3: *"if the  
total is 10,000 put 2,000 here... then at twice the distance, 50 percent. That is  
all — place those three orders."*  
  
**So the second leg sits exactly where a stop-loss trader gets stopped out.** The  
ladder is designed to buy the retailer's stop. That is a far more specific and  
more testable geometry than the boundary ladder we modelled, and it explains the  
20/30/50 split (T3) which we had mapped onto arbitrary price levels.  
  
### THE RESERVE RULE, finally as arithmetic  
  
The thing Part 4 only gestured at:  
  
> *"If you should have put 1,000 at the first boundary and only placed 500, the  
> remaining 500 JOINS the second boundary."*  
  
So at the second: its own 1,000 **plus** the 500 carried = 1,500. Unspent again,  
and the third holds 3,000. *"Because it is at under value we put a lot of money  
there — and that is what is safe."*  
  
**Money not deployed at a shallow level is not lost; it accumulates downward.**  
The deeper price goes, the more money is waiting for it. Our model simply capped  
each timeframe's fund and let the unspent part sit idle.  
  
### THE MINIMUM LOT IS THE CORRECTION FILTER  
  
The most practical rule in the chapter, and it is a beautiful piece of design:  
  
> *"You cannot buy 80 rupees' worth? Then the market has not corrected to that  
> extent. AVOID that entry."*  
  
If the broker's minimum order will not fit inside your tranche at that price, the  
correction is not deep enough to trade. **The instrument's own minimum size  
enforces T42 for free** — no percentage test needed. He shows it failing at 40 and  
60 rupees and only becoming placeable at twice the distance, where 340 is  
available. He also enables 1:10 margin on screen purely so the minimum lot fits,  
and says so explicitly — it is for the demonstration, not for leverage.  
  
### THE TWO EXITS USE TWO DIFFERENT REFERENCES  
  
> *"We put 0.25 on the AVERAGE, and 0.5 on the OVERALL. Putting both, the profit  
> comes."*  
  
**Our code measures both from the average.** The 0.5 is measured on the overall  
depth, not on the book's average entry — which makes the second exit a  
structurally different target, not simply a further one.  
  
### The rest, briefly  
  
**A fast fall is good for us** — *"if it went down fast that is very good for  
us"*, because the accumulation pressure is there.  
  
**Do NOT rest all the deeper orders in advance.** *"Don't go leaving all hundred  
orders sitting below — place them AFTER the 25 has filled."* Sequenced, not  
pre-loaded, which sits oddly beside "15 minutes a day, the rest is advance  
orders" and is the sharper of the two statements.  
  
**Never size DOWN.** *"You must not put ten here and three there"* — then you  
cannot reach your profit. Third confirmation of T37.  
  
**Three things you invest: TIME, EFFORT, MONEY.** *"You must put in time. You must  
put in effort. You must put in money... without putting in effort you will get  
nothing."* Since time is what we have most of, spend time and effort and save  
money. **The effort input is not in any backtest.**  
  
**His stated return: 3x to 5x normally, 5x to 7x when it goes well.**  
  
**One instrument at a time while learning** — *"do it only on Bitcoin first...  
don't be doing four or five at once."*  
  
**And trade RELAXED** — *"do it very relaxed, don't do it greedily"*, approach the  
market pattern by pattern, and stop drawing every dot and line.  
  
**He will not help you live.** *"I will not help with even one word when you are  
live"* — forty of you cannot sit in my car.  
  
### What Day31 adds  
  
| Rule | Testable as stated? |  
|---|---|  
| **T51** | Legs sit at **0, the STOP-LOSS distance, and 2x that distance**, sized 20/30/50 — the second leg buys the retailer's stop | **Yes, and it replaces our 0/1/2/4/8** |  
| **T52** | If the **minimum order size** will not fit the tranche, the market has not corrected — skip. The lot size IS the filter | Yes, and it needs no percentage |  
| **T53** | Entries go at the **three places retail is afraid**; expect the hand to be burnt 2–4 times per campaign | Partly — the three fears need numeric definitions |  
| **T54** | Unspent money at a shallow boundary **ACCUMULATES into the deeper one** | **Yes** — and our model let it sit idle |  
| **T55** | **0.25 is measured on the AVERAGE; 0.5 on the OVERALL depth** — two different references | **Yes, and our code has both on the average** |  
| **T56** | Do not rest the deeper orders in advance; place each **after** the shallower fills | Yes |  
| **T57** | Three inputs: time, **effort**, money. Return 3–5x normally, 5–7x when it runs | No — effort is unmodellable |  
  
**T51, T54 and T55 are three concrete corrections to `ladder_master_backtest.py`,  
and all three are cheap to make.** T51 changes where the legs go, T54 changes how  
much is available when price gets there, T55 changes where the book leaves. Every  
ladder number produced on 26-Sep-2026 predates all three.  
  
## Day32 (parts 1–2) — the minimum that makes a level tradeable, and the carry in his own numbers  
  
A live walkthrough of the ladder, and it turns two rules from guesses into numbers.  
  
### T52 finally has a threshold  
  
> *"At one place it only comes to 80 rupees... if it does not come above a minimum  
> 100 for that place, I will not even work that place — it means the market has  
> not corrected yet."* And: *"I always keep it so it is above 100."*  
  
So a leg must be worth **at least ~₹100 on his working scale**, and the boundary  
should span about **2%**, or the level is not worked at all. *"There is no rule  
that we must sit and keep trading."*  
  
**And the minimum GROWS with the timeframe:** *"when we make the next buy we need  
a minimum of one thousand rupees."* ₹100 at the small clock, ₹1,000 at the next.  
  
### T54, in his own arithmetic  
  
> *"I should have done 20 at this place. I didn't do the 20 — so this 20 plus 30  
> together."*  
  
Exactly the carry the Day31 file described, now watched happening. It also shows  
**why** our implementation was inert: the 20 went unplaced because it was *below  
the ₹100 minimum*, which is T52. The two rules are one mechanism.  
  
### Three more entry rules  
  
**Do not stack a second buy in a zone you already hold.** *"If I already have a  
buy here, I don't need a buy beyond this, so I did not take this buy."*  
  
**Entries go on the RETURN, not on the way down.** *"When its reversal comes back,  
I place the buy order."*  
  
**Take the deeper leg only if the value below beats the value above.** *"If taking  
that buy below gives more value than the one above — then I take it. Otherwise I  
don't. That is the simple rule."* He names the alternative **"retailer style"**:  
buying where the distance between your upper and lower buys is large.  
  
### The exits, and a check on our own code  
  
**Partial close when the 50% stalls.** *"It went to 50% and couldn't go higher...  
waiting above the 50 is foolishness"* — take the profit that is visibly near.  
  
> **And the line to test ourselves against: *"the overall 50% and the 25% come  
> CLOSE TOGETHER."***  
  
**Our T55 implementation makes them far apart** — swapping the second exit from  
the average to the overall depth cost 0.56% → 0.36% and halved the campaign  
count. If his two targets nearly coincide and ours diverge, our geometry is  
wrong somewhere. **This is a falsifiable check on the code, not on him**, and it  
is cheap: measure the gap between the two targets per campaign.  
  
**Why the 25 sits on the average:** *"even if we bought wrongly, I will come out  
at the 50 and we get profit — that is why we keep 25 on the average."* The  
average line is the safety net for a badly placed ladder.  
  
**The watch schedule:** five minutes every two days, twice a week; *"twice a  
month you get one big four-hour setup."*  
  
*(The file opens with a long Dubai/dual-citizenship/tax digression — 25 lakh  
minimum, 180 days residency. Not strategy; noted so nobody re-reads it looking  
for one.)*  
  
### What Day32 adds  
  
| Rule | Testable as stated? |  
|---|---|  
| **T58** | A leg must be worth at least a floor amount (~₹100 at his scale) and the boundary ~2%, else the market has not corrected — skip. The floor GROWS with the timeframe | **Yes** — and this is what makes T54 live |  
| **T59** | Never stack a second buy in a zone already held | Yes |  
| **T60** | Take a deeper leg only if the value below exceeds the value above; the opposite is "retailer style" | Yes |  
| **T61** | Partial-close when price reaches the 50% and stalls rather than waiting for more | Yes |  
| **T62** | **The overall-50% and average-25% targets land CLOSE TOGETHER** | **Yes — and it is a check on OUR code** |  
  
## Day33 part 1 — THE CAPITAL FORMULA, at last  
  
The single most important quantitative statement in the entire course, and every  
sizing rule up to now has been circling it.  
  
### capital ÷ 100 = the money for one percent of distance  
  
> *"Capital is the capital you bring yourself. If you have one lakh, then divided  
> by 100... what the capital-divided-by-100 formula is for: how much your capital  
> spends for ONE PERCENTAGE."*  
  
| Capital | Money per 1% of distance |  
|---|---|  
| ₹10,000 | ₹100 |  
| ₹50,000 | ₹500 |  
| ₹1,00,000 | ₹1,000 |  
  
> **SIZE = (capital ÷ 100) × the distance in percent.**  
  
*"That's all — with that the calculation is finished. After that, how much the  
REAL DISTANCE is in the market, that is what we..."*  
  
**And it retro-explains Day31.** That file's worked example was *"0.39 — so 390  
rupees"*, which never made sense against a ₹1,000 account. On ₹1 lakh:  
1,00,000/100 = 1,000 per percent, × 0.39 = **390.** Exact. The formula was being  
used two files before it was stated.  
  
### The percentage budget is SHARED across timeframes and must not double-count  
  
This is the reserve rule's true form, and it is a subtraction:  
  
> *"Already inside this distance we gave 1% on the 5-minute. Now the overall  
> distance on the 1-hour is 5%. So we already took 1 — we take the other 4."*  
  
And the constraint that makes it binding:  
  
> *"Within a distance that is 5%, how can you put 7% of money?"*  
  
**The total money is bounded by the total DISTANCE, and each timeframe gets the  
percentage the smaller clocks have not already consumed inside it.** Our model  
gave each timeframe an independent fixed share (10/20/30/40) and let them overlap  
freely. That is not a simplification of his rule — it is a different rule.  
  
### The 20/30/50, tied to percentages  
  
> *"In these two percent — 20 percent... then 30 percent... and where is the  
> remaining 50? The total distance is two... it goes to 4 percent, and only then  
> does the remaining 50 percent get invested."*  
  
So on a 2% structure the first two legs deploy inside it, and **the 50% leg only  
invests when the distance doubles to 4%** — which is T51's "twice the distance"  
restated in percent rather than in price. Two chapters, same geometry.  
  
**Also:** avoid an inside bar; draw the boundary fib with −1 and 200.  
  
### What Day33 part 1 adds  
  
| Rule | Testable as stated? |  
|---|---|  
| **T63** | **SIZE = (capital ÷ 100) × distance-in-percent.** ₹1 lakh spends ₹1,000 per 1% | **Yes, and it replaces every sizing guess we have made** |  
| **T64** | The percentage budget is **shared and non-overlapping** across timeframes: the 1H gets its span MINUS what the 5m already consumed. You cannot put 7% of money into a 5% distance | **Yes — and it replaces FUND_SHARE entirely** |  
| **T65** | Avoid inside bars | Yes |  
  
**T63 and T64 together are a complete position-sizing system**, stated in his own  
numbers, and they make `FUND_SHARE = {1: 0.10, 5: 0.20, 15: 0.30, 60: 0.40}` —  
a table I invented — obsolete. Every ladder result so far sized its legs by a  
guess. This is the third time in one day that reading further has replaced an  
assumption with his arithmetic (after T41 and T51).  
  
## Day33 part 2 + Day34 — **30/70 REPLACES 20/30/50**, and the win-rate claim  
  
The file is literally named `30_70`, and Day34 confirms the class has switched:  
  
> *"You were doing 20, 30, 50 — ask them in their own words. NOW WE DO 30, 70."*  
  
**So the three-leg ladder is the intermediate teaching and TWO legs is the final  
system.** Every result in this playbook up to Day32 — and every line of  
`ladder_master_backtest.py` written on 26-Sep-2026, including the T51 fix — sizes  
three legs. That is the earlier version of his method.  
  
### The two places, and they are named  
  
> *"How far the SELLER brought it — that line is here. How far the BUYER took it  
> — that line is here. Thirty percent at this place and seventy percent at this  
> place."*  
  
And the lower one: *"as far as the buyer took it, we keep TWICE that distance  
below."* Worked example: ₹500 total → **₹150 at the first, ₹350 at the second.**  
Exactly 30/70. Two orders, *"like the buyer came 2 times, we place 2 times."*  
  
**The first order's ceiling:** *"it can only go up until it breaks the head. Beyond  
that, no."*  
  
### The claim, and it is falsifiable  
  
| | Before | With 30/70 |  
|---|---|---|  
| Profit multiple | 2–3x | **5–7x** |  
| Winning trades per 100 | **40–60** | **80** |  
| Capital risk | — | *"halves"* |  
  
*"Earlier if we looked at 100 times we would see profit only 40 to 60 times, the  
other 40 loss. Here you can see 80 times in green, only 20 negative."*  
  
**A win rate of 80% is a hard, testable number** — and the highest-value claim in  
the course, because it is the one a backtest can settle outright.  
  
### And the English line he says in English  
  
> *"One step back and stay away from the crowd."* — *"We never go into that crowd  
> at all. Somebody buys, somebody's stop loss gets hit. For me, entry only when it  
> comes to THIS place."*  
  
### The rest of Day34  
  
**Rest LIMIT orders, not stop orders.** He corrects a student for placing the  
ladder as stop orders. Our model fills at a level, which is a limit — correct by  
accident.  
  
**Two legs then average then 0.25:** *"if two are bought, average them, and 0.25  
to the mother candle overall. That's all."*  
  
**Measure from the BIGGER leg, whoever made it:** *"whether what the buyer brought  
is bigger or what the seller brought is bigger — take the measurement from the  
big one."* And a **failed V is still usable** if it is the bigger structure, which  
softens Day15's "only the plain V".  
  
### THE QUALIFICATION THAT MATTERS MOST (Day34 part 1)  
  
> *"Should you approach a market that is simply coming down from above? NO — you  
> may approach only AFTER the BUYER'S INVOLVEMENT has appeared."*  
>  
> *"This system is correct. By fund management this is correct. **But technically  
> it is wrong.**"*  
  
**He says the ladder alone is technically wrong.** Fund management makes it  
survivable; it does not make the entry right. The missing precondition is  
evidence that buyers have entered — and our model has no notion of it at all.  
  
| Rule | Testable as stated? |  
|---|---|  
| **T66** | **The final sizing is 30/70 across TWO legs** — 30% at the seller's line, 70% at twice the buyer's distance below. 20/30/50 is superseded | **Yes, and it invalidates our three-leg sizing** |  
| **T67** | 30/70 claims **80 wins per 100** (from 40–60), 5–7x profit, half the risk | **YES — the most falsifiable claim in the course** |  
| **T68** | Never approach a market merely falling; wait for **BUYER INVOLVEMENT**. Without it the ladder is "money-management correct, technically wrong" | Needs T72's definition |  
| **T69** | Measure from the **bigger** leg, buyer's or seller's; a failed V counts if it is the bigger structure | Yes |  
| **T70** | Rest **LIMIT** orders, never stop orders | Yes |  
  
## Day35 part 3 — if the 30 did not fill, there is no trade  
  
Short and it closes a hole in the 30/70:  
  
> *"If you could not place the 30% order, then there is NO TALK of the target at  
> all."*  
  
And when the 30 is missed but price reaches the 70:  
  
> *"Take the 70. Then when the next place comes, add that 30 into it."*  
> *(With the analogy: if you didn't eat yesterday, what do you do today?)*  
  
**That is T54's carry again, now in the 30/70 form** — the unplaced 30 is not lost,  
it joins the next boundary. And the amount going down is always the larger one,  
*"never smaller."*  
  
| Rule | Testable as stated? |  
|---|---|  
| **T71** | If the 30% leg did not fill, the campaign has no target — and a missed 30 carries into the NEXT boundary | Yes |  
  
## Day37 — the retest is certain, and the W is the accumulation signature  
  
**Price always comes back.** *"Whatever the place, the market will not go without  
retesting it. However many years it takes, it will not fail to come back."* He  
ties it to SMC's gap-fill idea and says that is what a retest is.  
  
**Price and the trendline are magnetically bound:** *"a trendline and a price are  
like lovers — they will not stay far apart; like a magnet it will certainly pull  
toward the trendline."* So a fast, far move is itself the reason to expect return.  
  
**THE W BOTTOM IS WHERE THE BIG MONEY ACCUMULATES.** *"In this W bottom you can  
see the market accumulating. When the market is going to settle it makes a W.  
This is the big money in the market coming in."*  
  
**That is the operational definition T68 was missing.** "Buyer involvement" = a W  
forming. It is a shape, and it is detectable.  
  
**Speed scales with the clock:** a daily zone arrives *"20 times the speed"* of a  
5-minute one, and *"three pushes, then a control candle appears fast."* He points  
at his own YouTube video, *"time into speed."*  
  
| Rule | Testable as stated? |  
|---|---|  
| **T72** | **A W bottom is the accumulation signature** — the operational form of "buyer involvement" (T68) | **Yes, and it is a shape test** |  
| **T73** | Every level gets retested eventually; price is magnetically bound to the trendline, so distance implies return | Yes |  
| **T74** | Speed scales with the clock — a daily zone arrives ~20x faster than a 5-minute one | Yes |  
  
## Day38 — every instrument has a measurable nature, and no symmetry  
  
**Bitcoin, quantified by him:**  
  
| | |  
|---|---|  
| Average daily move | **2–3%** |  
| A 5–10% move | 2–4 times a month |  
| A 10–20% move | **once in ~3 months**, not monthly |  
| A vertical move like today's | once every **50–100 days** |  
  
> *"Every script has its own kind of NATURE."*  
  
**Measure the instrument before sizing it** — which is exactly the step our NIFTY  
work skipped, and it is why T41's 1% rule kept failing on 5-minute NIFTY  
structure.  
  
**AND NO SYMMETRY:** *"It fell this much, won't it rise that much? It will not rise  
like that."* A drawdown of X implies nothing about a recovery of X.  
  
**Speed by timeframe, again with multiples:** breaking the 15-minute takes **2x**  
the speed of the 5-minute, the 1-hour **3x**, the next **4x**.  
  
**And you do not need to know which cycle you are in** — *"we don't need to worry  
when which happens"*; mark the chart, place the orders, and an unexpected move  
needs no hand-wringing. *"Only the time investment is ours."*  
  
| Rule | Testable as stated? |  
|---|---|  
| **T75** | Each instrument has a **measurable nature** (BTC: 2–3% daily, 5–10% 2–4x/month, 10–20% once in 3 months). Measure it BEFORE sizing | **Yes, and it should precede any test** |  
| **T76** | **A fall of X does not imply a rise of X.** No symmetry | Yes |  
  
## Day41 — the money falls as the method rises  
  
The cleanest quantification of Part 4's "time buys money", and it is per METHOD:  
  
| Method | Money invested |  
|---|---|  
| **Candle** | **70%** |  
| **Pattern** | **50%** |  
| **Trend** | **30%** |  
  
> *"When doing it on the candle, how much risk is in the candle? 70 percent money  
> investment. On the pattern, 50 percent. On the trend, 30 percent."*  
  
So the ladder of methods **is** a ladder of capital, descending. This is T48  
(higher timeframe, less capital) restated as a table — and it agrees with the  
measurement of 26-Sep-2026, where the swing scale earned the same money on a  
fifth of the capital.  
  
**Multiple entries are not optional:** without a multiple-entry system a market  
like S-Bank traps you and cannot come back up.  
  
**When price sits under value without forming the 50% structure, take multiple  
trades there** rather than waiting for a structure that is not coming.  
  
**And every instrument is a different car:** *"all are cars, but each car has its  
own nature — some run well on road, some in desert, some in water."*  
  
**If the position exceeded your budget, the fund management was wrong**, not the  
market.  
  
| Rule | Testable as stated? |  
|---|---|  
| **T77** | Money by METHOD: **candle 70%, pattern 50%, trend 30%** — the higher method commits less | **Yes, and it quantifies T48** |  
| **T78** | A single-entry system is insufficient; multiple entries are required, and under value without a 50% structure calls for multiple trades | Partly |  
  
## Day14 — the base entry, and what protects it  
  
An earlier file, banked late. Its content is the **base entry**: the first entry  
of a book, and the fact that the traditional method protects it with a stop loss  
while this one protects it with the entries that follow. Risk is framed against  
total capital (his example: ₹90,000 of a total), and the symmetry rule —  
*"they bring it back down the same amount"* — is stated as the basic rule the  
later boundary work rests on. No new testable rule that Day15 onward does not  
state more precisely.  
  
## Day39 — margin, debt, and what the retailer's balance sheet actually looks like  
  
**The professional and the retailer hold opposite balance sheets.** *"The rich man  
keeps only 10% in the contract."* The retailer is the mirror image — *"only 10% sits  
in the asset, 200% sits in the contract, in futures, in options, and that is why  
they all take a loss."* The asset is the ballast; the contract is the bet. Reverse  
the ratio and you reverse the outcome.  
  
**His actual cost of money, stated as rates:** pledging your own holdings costs  
about **6%**; borrowing in a hurry costs **11%**; and if the broker simply hands  
your own money back to you he still takes about **5%**. On a 1 lakh position the  
margin desk asks **20–30%** up front.  
  
**The one condition under which he will borrow:** *"I take a loan at 7% interest,  
and if I invest it as an asset and it is going to give me 20%, then I may borrow."*  
Borrow only when the spread is roughly 3x the interest — never to fund a contract.  
  
**Why the impatient buyer is destroyed:** *"the market corrects 20%, he buys at  
20,000, and when it sells it sells at 4,000, at 3,000 — he will not have the  
patience for it."* The correction is not the risk. The absence of patience is.  
  
| Rule | Testable as stated? |  
|---|---|  
| **T79** | Professional/retailer inversion: ~10% in the contract and the rest in the asset, not 200% in the contract | Yes, as a position-sizing constraint |  
| **T80** | Borrow only when the expected return is ~3x the interest (7% borrowed against 20% expected), and never for a contract | Yes |  
| **T81** | Cost-of-money ladder: pledge ~6%, emergency ~11%, broker's own-money float ~5%, margin call 20–30% of position | Reference numbers, not a rule |  
  
## Day40 — the honest number, and who gets funded  
  
**HE NAMES THE PROFESSIONAL RETURN, AND IT IS SMALL.** Speaking of the people who  
actually get client money to manage: *"the 4%, 7% he talks about — 'that is not  
enough for us'. If you think that, they will not fund you."* The greedy are filtered  
out precisely because they reject single-digit returns.  
  
**This is the most important sentence in the course for anyone backtesting it.**  
Every measurement in this playbook that lands at 4–8% a year is landing exactly  
where he says a professional lands. A configuration that claims far more is not  
better — it is a warning that the test is wrong.  
  
**The sizing arithmetic, restated in rupees:** *"keep 100 rupees as what you must  
buy. Can you split it and buy 25? You cannot, sir. This much market moves 2%, 3%  
...there is 4%; for 4 you must buy 4,000, buy 1,000."* One lakh spends **1,000 per  
1%** — T63 again, from a different direction, and the reason a single buy can never  
be a quarter of the account.  
  
**The filter he expects:** of forty people in the community, he reckons ten will do  
it correctly, and *"I will not have to tell you to leave — you will disappear on  
your own. The market will chase you out."* And: *"greed — God himself cannot save a  
greedy man."*  
  
**The gate on the teaching itself:** *"only if you do investment correctly will I  
teach trading."*  
  
| Rule | Testable as stated? |  
|---|---|  
| **T82** | **The professional's return is 4–7% a year.** Rejecting that as too small is the disqualifying trait | **This is the benchmark to judge every backtest against** |  
| **T83** | One lakh spends 1,000 per 1% of distance — a single buy is never a quarter of the account | Yes; identical to T63 |  
| **T84** | Investment competence precedes trading; trading is taught second | Not testable |  
  
## Day43 — the distance is a RATIO, and the reference point changes the answer  
  
**The whole session is one arithmetic lesson: measure the sub-leg as a FRACTION of  
the total fall, never as an absolute percent.** He measures the total drop, gets  
**0.78**, measures the near leg, gets **0.38**, and divides one into the other. On a  
bigger frame: the near leg is **12%**, the whole is **38%**, and he works the ratio  
out loud — *"38 by 12, how much? 26."*  
  
**And the warning that makes this necessary:** *"this high price is different, this  
price is different — when you take the percentage from THIS price the percentage  
grows; 30 percent of 80, then 30 percent of 60 — the percentage changes depending  
where you take it from."* So the same geometric level yields different percentages  
depending on the anchor. **Fix the anchor or the number is meaningless.**  
  
**Multiple angles, not one entry:** *"think from multiple angles — do not think the  
trade must be taken only here. Take it there, and a minor trade here as well."*  
Whether price goes up or down *"it takes time either way"*, so more than one entry  
point is how the time is used.  
  
| Rule | Testable as stated? |  
|---|---|  
| **T85** | **The distance is a RATIO — the sub-leg divided by the whole fall — not an absolute percentage** | Yes; this is what T34's fib floor already does, now with his arithmetic |  
| **T86** | The same level gives a different percentage from a different anchor, so the anchor must be fixed before measuring | Yes, and it is a real source of error |  
| **T87** | Multiple angles: a major entry AND a minor entry, because time passes either way | Yes; the multi-entry rule of T78 restated |  
  
## Day44 — the closing demo: the entry price is the LOWEST of three candidates  
  
**THE MOST PRECISE ENTRY RULE IN THE WHOLE COURSE, and it is stated once, at the  
end.** The buy goes at whichever of these three is LOWEST:  
  
1. **the current candle's 50%**  
2. **the overall reverse 0.25** — a quarter of the way back  
3. **the previous candle's low**  
  
*"the current candle's 50%, or this overall's reverse 25, 0.25, or the previous  
candle's low — whichever of the three is lower, take that one."*  
  
**This supersedes any single-source entry.** Earlier days give the previous-candle  
level (T21) and the quarter-back level (T10) separately; here he says to compute all  
three and take the lowest. It is strictly more conservative than any one of them.  
  
**Order placement and the discipline around it:** *"when it comes to its 0.25, place  
the buy order at that place"*, and the next order below it *"let it break"* first.  
Once placed, *"do not disturb any of those orders at all — leave them as they are"*,  
and at the deeper place *"place only the order the balance allows"*. A 2% order is  
named at one such level.  
  
**And where it will be cut:** *"wherever you drag it from, at its 25 the trade will  
be cut — at 0.25 it will certainly be cut."*  
  
| Rule | Testable as stated? |  
|---|---|  
| **T88** | **ENTRY = min(current candle's 50%, the 0.25 reverse, the previous candle's low)** — all three computed, the lowest taken | **Yes, and it is NOT what our model does** |  
| **T89** | Once an order is placed it is never moved; the deeper order waits for the level above to break first | Yes |  
| **T90** | Size the deeper order to the remaining balance, not to the plan | Yes; this is T54's carry seen from the other side |  
  
# THE PUBLIC "TECHNICAL — டெக்னிக்கல்" PLAYLIST (82 videos, 94.4 hours)  
  
Pulled 28-Sep-2026 from YouTube AUTO-CAPTIONS in Tamil, not whisper: 82 files in  
ten minutes instead of ~236 hours of machine time, 845,799 words. Quality is good  
enough for rule extraction (`yt-dlp --write-auto-subs --sub-langs ta`; the text  
lives in the lines carrying inline `\<c>` word timings — the plain lines are the  
same words triplicated by the rolling window).  
  
**This is a DIFFERENT body of material from the 53-video paid class.** Topic counts  
across all 82: candle 4,093 · buy 4,899 · pattern 1,065 · volume 498 · gap 404 ·  
support 377 · stop-loss 350 · breakout 270 — against **mother 20 (5 files),  
trendline 2, fibonacci 0**. So the ladder system is barely here; this is the  
public channel teaching candles, patterns and structure, WITH stop losses. Where  
the two disagree the paid class is the authority. Four rules here are new.  
  
## T91 — INVOLVEMENT AND STRENGTH ARE DIFFERENT THINGS  
  
*"The buyers only carried it 80 points — so buyer involvement IS there in the  
market. But it means the buyer's STRENGTH is decreasing."* And: *"the market is in  
the buyer's control, there is no argument about that. But the buyer's strength is  
getting less. THIS IS VERY IMPORTANT."*  
  
He uses an army analogy: a force that only advanced 80 km of the ground it used to  
take has fewer men left, whatever flag still flies.  
  
**This refines T68.** Buyer involvement (a W, a bounce) tells you buyers are  
present. It does NOT tell you they are winning. **Strength is the SIZE of each  
successive push**, and a series of smaller up-legs is a weakening buyer even while  
"involvement" keeps testing true.  
  
| Rule | Testable as stated? |  
|---|---|  
| **T91** | **Involvement ≠ strength.** Measure strength as the size of each successive buyer push; shrinking pushes = a weakening buyer even where involvement is present | **Yes — compare consecutive up-leg heights. We have never tested this** |  
  
## T92 — VALIDITY: A PATTERN EXPIRES, AND ITS LIFE IS SET BY ITS TIMEFRAME  
  
*"The more you raise the timeframe, the more time is invested, and its VALIDITY  
also increases."* Then the number: *"if you analyse on 5 minutes... its validity is  
only some 10 minutes, 15 minutes. After that its validity EXPIRES."*  
  
And the instruction that follows from it: *"if I analyse a trade and I need the  
validity time to be five hours, or one day, then you must go to the frame that  
suits it and analyse there."*  
  
**THIS INVALIDATES A WHOLE CLASS OF OUR BACKTESTS.** Every run that found structure  
on a 5-minute chart and then held the position for days was using a pattern long  
past its expiry. Measured 27-Sep-2026: the "intraday" configuration had a median  
hold of 32.7 hours and a 75th percentile of 163 hours off 5-minute structures —  
by this rule, hundreds of times the validity of the signal that opened it.  
  
**Choose the timeframe from the intended HOLD, not from the entry precision.**  
  
| Rule | Testable as stated? |  
|---|---|  
| **T92** | **A pattern's validity ≈ a few bars of its own timeframe and then expires**; pick the analysis timeframe from how long the trade must stay alive | **Yes, and it is a hard constraint on every result we have** |  
  
## T93 — VIOLENT HOURS: NIFTY HAS A TIME-OF-DAY MAP  
  
*"In NIFTY, 9:00 to 10:00 is very violent hours. Violent hours. Every place has  
these violent hours. After that the remaining hours are fully SILENT hours."* And  
the close: *"12 to... 3:15, 3:30 to three — again violent hours for NIFTY. All this  
is very important."* Then the equivalence: **"violent hours — you can also call  
them VOLUME hours."**  
  
He ties it to traffic: the road is busy at the same times every day.  
  
| Rule | Testable as stated? |  
|---|---|  
| **T93** | **NIFTY's violent (= volume) hours are ~09:15–10:00 and ~14:45–15:30; the middle of the session is silent.** Approach the market in the violent hours | **Yes, directly — and it is the first TIME-OF-DAY rule in the whole course** |  
  
## T94 — TAKEOVER: WHO HOLDS THE LEVEL, READ OFF THE CANDLE  
  
*"An OUTSIDE candle is sitting there — so you know the buyers took over."* And the  
reverse: *"the moment it cannot be held past this, automatically the seller takes  
over."* Confirmation point: *"right here, at the market's 50%, we can easily  
CONFIRM the sellers took over."* The limit of the buyers' reach defines the high:  
*"the maximum high means — up to here the buyers took over; beyond this they could  
not carry either body or wick."*  
  
And the caveat that makes it honest: *"the buyers took over, BUT there was not  
enough VOLUME to push it up."* Takeover without volume is a flag planted, not  
ground held — which is T91 again from the other side.  
  
Three candles where buyers take over and carry past the base make it an **extra V**  
(ties to T22's Equal / Failed / Extra V taxonomy).  
  
| Rule | Testable as stated? |  
|---|---|  
| **T94** | **Takeover is a control test at a level**: an outside candle, or carrying body/wick past the prior extreme, says who won — and it is only real if volume came with it | **Yes, and it is a sharper form of T68 than the W bottom** |  

---

# THE PUBLIC PLAYLIST, READ IN FULL — video by video

T91–T94 came from a keyword pass over all 82 files. From here each video is read end
to end, in `docs/playlist-transcripts/`, and every rule carries **video · timestamp** so
it can be checked at the second he says it. Auto-captions: words he only *shows* on
the chart are not in the text, and anything the caption does not settle is marked
*(unclear)*.

## Video 001 — candle, pattern, trend; layers; how far the bounce went

The course's opening lesson, and the vocabulary every later video assumes. Candle is
a letter, a pattern is a word, a trend is a sentence (00:05:11–00:07:12) — the same
candle → pattern → trend progression as Part 2.1 of the paid class, so that is now
corroborated from a second source. Three patterns only: **V, A and O** (00:03:39,
00:14:20). A V is red candles down then at least one green candle up (00:25:29).

## T95 — INSIDE AND OUTSIDE CANDLE: the close decides

*"If the next candle's CLOSE finished below this candle's body and wick, it is an
outside candle. The candle broke that candle's high or low and closed outside it —
confirmed — then that candle is an outside candle."* (001 · 00:07:44) And the
negative: *"these two did not close beyond the body or the wick — so these are inside
candles."* (001 · 00:08:15)

A wick through the level is not enough; **the close has to be beyond the previous
candle's high or low, wick included.** That is T1's close-not-touch rule applied to a
single candle, and it is the definition T94's "outside candle" was using without
stating it.

## T96 — LAYERS: count the green candles the buyer could hold

*"The market came back with only ONE green candle; the buyer could not carry it
further… the buyer could hold for just one candle, five minutes. We call that a
SINGLE LAYER."* (001 · 00:15:22) Then the count: *"one green candle is single layer,
more than one is double layer, more than three is multiple layer."* (001 · 00:22:57)
And why it matters: *"three layers means fifteen minutes — only if the buyer holds the
market can he put three layers. If the buyer took control for fifteen minutes, the
buyer is a little strong."* (001 · 00:21:26)

His car image (001 · 00:16:24): a light touch of the brake, a harder one, then a
hard stop — single, double, then multiple layers, and the low stops breaking.

*(unclear)* whether "double" is exactly two and "multiple" three-plus or four-plus;
he says both "more than three" and "three layers" for multiple.

## T97 — HOW FAR THE BOUNCE WENT, in quarters of the previous leg

*"Whatever came down from here, split it into four: 25%, 50%, 75%, 100%. Whether it is
a candle, a pattern or a trend — you MUST split it in four."* (001 · 00:10:48) Then the
reading: *"if it only went 25%, the seller still holds 75%; the buyer could not take it
past 25 and it came back down."* (001 · 00:20:56)

No indicators: *"split it in your head. We use no indicator."* (001 · 00:19:26)

## T98 — LAYERS WITHOUT DISTANCE: the buyer arrived, the volume did not

The rule that combines the two. *"Multiple layers — the buyer took over. But of the
whole high-to-low it only went 25%. The buyer came, but volume accumulation has not
come yet."* (001 · 00:23:57–00:24:29) And what follows: *"if an outside candle comes
beyond this, the market comes down."* (001 · 00:24:59)

His picture: a car in a ten-foot ditch three men can push out; the same car in a
fifty-foot ditch needs a crane. Many green candles that cover little ground are the
three men.

**This is T91 made measurable.** Strength = distance, as a fraction of the previous
leg; involvement = layers, as a candle count. He calls the pair *"time and speed"* —
*"how long it took and how far it went; that is the real result"* (001 · 00:21:57).

## T99 — A PATTERN IS OVER WHEN AN OUTSIDE CANDLE BREAKS ITS HEAD

*"If it breaks this head and an outside candle closes out there — that's it, it's
finished. The first V pattern's result is over. Announced."* (001 · 00:23:27)

The "head" of a V is its low (the point); of an A, its high. A pattern is live until a
candle **closes** beyond that point (T95), and then it is done — whatever its layers
said.

| Rule | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|
| **T95** | **Outside candle** = closes beyond the previous candle's high or low (wick included); otherwise **inside** | **Yes, exactly** |
| **T96** | **Layers** = consecutive green candles on a bounce: 1 single (weak), 2 double, 3+ multiple (buyer holding ≥15 min on 5m) | **Yes** — the 3-vs-4 boundary for "multiple" is *(unclear)* |
| **T97** | Measure every bounce in **quarters (25/50/75/100%) of the previous leg's high–low**; a 25% bounce leaves the seller in control | **Yes** |
| **T98** | **Multiple layers + ≤25% distance = buyers without volume**: expect the low to break on the next outside candle | **Yes — and it is T91 with numbers** |
| **T99** | A pattern ends when an **outside candle closes beyond its head** (a V's low, an A's high) | **Yes** |

## Video 002 — body and wick, whose outside candle, and a winner that stops

Recaps 001, then reads a real 5-minute chart candle by candle. His running image is a
tug-of-war: buyers and sellers pull one rope, and a side that stops pulling has lost
(00:06:07). Two reads he insists on together: the **individual** pattern and the
**overall** one it sits inside — never the individual alone (00:03:34).

## T100 — THE BODY BELONGS TO ITS COLOUR; THE WICK BELONGS TO THE OTHER SIDE

*"Whatever colour the body is, that belongs to them. The opposite — the wick — does not
belong to them. A red candle's body belongs to the seller, but its wick belongs to the
buyer."* (002 · 00:27:11) Why: inside that 5-minute red candle *"the first two or
three one-minute candles would have been green; only the last two or three closed
red"* (002 · 00:10:46) — the wick is the part the other side won and then lost.

So a move is only owned when the **body** gets there: *"only if the body goes is it
theirs; if only the wick peeps out, it belongs to the opposite side."* (002 · 00:28:13)
And the read that follows: *"only the wick went past the wick; the body could not.
Right here we can confirm the seller has taken 50% control."* (002 · 00:17:27)

## T101 — WHOSE OUTSIDE CANDLE: the side it closes on

T95 made precise by direction. *"If it closes on their own side it is the seller's
outside candle; on the opposite side, the buyer's outside candle."* (002 · 00:08:08)
The first buyer outside candle after a fall is the takeover sign — here with the
bounce already at 75% of the fall: *"two things are clear: the buyer has taken over
… it went 75 of the market's distance and put an outside candle."* (002 · 00:05:07–
00:05:37)

And the reverse, which he calls the point of no return: *"if the sellers put an
outside candle beyond the highest green candle the buyer made, they WILL break it
aggressively — because they get confidence."* (002 · 00:13:21)

## T102 — A WINNER THAT STOPS HAS STOPPED WINNING

*"Those who are carrying it must keep carrying it without thinking. If thirty minutes
have gone here, the buyer is thinking. That means many are leaving."* (002 ·
00:15:55) And the war image: *"whoever is winning must keep moving. If they stand still
in one place, they could not advance further."* (002 · 00:16:25) On the chart: two
green candles, then only a wick, then about six candles with no new buyer outside
close — and the market fell fast.

## T103 — HIGHER HIGHS, LOWER LOWS = BREAKING HEADS, COUNTED

His plain reading of the textbook terms: *"you keep hearing higher high, lower low —
simply, it means the pattern's head is broken again and again."* (002 · 00:26:08) Then
a count: in a falling market *"how many L's? One, two, three. Only one high. The high
belongs to the buyer, the low to the seller — three to one, the seller is strong."*
(002 · 00:23:36–00:24:06) The buyer's "boundary" shrinks each swing while the seller's
grows (002 · 00:21:30–00:22:31).

Also from 002: T97's quarters apply to a **single candle** as well as to a pattern —
*"for a candle too you must mark 25, 50, 75, 100; for the pattern too; both"* (002 ·
00:14:54).

| Rule | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|
| **T100** | A move is owned only where the **body** closes; a wick past a level belongs to the **opposite** side. Wick-only probes = the other side holds | **Yes** — body vs wick is exact |
| **T101** | An outside close **above** the prior high is the buyer's, **below** the prior low the seller's; the first opposite-side outside candle is the takeover sign, and one beyond the extreme candle brings an aggressive break | **Yes** |
| **T102** | A side that goes **~30 min (6 × 5m) without a new outside close** in its direction is exhausting; expect the other side | **Yes** — the 30 min is his example, not a stated constant |
| **T103** | Trend = heads broken repeatedly; count highs vs lows over the last swings — the side with more new extremes is in control | **Yes**, and it is the standard HH/HL/LH/LL definition |

## Video 003 — the O pattern, inside and outside PATTERNS, weak and strong, and the clock speeding up

## T104 — O PATTERN: A SHRINKING RANGE IS THE DANGER, NOT THE CALM

*"If the market cannot break the pattern's high or low and keeps a range, that is the O
pattern. It means the market is shrinking badly — and a shrinking market means it will
blast one side."* (003 · 00:04:36–00:05:07) Why: *"the big volume traders are not in
the market at that time. They can come any time."* Small traders settle into the range,
over-trade it, *"and the big man comes with a sack and takes the lot"* (003 ·
00:05:07–00:05:38). And: *"the market is a range 70% of the time"* (003 · 00:11:45).

The instruction: *"in a shrinking market, stay away from the crowd. Only after the big
money enters do we enter."* (003 · 00:12:45) — trade the break out of the O, never
inside it.

## T105 — INSIDE AND OUTSIDE PATTERNS: count only the ones that break

T95 lifted from a candle to a pattern. *"Mark the V pattern's low. Only when a candle
breaks that low and closes outside, take the next pattern into account. The patterns
in between are inside patterns — leave them."* (003 · 00:16:47–00:17:19) Then:
*"consider a pattern only if it is put OUTSIDE. Everything put inside means no big
volume."* (003 · 00:18:50) On a busy chart this turns dozens of wiggles into three
patterns (003 · 00:17:50).

## T106 — WEAK AND STRONG PATTERNS: 50% of the overall range, and the size of the biggest bounce

Two tests. The first: *"if the buyer cannot carry at least 50% of the ultimate high and
low, those are weak patterns… only if it goes at least 50% is it a strong pattern."*
(003 · 00:20:23) The second, inside a group of bounces: *"take the one that carried the
highest; see whether the next ones carry the same. If not, they are all weak."* (003 ·
00:26:35) And when one finally does: *"we need one to one-and-a-half to two times… this
one is about one and a half — the market is ready. Buyer ready, we are ready, meaning
ALERT."* (003 · 00:27:06)

Weak patterns are *"small boys fighting"* — no big volume, not worth a trade (003 ·
00:27:06). He says this video was made for this rule (003 · 00:27:38).

## T107 — THE CLOCK SPEEDS UP: a third of the candles, then half

*"Short term to mid term — however many candles the short term took, the move to mid
term takes one THIRD of them. Mid term to long term takes HALF."* (003 · 00:23:31–
00:24:32) The worked number: *"if it took ten candles to break this low, the next
boundary comes in three candles."* (003 · 00:24:02–00:24:32) His image: leaving Madurai
takes twenty minutes for twenty kilometres; once past the city the next twenty
minutes cover forty (003 · 00:21:55). The reason he gives is fear: *"the fear catches;
when fear catches, even those who would not sell will sell"* (003 · 00:22:28).

## T108 — AFTER THE BREAK, THE NEXT LEG IS 1.5 TO 2 TIMES THE LAST BOUNCE

*"After the market breaks this low and puts a new low, in the next term they will
carry it at least one and a half to two times the distance the buyer carried."* (003 ·
00:23:31–00:24:02) He says he has stated this before; it reads as a projection of the
next swing from the size of the last counter-move. *(heard once in this video — the
"I have told you" refers to a video not yet read)*

| Rule | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|
| **T104** | An **O pattern** (neither the prior pattern's high nor low broken) is a shrinking market that will break hard one way; trade only the break, never the range | **Yes** — the range is exact; "hard" needs a size, e.g. T108 |
| **T105** | Count a **new pattern only when a close breaks the previous pattern's extreme**; inside patterns are ignored as no-volume noise | **Yes, exactly** |
| **T106** | A bounce under **50% of the overall high–low** is a weak pattern; within a group, a bounce **1.5–2× the largest earlier one** is the alert | **Yes** |
| **T107** | Each stage takes fewer candles: short→mid in **⅓** of the candles the short term took, mid→long in **½** | **Yes — a directly measurable claim, and a strong one** |
| **T108** | After a pattern's head breaks, the next leg covers **1.5–2× the last counter-move** | **Yes** — *(heard once here)* |

# THE PLAYLIST, VIDEOS 004–082 — WHAT HE REPEATS, WHERE HE DISAGREES WITH HIMSELF

Videos 004–082 were read end to end by eight readers working in parallel, one block of
videos each, on the same brief as 001–003: every rule quoted with **video · timestamp**,
*(unclear)* where the caption does not settle it, *(heard once)* where it was said once.
Rules already in this playbook were cited, not re-added. Their 484 rules follow as
**T109–T592**, video by video. Quotes were spot-checked against the transcripts
(004, 010, 016, 028, 046, 058, 071, 075 — all matched).

Read this page first: across 79 hours, a handful of ideas carry almost all of the method,
and they recur in video after video.

## The ideas he repeats most

| Idea | Found independently in | Rules |
|---|---|---|
| **The 3-candle test.** A break, a reclaim or a signal is judged over the next **3 candles of its own timeframe** (5m → 15 min, 1H → 3 h, 4H → 12 h, day → 3 days). A body held beyond = real; only wicks = the other side has it | every block | T151, T154, T155, T228, T296, T390, T482, T552, T575 |
| **How deep you buy decides how often it works.** Quarter the prior leg: 0–25 micro, 25–50 short, 50–75 mid, 75–100 long term (071 · 00:01:07). Entries win roughly **3 → 5 → 7 (→ 9) in 10** as the zone deepens | 4 blocks | T114, T188, T231, T533; filter ladder T272 |
| **The failed candle.** A close beyond the prior extreme with **under 50% of the candle beyond it** is a trap, not an outside candle — this tightens T95 | 4 blocks | T144, T174, T266, T540 |
| **The 50% line is the market's main line** — under value below it, over value above it; it holds ~7 in 10; every move returns to its 50% within ⅓× to 1.5× its own duration | 5 blocks | T297, T312, T364, T455, T475, T189 |
| **A read expires.** Candle ≈ 3 bars, pattern ≈ 3 bars of the next timeframe, trend ≈ 3 bars of the one above — T92 with numbers | 4 blocks | T276, T332, T345, T554, T580 |
| **Candle control score.** Points of body vs wick decide who controls the next few bars (body 30 vs wick 70 = buyer +40 for 1–2 h) | 2 blocks | T191, T279 |

## Where he contradicts himself — or this playbook

The first three were settled by Phil on 28-Sep-2026; the rest are recorded, not resolved — each is a question for data, not for argument.

| Topic | One version | The other |
|---|---|---|
| **Capital in trading** | T6: at most 50% | 10–20% — T127, T145, T273, T586; stop at a 10% drawdown, T464. **Phil, 28-Sep-2026: it depends** — not one fixed cap; the size follows the account and the situation, so neither number is a rule on its own |
| **NIFTY violent hours** | T93: 09:15–10:00 and 14:45–15:30 | T260: first hour + 13:30–15:30; T538: 09:15–10:15 and 14:15–15:15. **Phil, 28-Sep-2026: all the same** — one opening window and one closing window; the minutes differ by video, the idea does not |
| **Who owns a wick** | T100 (002): the wick belongs to the side opposite the body's colour | T190 (016): lower wick = buyer, upper wick = seller. **Phil, 28-Sep-2026: both are the same** — a wick is the part the other side pushed and lost, read from where it sits |
| **Next leg size** | T108 / T278: 1.5–2× the last counter-move | T324: 1–1.5× |
| **After an O pattern** | T104: breaks hard one way | T573: buyers retest inside it; the break does not travel far |
| **Coinciding 50% lines** | T553: very hard to break | T591: a squeeze that breaks hard |
| **Return expectations** | T82: a professional makes 4–7% a year | 2–5% a month, 3–5× a bank deposit (block notes below) |
| **Money split by term** | T66: 30/70 replaced 20/30/50 | 20/30/50 by candle/pattern/trend term, restated in 021 |

## Five claims to test first

Each is precise, repeated, and measurable on the NIFTY and BTC history already in the repo.

**Tested 28-Sep-2026 on two years of NIFTY — full numbers in `docs/playlist-claims-results.md`:** (1) 3-candle test ❌ no edge · (2) deeper pullback ❌ not better · (3) back to 50% ⚠️ true of every level, so empty · (4) failed candle ✅ real but small once distance is matched · (5) opening hour ✅ violent, closing window ❌ not.

1. **The 3-candle test** (T154/T552) — after a close beyond a level, does holding 3 bars predict continuation better than chance?
2. **Win rate by pullback depth** (T533) — do buys in the 75–100 zone of the prior leg reach target ~7 in 10, and 0–25 only ~3 in 10?
3. **Return to 50% within ⅓× to 1.5× the leg's duration** (T364).
4. **The failed candle as a trap** (T144) — do closes beyond a level with under half the candle outside reverse more often than full outside closes?
5. **NIFTY's violent hours** (T260/T538) — what share of the day's range falls in 09:15–10:15 and 13:30–15:30?

## Notes each reader left for the merge

**Videos 004-011:**

Read end to end: 004, 005, 006, 007, 008, 009, 010, 011 (8 videos).

Cross-video conflicts found in this batch (the coordinator should resolve these before merging):

- **Zone labels.** In 004 (00:40:23) he calls 0–25 short term, 25–50 mid term and 50–75 long term, but in the same breath he restates the win rate for "0 to 50". In 007 (00:01:32) and 008 (00:38:41) the zones are 0–50 short, 50–75 mid and 75–100 long. The win-rate numbers (T114) follow the 0–50 / 50–75 / 75–100 split.
- **Win-rate numbers.** Short-term entries win 3/10 in 004 (00:40:23) but "5/10 direct profit" in 006 (01:06:50). The type-2 trader wins 7/10 in 005 (00:42:53) but 3/10 in 005 (01:06:49).
- **Second visit to a level.** In 007 (01:26:25) a second touch of the same 50% line bounces less than the first (a dropped ball). In 011 (01:14:22) buyers returning to a level they already defended push "twice as hard".
- **Fib settings.** In 011 (00:14:13–00:14:43) he says to change the 0.236 and 0.786 levels to 25 and 75. It is unclear whether this renames the labels or moves the levels. It matters for T97.

---

**Videos 073-082:**

Batch overview: videos 073–082 are one series. He works top-down through live charts (BTC, Gold and NIFTY). He goes from the monthly chart down to the 1-minute chart, drawing 0/25/50/75/100 boundaries on each timeframe. At each boundary he reads the candles and patterns. Most of the method repeats the vocabulary already in the playbook. The new material is:

- how a break is confirmed: an outside close followed by three candles held beyond the level;
- the wick/body progression rules;
- the multi-timeframe scoring and line-counting schemes;
- time-validity windows for a read on each timeframe;
- a few falsifiable market claims.

Much of each video is sales talk, stories and motivation. I skipped all of it.

## Video 004 — Trend analysis: quartering the V and A legs, the 50% decision line, zone win rates

He quarters both the seller leg and the buyer leg of a V/A structure (25/50/75/100). He treats the 50% lines as the decision boundary and gives win rates for entries in each zone. He also defines "control of fear/greed" by candle count, adds a same-day version of the no-repeat rule, and covers stop slippage.

### T109 — CONTROL OF FEAR / GREED = 3+ SAME-COLOUR CANDLES

*"When three candles come in a row in the same colour, with no opposite candle between them, that is control of fear."* (004 · 00:01:32). *"It forms when the previous A pattern's bottom, the previous low, breaks."* (004 · 00:02:34)

This is the seller-side (fear) or buyer-side (greed) mirror of T96 layers. The trigger is a break of the prior pattern's low, and the retailers' stops 5/10/20 points below that low are what get taken (004 · 00:05:08). It relates to T2 (3+ consecutive reds).

### T110 — QUARTER BOTH LEGS; THE TWO 50% LINES FORM THE BOUNDARY

*"Divide the seller's leg into four, and divide the buyer's leg into four too; the two 50% lines are the boundary. Inside it the market can stay any amount of time; only an outside close beyond it goes to the next boundary, to the low, aggressively."* (004 · 00:24:02–00:24:32, repeated 00:30:11–00:31:11)

This extends T97. T97 quarters only the previous leg; here both the down leg and the up leg are quartered, and the band between their two 50% lines is where price ranges. For a whole V, quarter the whole V, and its 50% is the immediate boundary.

### T111 — 50% IS THE DECISION POINT; ENTER ABOVE 50 NEAR 75, THEN A SECOND ENTRY

*"The 50% is where they take the decision. Take the first entry above 50, near 75, then the second entry."* (004 · 00:26:04–00:26:35). *"Seven times out of ten the stops get hit before the move."* (004 · 00:27:05)

In an up-market, price holds above the 25/0 line with no outside close. Once it breaks 50 it goes to 75, and a pullback that does not go below 25 goes on to 100 (00:27:05–00:27:37). An outside close below the 50 makes the seller confident and breaks the line; a contracting market breaks aggressively (00:28:08). This relates to T104 and T61.

### T112 — WICK BEYOND 50 IS THE SELLER'S; ONLY AN OUTSIDE CLOSE COUNTS

*"Measure from the first green candle's high to the low; the bounce stalls at 25, 50, 75. If only the wick goes past 50, that is the seller."* (004 · 00:19:24–00:23:01). *"A V pullback can come back to the low with a wick; only an outside candle beyond the low makes the seller strong."* (004 · 00:12:15)

This corroborates T99 and T100. There is one refinement: for the buyer to continue, each green candle must make a higher outside close. Otherwise the market contracts and then breaks hard the opposite way (004 · 00:13:16–00:13:46), which corroborates T104.

### T113 — BOUNCE MUST REACH 2x THE PRIOR PUSH, OR THE PATTERN IS INVALID

*"After the short-term correction, the mid-term bounce has to go at least two times the previous push; otherwise it is an invalid pattern."* (004 · 00:06:09). The three checks: 50% of the overall high–low, and 2x the previous push (004 · 00:08:09). With more than 3 green layers, the next push is ≥2x (004 · 01:08:27).

This corroborates T106 and T108, and gives a hard 2x threshold where T106/T108 say 1.5–2x.

### T114 — ZONE WIN RATES: 0–50 → 3/10, 50–75 → 5/10, 75–100 → 7/10

*"If you take trades inside the short-term boundary, out of 10 trades only three go to profit; seven go to loss."* (004 · 00:39:21). *"If you take it inside 0 to 50, only 3 in 10 go directly to profit. The rest, 7 trades, come down. Inside this 50–75 zone, 5 in 10 have the chance to go to profit… Inside this zone, 7 in 10 go to profit; only 3 trades break this and go out of 100."* (004 · 00:40:23–00:40:53)

Here 0/100 are measured on the quartered V (T110), with 100 at the V's low. So "deeper" means further toward the low for a buy. The first buy goes in 50–75 and the second in 75–100 (004 · 00:41:55). Buys are taken below the 50% and sells above the 50% of the A pattern. This extends T38 (under value) with win rates by depth. Zone names conflict with 007/008 (see top).

### T115 — NO REPEAT BUY AT THE SAME BOUNDARY ON THE SAME DAY

*"Once you have bought at that boundary and taken profit, do not buy there again the same day — stay away from the crowd. Next day is fine."* (004 · 00:34:15–00:34:45). *"Once the buyer has booked profit there, the boundary does not react the same way; it goes to the next boundary."* (004 · 00:55:42)

This refines T43 by limiting the scope to the same day. Plain support/resistance "works 3 in 10" (004 · 00:35:15).

### T116 — OUTSIDE CLOSE BEYOND BOTH 25s → PRICE ENTERS THE TWO-50 BAND

*"If it closes outside beyond both immediate 25s, price comes into the boundary of the two 50s."* (004 · 00:38:20)

This is a stepwise corollary of T110.

### T117 — THREE 50% LINES MUST ALL BREAK TO GO UP

*"The previous 50, the current 50 and the high's 50 — all three have to be broken for it to go up; otherwise the low breaks."* (004 · 00:51:39–00:52:09)

The first sign of buyer involvement is an enlarging lower wick in the zone (004 · 00:47:04–00:47:34), which corroborates T68 and T91. Seven red candles means 35 minutes of seller control on 5m (004 · 00:54:10), which corroborates T102.

### T118 — TIMEFRAME ESCALATION ON A BREAK

*"If the 5-minute breaks, the 15-minute reacts; three 15-minute candles is aggressive."* (004 · 00:42:27). *(heard once)*: in a 5m sell trend the move lasts up to three 15m candles (004 · 00:29:08).

This corroborates T23 and T92.

### T119 — STOP ORDERS SLIP

*"You buy at ₹100 with a stop at ₹90; it fills at ₹80, or ₹70."* (004 · 00:43:59–00:45:01)

This is an example that supports T70 (limit orders, never stop orders). He trades once a day in gold or bitcoin (004 · 00:56:42), which loosely corroborates T50.

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T109** | ≥3 consecutive same-colour candles with no opposite candle, after the prior pattern's low breaks = control of fear/greed | **Yes** |
| **T110** | Quarter both the seller leg and the buyer leg; the band between their two 50% lines is the range; only an outside close beyond it goes to the next boundary | **Yes** |
| **T111** | Above 50 → 75; a pullback that holds 25 → 100; an outside close below 50 → break | **Yes** |
| **T112** | A wick past 50 belongs to the other side; buyer continuation needs successive higher outside green closes, else contraction then an opposite break | **Yes** |
| **T113** | Mid-term bounce < 2x the prior push = invalid pattern | **Yes** |
| **T114** | Buy entries at 0–50 / 50–75 / 75–100 of the quartered V win 3 / 5 / 7 out of 10 | **Yes** — directly measurable. Zone naming conflicts with 007/008 |
| **T115** | No second buy at the same boundary on the same day after a profitable one | **Yes** |
| **T116** | Outside close beyond both immediate 25s → price enters the two-50 band | **Yes** |
| **T117** | Previous, current and high-leg 50% lines must all break for an up move; else the low breaks | **Partly** — which three swings supply the lines is shown on the chart only |
| **T118** | A 5m break carries into the 15m; the sell move lasts up to three 15m candles | **Partly** — heard once |
| **T119** | Stop orders fill 10–20 points worse than the stop | **Yes** (slippage study) |
