## Video 005 — Three kinds of trader (angler / boat / ship = retail / boundary trader / big player)

He splits market participants into three types and describes each one's entry: retail buys the first green candle, the boundary trader buys at the stop-hunt, and the big player buys only after accumulation finishes. He also covers how the big player moves price and how much he risks.

### T120 — TYPE 1 (RETAIL): FIRST GREEN 5m CLOSE, STOP BELOW LOW → 7/10 LOSSES

*"The one who buys as soon as he sees a green colour and keeps his stop at the low — he loses."* (005 · 00:42:22). Seven out of ten of these trades lose (005 · 00:30:41, 00:32:42, 00:39:49, 01:06:19).

This is a falsifiable baseline: a first-green-close entry with a stop at the swing low. It corroborates T22 from the losing side.

### T121 — TYPE 2 (BOUNDARY TRADER): BUY AT THE BOUNDARY AFTER THE STOP HUNT, EXIT IF AN OUTSIDE CANDLE BREAKS THE LOW

He buys at the boundary right after the stop hunt, with a tight entry and exit, and gets out if an outside candle breaks the low (005 · 00:39:18, 00:40:51). He wins 7/10 (005 · 00:42:53). *(unclear)* Later he says type 2 in the 25 zone gets only 3/10 (005 · 01:06:49).

### T122 — TYPE 3 (BIG PLAYER): BUY ONLY AFTER THE BUYER CARRIES BACK ABOVE THE LINE WITH AN AGGRESSIVE CLOSE

*"They stop-hunt, and only if the buyer carries it back above this line do they call it an aggressive close — that is 'accumulation finished'. The buyer has filled his sacks… They will not buy this red outside candle."* (005 · 00:41:51). *"Within the next three candles he finishes in overall profit."* (005 · 00:43:53)

The entry is the reclaim: a close back above the line that was stop-hunted. The next 3 candles should deliver profit. He carries price past 50% of the overall distance easily (005 · 00:44:23) and refills without breaking the old low. The big player breaks 50% of the overall, makes a new low, then brings price back (005 · 01:06:49). This relates to T68, T72 and T94.

### T123 — IF THE BOUNCE DID NOT MAKE ≥1x THE PRIOR LEG, THE BIG PLAYER IS ABSENT

If the bounce did not travel at least 1x the prior leg, big investors are not present, so do not focus entries on the return (005 · 00:50:01). The market does not come back to the same place. After the short term finishes it goes to the mid term. If 25 was not reached and the low is broken by an outside candle, the move is aggressive. Plan the next trade only after the mid term is reached (005 · 00:49:01, 00:53:06–00:53:36).

This is a weaker form of T113/T106 (1x rather than 1.5–2x).

### T124 — ORDER SIZE ≈ 20% OF ACCOUNT

With a $100 account he places only $20 orders (005 · 00:20:00, 00:45:57). *(heard once)* Risk 10% for 100% reward, or 10% reward for 0.1–0.2% risk (005 · 00:48:30). He prefers BTC and gold because money flows 24h (005 · 00:28:07), which corroborates T40.

See T127/T145 for the same 20% cap stated in rupees.

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T120** | Buying the first green 5m close after a fall with a stop at the low loses 7/10 | **Yes** |
| **T121** | Boundary buy after the stop hunt, exit on an outside candle below the low, wins 7/10 | **Partly** — the win rate conflicts within the video (7/10 vs 3/10) |
| **T122** | Buy only when price closes back above the stop-hunted line (aggressive close); profit expected within 3 candles | **Yes** |
| **T123** | Bounce < 1x the prior leg → no big player → no entry on the return | **Yes** |
| **T124** | Order size ≈ 20% of the account | **Yes** (sizing rule) |

---

## Video 006 — Course overview: the 20% risk cap, trading hours, runway channel

This is mostly a course and demo video. It restates his risk cap, gives his trading hours, and introduces the "runway" (a trendline or channel through A-pattern heads) as the place big players come, not a breakout signal.

### T125 — TRADING HOURS: 7–10 PM IST; ASIAN SESSION IS QUIET

*"My maximum trading hours are evening, night, 7 to 10, because we do bitcoin, the US market, then gold."* (006 · 00:23:23). The Asian session has no big movement.

This is a time-of-day claim for BTC, US and gold, and it parallels T93 for NIFTY. Also: 99% of intraday traders are finished in the first 15 minutes (006 · 00:31:35) *(heard once)*.

### T126 — NO TRENDLINE-BREAKOUT TRADES; THE RUNWAY IS WHERE BIG PLAYERS ARRIVE

The runway joins the A-pattern highs, which makes it a trendline. Do not trade trendline breakouts (006 · 00:54:02–00:54:33, 00:59:39–01:00:41). The runway channel joins the lows, with a parallel line through the highs. The *extension* of the runway is where big players come. Three touches mark the short, mid and long boundaries (006 · 01:04:18–01:05:50, 01:10:22–01:11:54). *(unclear)* Short-term zone: 5/10 direct profit (006 · 01:06:50), which conflicts with T114.

This refines T14 and T15: the trendline is a location, not a trigger. Swing high/low trading works 3/10 (006 · 00:53:32).

### T127 — NEVER RISK MORE THAN 20% OF CAPITAL; WITH 10x MARGIN NEVER MORE THAN 2x CAPITAL

*"None of our trades go beyond ₹20,000"* on a ₹1 lakh account (006 · 00:19:47). With 10x margin, notional is never above ₹2 lakh and ₹80k stays idle (006 · 00:19:47–00:20:18). On a $100 account, $20–30 (006 · 00:22:21). *(unclear)* Add about 30% as the position grows (006 · 00:25:26).

This is the same rule as T145. It conflicts with T6 (50% cap): here trading capital is 20%.

Other points: inside-pattern prices are retail, and only outside candle/pattern prices are the big player's (006 · 00:51:28–00:52:30), which corroborates T105. ATM options move at least 1/3 of the index and OTM is avoided (006 · 00:34:11–00:35:14, 00:38:16). No stop loss and no indicators (006 · 00:14:40, 00:49:27), which corroborates T11.

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T125** | BTC/US/gold move mainly 19:00–22:00 IST; the Asian session is quiet | **Yes** (volatility by hour) |
| **T126** | Do not trade trendline breakouts; the runway extension is the big-player zone | **Partly** — the "three dots" placement is shown on the chart only |
| **T127** | Trading exposure ≤ 20% of capital; with 10x margin, notional ≤ 2x capital | **Yes** (sizing constraint) |

## Video 007 — Multi-timeframe boundaries, candle counts, over/under value

He sets timeframes (5m/15m/1H for trading; day/week/month for investing), zones (0–50 / 50–75 / 75–100 / >100), how to step up a timeframe when no boundary is visible, confluence, candle-count timing and value-based win rates.

### T128 — WHEN THE 1H HAS NO VISIBLE LOWER LOW, TAKE THE BOUNDARY FROM 4H, THEN DAY

The trading frames are 5m short / 15m mid / 1H long, and the investment frames are day/week/month (007 · 00:00:32), which corroborates T23. The zones are 0–50 short, 50–75 mid, 75–100 long, and beyond 100 is the extension (007 · 00:01:32, 00:02:02). A pullback may wick to 50, but an outside close beyond 50 returns to the base (007 · 00:03:02–00:03:32). If 1H shows no lower low, go to 4H and then Day for the boundary (007 · 00:04:34–00:05:05). Once the 1H boundary is reached, the frames shift to 1H/4H/Day (007 · 00:10:13).

### T129 — CONFLUENCE: 1H BASE + 4H BASE + DAY 50% → HOLDS 50–60%

When the 1H base, 4H base and Day 50% coincide, the level holds 50–60% of the time and breaks 40% (007 · 00:05:35–00:06:09). He also mentions "3 resistances / 1 support" (007 · 00:08:13) and "nine zones" (007 · 00:13:15) *(unclear)*. His three steps: identify the zone, confirm the big player arrived, size to the zone (007 · 00:13:45). Trade ₹10–20k of ₹1L (007 · 00:15:17), which corroborates T127.

### T130 — THE NEXT BOUNDARY IS REACHED IN HALF THE CANDLES

*"If it took this many candles to come to this short term, it takes half the candles to go to the next boundary."* (007 · 00:24:54). *"However many candles there are until the breakout, until the outside candle — within half of those candles they bring the market from this boundary to this boundary."* (007 · 01:18:46). About 4 candles into the safe zone after accumulation (007 · 01:19:17).

This corroborates T107 (the ½ ratio). Repeated in 009 (00:34:39) and 011 (00:33:07). T107's ⅓ ratio for short→mid is also mentioned at 00:24:54. On weekly frames the boundary can come in one candle (011 · 00:33:38).

### T131 — BEYOND THE 1H BOUNDARY, 99% REVERSE

*(heard once)* The 1H boundary is reached only on aggressive days, 1–2 days a week (007 · 00:43:19). Beyond the 1H boundary, 99% reverse (007 · 00:44:50).

### T132 — VALUE LADDER WIN RATES: CHASING 3/10, ZONE 5/10, UNDER VALUE 7/10, + BIG PLAYER 8–9/10

Over value is chasing a running bus, the zone is where accumulation starts, and under value is below that, at the place everyone's stop sits (007 · 01:21:19–01:22:50). *"Seven trades out of 10 go… and if you do it after the big player has come, almost eight to nine trades go."* (007 · 01:23:52). Profit comes within 4–5 candles (007 · 01:22:19). Buy below where you would put your stop → 5/10 natural profit (007 · 01:20:18). Support breaks 7/10 (007 · 01:17:45).

This extends T38 and T53 with win rates. It is consistent in direction with T114.

### T133 — SECOND VISIT TO THE SAME 50% LINE BOUNCES LESS

A second visit to the same 50% line bounces less than the first, like a dropped ball (007 · 01:26:25–01:26:55). This conflicts with 011 (01:14:22); see the top note.

Other points: one trade a day for ₹2,000 beats three trades for ₹500 (007 · 01:24:23), which corroborates T50. A new V after a broken V goes 2x (007 · 01:35:34), which corroborates T108. A flat area takes about 3x the candles (007 · 01:32:31) *(unclear)*. Don't shave ₹1 off an exit — it is the difference between capital doubling in 2 years and in 7 (007 · 00:55:26) *(heard once)*.

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T128** | No visible lower low on 1H → take the boundary from 4H, then Day | **Yes** |
| **T129** | 1H base + 4H base + Day 50% confluence holds 50–60% | **Yes** |
| **T130** | After the breakout, the next boundary is reached in ≤ ½ the candles the prior area took | **Yes** |
| **T131** | Beyond the 1H boundary, price reverses 99% of the time; reached 1–2 days a week | **Partly** — heard once |
| **T132** | Win rates: over value 3/10, zone 5/10, under value 7/10, with big-player confirmation 8–9/10; profit within 4–5 candles | **Partly** — "big player arrived" needs T138/T122 to be defined |
| **T133** | A second test of the same 50% line bounces less than the first | **Yes** — conflicts with 011 |

## Video 008 — Candle analysis: the open-position test, inside/outside, individual vs overall, horizon ×3

He shows how to read a single closed candle: where the next candle opens inside its quarters, what makes an outside candle, individual vs overall references, and how far ahead each timeframe's candle analysis reaches.

### T134 — MINIMUM STRUCTURE SIZES AND HORIZONS

At least 2 candles make a pattern. Three patterns, about 30 candles, make a trend (008 · 00:13:15–00:13:45). A candle forecasts the next 5–10 minutes, a pattern 1–2 hours, and a trend 1–2 days (008 · 00:19:22). On a 5m chart: candle → 15 min, pattern → 1–2 h, trend → 4–5 h (008 · 01:36:57–01:37:58).

This corroborates T27 and T92.

### T135 — OPEN-POSITION TEST: QUARTER THE CLOSED CANDLE, READ WHERE THE NEXT ONE OPENS

*"Take this candle and divide it into four… 0 to 50 is short term, 50 to 75 mid term, 75 to 100 long term… let the candle finish, then see in which boundary the next candle's open is."* (008 · 00:38:41). If the next candle opens in 0–25, buyers are not involved; an open at 5% means 95% seller (008 · 00:38:41–00:43:22). Against the overall range, an open at 20% means 80% seller control (008 · 01:05:41).

This is a new measurable. It relates to T97 but applies to a single candle.

### T136 — BUYER "PASS MARK": 25, THEN 50, THEN NEVER BACK BELOW 25

Price must pass 25 (the "pass mark") and then 50. After passing 50 the body must not return below 25 (a wick is fine), else it goes back to the low (008 · 00:43:22–00:47:57). The heaviest fight, 2–3 candles, and most contraction happen at the 50% line (008 · 00:44:53).

This corroborates T97 and T100.

### T137 — INDIVIDUAL = PREVIOUS CANDLE; OVERALL = ALL PRIOR CANDLES; LINE COUNT = STRENGTH

"Individual" means only the previous candle, and "overall" means all candles before the current one (008 · 01:03:40–01:05:11). One line is a price line. Two lines that coincide make a boundary, and strength is the number of coinciding lines (008 · 01:13:24). To go up, price must close above the overall's 25/75 and the individual's 25 and 50 (008 · 01:13:54).

This defines the "individual vs overall" vocabulary.

### T138 — CLOSED-MARKET ANALYSIS, THEN VOLUME; NO BIG PLAYER, NO TRADE

First analyse the closed market, then use volume to confirm the big player. If he has not arrived, do not trade even at the boundary (008 · 00:55:05–00:55:36). With analysis, losses drop from 5/10 to 1–2/10, and a precise entry is in profit by the 3rd candle (008 · 01:00:08).

This corroborates T68 and T94.

### T139 — CANDLE-ANALYSIS HORIZON ≈ NEXT TIMEFRAME UP

*"If you take a five-minute candle and do candle analysis, you can find how the market will be for the next 15 minutes."* (008 · 01:14:54). *"…one hour's closed candle and current candle → the next four hours… a week → the next three months… a month → one to three years."* (008 · 01:15:24–01:15:54)

The mapping is 5m→15m, 15m→1H, 1H→4H, 4H→day, day→week (later "one week to a month, max 3 weeks"), week→3 months, month→1–3 years (008 · 01:14:54–01:16:56, 01:34:22–01:35:23). This refines T92 into a concrete lookup table.

Inside and outside are defined as in T95; inside = contraction and outside = expansion (008 · 00:49:57–00:54:35, 01:09:48–01:10:50). The market moves only on outside candle/pattern/trend (008 · 01:23:04–01:24:05), which corroborates T105.

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T134** | Pattern ≥ 2 candles; trend ≈ 3 patterns / 30 candles; on 5m, candle → 15 min, pattern → 1–2 h, trend → 4–5 h | **Yes** |
| **T135** | The next candle's open position within the prior candle's quarters = buyer share (open in 0–25 = no buyers) | **Yes** |
| **T136** | Buyer needs a body above 25, then 50, and no body back below 25 after 50; else return to the low | **Yes** |
| **T137** | Up move needs closes above overall 25/75 and individual 25/50; coinciding lines = stronger boundary | **Yes** |
| **T138** | No trade at a boundary without volume confirming the big player | **Partly** — the volume threshold is not given |
| **T139** | Candle analysis horizon: 5m→15m, 1H→4H, day→week, week→3 months, month→1–3 years | **Yes** |

## Video 009 — Candle, pattern and trend by trader type; the percentage-doubling structure

He matches the analysis method to the instrument and holding time (options → pattern; futures → trend; investor → day/week/month). He then describes the market's fall structure as a sequence of doubling percentages.

### T140 — METHOD BY INSTRUMENT, WITH TARGETS

Option buyers use pattern analysis and need profit within 1–2 hours because of time decay (the example is ₹50/hour) (009 · 00:05:39–00:06:40). Futures and margin traders use trend analysis, hold 1–2 days, and aim for 6–7% per trade. Investors use day/week/month and aim for 26–40%, never less than 7% (009 · 00:06:40–00:07:40). In points: scalper candle 5–10, intraday pattern 25–50, trend 100–250 (009 · 00:16:19–00:16:49).

This relates to T40 and T92.

### T141 — A BODY BEYOND 25 MUST HOLD ~10 MIN; A 50% BODY CLOSE WITHIN 15 MIN CONFIRMS

A body beyond 25 must be held for about 10 minutes, or it falls back (009 · 00:12:44). A confident breakout needs fresh buying volume (009 · 00:13:14). A body close beyond 50% within 15 minutes is needed to confirm (009 · 00:30:32). On a ₹100 range, 50 is "satisfaction"; to reach 75 it needs a 25 pullback to reload (009 · 00:17:19–00:17:50).

### T142 — PERCENTAGE-DOUBLING STRUCTURE: 1% FALL + ½% BOUNCE → NEXT FALL 2%, THEN 4, 8, 16

*"If the market came down 1% and then went up only ½%… it goes back down 2 to 3%… if it fell 1% and the buyer could carry it up only half a percent, then next they take the market 2%."* (009 · 00:38:42–00:39:13). The ladder continues 2 → 4 → 8 → 16% (009 · 00:43:16–00:44:50). *"Don't take the 2 literally; it is the concept."* (009 · 00:46:21). He also gives a smaller version, ¼% → ½% → 1% (009 · 01:21:40). Repeated in 011: *"If it can't break its high, 2 goes… 4% control of fear… 8% control of fear."* (011 · 00:34:08)

Rule: if a bounce retraces only half of a fall, the next fall is about 2x the previous one. This corroborates T108 on the downside and T46's 2-4-8-16 sequence.

### T143 — ESCALATOR DESCENT, THEN A VERTICAL FALL OF 3–5 REDS = ACCUMULATION ENDING

A slow "escalator" descent is followed by a vertical fall of 3–5 consecutive red candles. That is control of fear, and accumulation is ending. Do not approach during the escalator phase (009 · 00:39:43–00:41:45, 01:25:17). After a double flush comes accumulation; if the low is not broken, the next move is ≥2x the prior maximum (009 · 00:27:28–00:27:58). Declining buyer involvement plus a failed 25 in an inside pattern leads to an aggressive break (009 · 00:26:28–00:26:58).

This corroborates T2, T8, T72 and T108.

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T140** | Options: pattern, 1–2 h; futures: trend, 1–2 days, 6–7%; investor: 26–40%, ≥7% | **Partly** — targets, not signals |
| **T141** | A body beyond 25 must hold ~10 min; a body close beyond 50% within 15 min confirms | **Yes** |
| **T142** | Bounce ≤ ½ of the fall → next fall ≈ 2x the prior fall (1→2→4→8%) | **Yes** — he says the 2x is approximate |
| **T143** | 3–5 consecutive reds after a slow descent mark the end of accumulation; don't buy the slow descent | **Partly** — "escalator" slope is not quantified |

## Video 010 — Wick leads body; the failed candle; the 20% capital rule

He shows that the wick probes first and the body follows, and defines a "failed candle" as a close beyond the level with less than half the candle outside. He gives his capital rule (≤20% in trading), the marks for each skill, and how to recover a losing position.

### T144 — FAILED CANDLE: CLOSE BEYOND THE LINE BUT < 50% OF THE CANDLE OUTSIDE = TRAP

*"It goes outside, but only puts price outside — we call this a failed candle."* (010 · 00:10:37). *"A candle has a boundary, high and low. If it closes outside that boundary we call it outside. But only if 50% of this second candle's size is outside is it originally an outside candle."* (010 · 00:11:07). *"Only if at least 50% is outside the price line and the closing price is outside too."* (010 · 00:11:38). Repeated at 011 (01:00:42): *"the closing price is beyond, but if 50% of the body is not beyond this price line, I've called that a failed candle — it means trap."*

There are three candle types: inside, outside and failed (010 · 00:25:22). This corroborates and names T20. It also refines T95: a close beyond the prior high/low is "outside" only if ≥50% of the candle is beyond it.

### T145 — NEVER MORE THAN 20% OF CAPITAL IN TRADING; INVESTMENT FLOATING LOSS ≤ 30%

*"If your capital is 1 lakh, whatever the reason, do not go beyond 20,000… he gives 10% margin, fine… you will take risk only up to about ₹2 lakh — take risk of one more multiple of your capital in trading, not beyond."* (010 · 01:00:38). Repeated at 01:09:18 and 01:23:37. The floating loss on investments must not exceed 30% of assets (010 · 01:01:39–01:02:40). Investment trades make 20–30% in 3–4 days after about 2 months of waiting (010 · 01:02:40).

This is the same as T127, and it conflicts with T6 (50%).

### T146 — WICK LEADS, BODY FOLLOWS; EACH BODY ≥50% OUTSIDE OR PRICE RETURNS TO 50%

The wick comes first and the body follows; the wick is the advance signal (010 · 00:05:35–00:06:35, 00:13:08; repeated 011 · 00:21:18–00:21:49). Each successive body must be higher and ≥50% outside. A wick-only probe followed by an inside close returns to the swing's 50% (010 · 00:17:10–00:18:42). Reset the chart for each timeframe: the visible ultimate high/low is that timeframe's boundary, and three timeframes are analysed (010 · 00:16:10). A closed candle plus the current one forecasts the next 3 candles (010 · 00:22:49).

### T147 — RECOVERING A LOSING POSITION: ≥ ½ THE POSITION IN RESERVE, RECOVERY 1.5–3x THE FALL'S DURATION

To recover a losing position you need at least half the position size in reserve, and 2x is smarter (010 · 01:19:01–01:20:33). Recovery takes 1.5–3x as long as the fall did, cut to 6–8 months by adding at the confirmed best price (010 · 01:21:04–01:21:36). In control of fear, bounces reach 50, then 25, then not even 25. The "emergency exit" is at 25, and it needs ≥3x backup (010 · 01:21:36–01:22:37). *(unclear)* "1-3-3 construction" (010 · 01:25:42).

This relates to T37 and T54.

Other points: read falls by the red candles only — is each red outside? The first sizeable lower wick is the start of accumulation, and the next wick is about 2x (010 · 00:38:42–00:41:47) *(heard once)*. Allocation is 20% short / 30% mid / 50% long, nested (010 · 00:41:47), which corroborates T3. Marks: technical 10, fundamental 10, fund management 30, psychology 30, trading system 20 (010 · 00:26:54–00:29:30). Scale up as the market reaches stronger levels; the rich invest ₹10/15/25/50 increasing (010 · 01:03:11, 01:30:21), which corroborates T37. Investing by distance from the lifetime high: gold 21% below its lifetime high → ₹20k of ₹1L (010 · 01:29:18–01:29:49), which corroborates T63.

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T144** | A close beyond the prior high/low with < 50% of the candle beyond = failed candle (trap); expect the opposite move | **Yes** |
| **T145** | Trading capital ≤ 20% of account (notional ≤ 2x capital with margin); investment drawdown ≤ 30% | **Yes** (sizing constraint) |
| **T146** | Wick-only probe beyond a level followed by an inside close → return to the swing's 50% | **Yes** |
| **T147** | Recovery needs ≥ ½ position in reserve; recovery takes 1.5–3x the fall's duration | **Partly** — reserve rule is testable; the duration claim is loose |

## Video 011 — Fib setup, multi-swing 50% clusters, candle-size escalation, 3-candle confirmation

He gives the Fibonacci tool settings and draws 50% lines on several swings to find "tightening points". He describes how fall candles escalate in size, and teaches the 3-candle test on the previous candle's body and wick, which he uses to avoid traps.

### T148 — FIB SETTINGS: 0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1; NO FILL

Keep 0 and 1, change 0.236 to 25, keep 0.5, and *"change the 0.786 to 75"* (011 · 00:14:13–00:14:43). Remove the background fill. *(unclear)* It is not clear whether this renames the labels or moves the levels to 0.25/0.75. T97's quarters imply moving the levels.

### T149 — 50% LINES OF THE LAST 3–4 SWINGS CLUSTER INTO A "TIGHTENING POINT"

*"The 50% line is the most important line; the market takes its decision at 50%… I keep only the 50%, I cut 0, 1, 25, 75."* (011 · 00:26:24). Draw the fib on each of the last 3–4 swings and keep only the 50% lines. Where they cluster, *"breaking beyond it is very, very hard"* (011 · 00:28:57). If it is not broken, the market contracts and then moves in one candle (011 · 00:26:24–00:30:31). At least 3 swings are needed (011 · 00:31:33, 00:43:49). A wick-only probe past the 50% with no body means reversal (011 · 00:28:25).

This extends T117 and T137 (line count = strength).

### T150 — FALL CANDLES ESCALATE IN SIZE: 5m-SIZED, THEN 1H-SIZED, THEN DAY-SIZED

A fall repeats as a single, then double, then multiple candle structure, and the candle size escalates: 5m-sized, then 1H-sized, then day-sized. Control of fear is ≥3 consecutive reds: three 5m-sized, then three 1H-sized, then three day-sized (011 · 00:35:10–00:37:43). The % doubling (T142) is repeated (011 · 00:34:08). Rebuild capital stepwise 1→2→5→10→20→40→80→100 (011 · 00:08:05).

### T151 — 3-CANDLE CONFIRMATION ON THE PREVIOUS CANDLE'S BODY/WICK

*"Mark the previous candle's wick, mark its body — whichever is lower."* (011 · 00:59:40). *"Give the next three days."* (011 · 01:00:12). *"Beyond this candle, within the next three days, it must put a body below — a wick doesn't matter, only if the body goes below."* (011 · 01:00:42). *"If the candle closes beyond the existing wick and body, the market will struggle to break this low… it goes to target before coming back to this boundary."* (011 · 01:01:44). *"Give three days… on the second day it put a body… so, confirmed, the chance of breaking this is low. Low, not zero."* (011 · 01:12:21). If a new low forms beyond it within the 3 candles, roll the reference forward to the next closed candle (011 · 01:11:51).

Procedure: after each closed candle, mark its body and wick levels and allow 3 candles (15 min on 5m, 3 days on daily). If price closes a body back beyond them within those 3 candles, the level is unlikely to be broken back and price reaches its target first. If a body closes through them instead, the lows break. A bigger buyer wick than the previous one means buyers are increasing (011 · 01:01:14). This does not trigger an entry by itself: *"even so, you must not take a trade here"* (011 · 01:08:48). It relates to T144 (failed candle = the fail case) and T21.

### T152 — BUYERS RETURNING TO A LEVEL THEY ALREADY DEFENDED PUSH HARDER

*"When they come to the same place again, they push with twice the force… psychologically, when the buyer comes back to that place he gets stronger."* (011 · 01:14:22)

This conflicts with T133 (007: the second visit bounces less).

### T153 — TIGHTENING THE SETUP LOWERS THE SHORT-TERM LOSS RATE 7 → 5 → 3 → 2 → 1 OF 10

*"Taken at a short-term mount, seven trades lose. Tighten more and you can make it five, more — three, two, one… but it will never go beyond seven."* (011 · 01:14:54). He expects the balance to rise at the end of every third trade (011 · 01:15:24).

This corroborates T114: the baseline short-term loss rate is 7/10, and 7 losses per 10 is the ceiling.

Other points: trader timeframes are 1m/5m/15m (and 1H), investor timeframes day/week/month (011 · 00:44:19). Look for reasons *not* to take a trade (011 · 00:45:49). A pullback beyond 25 goes to 50 (011 · 00:22:20), which corroborates T136.

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T148** | Fib levels 0 / 0.25 / 0.5 / 0.75 / 1 with no fill | **Partly** — rename vs move is unclear |
| **T149** | Coinciding 50% lines of the last ≥3 swings form a level that rarely breaks; if held, contraction then a one-candle move | **Yes** |
| **T150** | Fall candles escalate 5m-sized → 1H-sized → day-sized, three each | **Partly** — "sized like" is not quantified |
| **T151** | Mark the prior candle's body/wick; if a body closes back beyond it within 3 candles, the level holds and the target is hit first; a body through it → lows break | **Yes** |
| **T152** | A second test of a buyer-defended level gets a stronger push | **Yes** — conflicts with T133 |
| **T153** | Baseline short-term loss rate 7/10, never worse; tighter filters lower it | **Partly** — "tightening" is not defined |

## Video 012 — Three-candle confirmation window at the 50% line; timeframe stack for traders

He re-draws gold (daily, then 1H, 15m, 1m) and reads each boundary using only the current candle and the one just closed. The core procedure: at a boundary (especially the 50%), give the market three candles; the side that closes a body outside the other side's last push takes control, while wick-only probes hand control to the opposite side. He also sets the trader's timeframe stack (5m/15m/1H, adding 1m for the 7-hour Indian session) and ranks the 50% line above the 25/75 lines.

### T154 — THREE-CANDLE CONFIRMATION WINDOW, SCALED TO THE TIMEFRAME

*"This current candle, within the next three days, within three candles — if 1 minute, within the next three minutes; if 5 minute, within the next 15 minutes; if one hour, within the next three hours; if 4 hour, within the next 12 hours; if weekly, within the next three weeks; if monthly, within the next three months — for each you must give that time duration; only then does volume accumulate inside it and then there is buyer involvement."* (012 · 00:04:09–00:04:40)
*"Whatever candle you take, if you do current-candle / previous-candle analysis, it holds for about the next three candles — 5 minutes, next three candles, 15 minutes … 1 hour, the next three hours … 4 hours, the next 12 hours … day, the next three days."* (012 · 00:13:22)

A candle's signal is judged over exactly the next 3 candles of the same timeframe. This sharpens T92 ("a few bars") to a fixed count of 3. Note at 00:12:21–00:12:52 he also gives a looser, inconsistent mapping (15m → "next one or two hours", 4H → "8 hours") before settling on the ×3 rule; the ×3 version is the one he repeats.

### T155 — BODY OUTSIDE WITHIN 3 CANDLES = CONTINUATION; WICK ONLY = CONTROL SHIFTS

*"When the three candles give time, what should happen in the market? It must put an outside candle beyond the body and wick of the candle before. … What must not happen? If the current candle's body, within the next three days, again puts a selling outside candle beyond this candle's low, then there is a chance the market follows further down — the same trend continues."* (012 · 00:01:34–00:02:06)
*"If for three days the buyer only put a wick beyond this and maintained it without being able to put a body, what happens to the market — it changes to the buyer's control."* (012 · 00:02:36)
*"Only if within the next three days they put an outside close beyond the existing body-wick here is there a chance the seller takes control again."* (012 · 00:16:25)

The test at a boundary: over the next 3 candles, does the in-control side close a **body** beyond the previous candle's full range (body+wick)? Yes → continuation. Only wicks → the opposite side takes control. This combines T95/T100 with a fixed 3-candle deadline (T154).

### T156 — AN OUTSIDE CLOSE BEYOND THE OTHER SIDE'S LAST PUSH MAKES THE ORIGIN HOLD

*"If within the next three days the buyer put an outside candle beyond this, the buyer's confidence increases … because they closed beyond where they had already carried it, the chance of coming back below where they currently carried it from is very, very low. Even if they come, they will put a wick; the chance of putting a body is very, very low."* (012 · 00:05:11–00:05:42)

After a qualifying outside close (T155), the origin of that push is expected to hold on a closing basis (wicks may probe). Falsifiable: after the outside close, count how often a later body closes below the push origin.

### T157 — A CONFIDENT SIDE CARRIES PRICE TO THE 50% OF THE HIGH–LOW, THEN PAUSES

*"If the buyer's confidence is high, what will happen? There is a chance the buyer carries it to 50% of this high and low. After going 50%, won't it go 75? It will … there you must do candle analysis again. At 50% they will take a break — fuel."* (012 · 00:11:21–00:11:51)
*"The fall will come down at least 50% of however far it went from here — in one stretch."* (012 · 00:36:14)

The minimum expectation after a control change is the 50% of the leg; beyond that is re-read, not assumed. Consistent with T26/T97.

### T158 — FAILING TO PASS THE 50% MEANS A RETURN TO THE LEG'S 50% / SOURCE

*"If it cannot go beyond this trend's 50%, it means the market will come to this trend's 50%. … Even after giving this candle three hours' time, three candles' time, if its involvement cannot continue the trend, the market changes to the opposite trend."* (012 · 00:33:10)
*"50% — only a wick beyond, it could not put a body. Then the market has a chance to come to its source."* (012 · 00:34:41–00:35:11)

A leg that reaches 50% and, within 3 candles, only wicks beyond it (no body) is expected to reverse to its origin/50%. Refines T102 (exhaustion) with a 3-candle deadline instead of 6.

### T159 — A CANDLE'S OWN 50% MUST NOT BE CLOSED THROUGH IN THE NEXT 3 CANDLES

*"Divide this boundary in four, 25 50 75 100; take 50 generally. What must not happen: it must not close below this candle's 50%. That is the immediate."* (012 · 00:21:30)
*"This candle's 50% comes here. Then within the next three candles what must not happen — within one, two, three days, a close below this place."* (012 · 00:23:00–00:23:30)

Applies the quarters (T97) to a single candle: for a move to continue, none of the next 3 closes may be beyond the 50% of the latest candle.

### T160 — THE 50% LINE IS THE STRONG BOUNDARY; 25/75 ARE "SUPPORTING"

*"In one minute don't consider 0.25 at all. … Always, everywhere, look at 50% … out of 10 times, seven times the market is likely to take a decision at that place. … If you look at the 25 boundary, only 3 to 5 times out of 10 is there a chance of a decision there. Those are supporting boundaries — 25, 75 — 50% is strong."* (012 · 00:39:46–00:40:17)
*"The results there will be about 25 to 50%; at 50 there is a chance of 50–70%. So follow only the major boundaries."* (012 · 00:40:47)

Falsifiable claim: price reacts at the 50% about 50–70% of the time, at 25/75 only 25–50%. On the 1-minute chart, ignore the 25/75 lines.

### T161 — TRADER'S TIMEFRAME STACK: 5m SHORT, 15m MID, 1H LONG; ADD 1m FOR INDIAN MARKETS

*"For a trader, short term is 5 minutes, mid term is 15 minutes, long term is 1 hour. Analyse these three, which zone the market is in … then candle analysis."* (012 · 00:29:36)
*"An intraday trader: first analyse 5 min, 15 min, 1 hour, then analyse 15 minutes strongly, and only then come to 5 minutes, analyse the 5-minute candles and take the trade. Your base is 15 minutes."* (012 · 00:30:07)
*"The Indian market has only 7 hours … Bitcoin, gold are 24-hour markets, so take 5 min, 15 min, 1 hour. Those in the Indian market should analyse 1 minute and then 5, 15, 1 hour — all four."* (012 · 00:30:38)

A scalper (trade done within 15 minutes) enters off 5m after reading 5/15/1H. Consistent with T38 (which already lists 1m/5m/15m/1H).

### T162 — WITHOUT THE 3-TIMEFRAME READ, 7 OF 10 TRADES LOSE

*"If you take trades without analysing these three — 5 min, 15 min, 1 hour — trend, then pattern, then candle, you will lose 7 trades out of 10."* (012 · 00:44:50)
*"Because they know you will take it [at support/resistance], out of 10, three trades go, seven go negative."* (012 · 00:22:30)

A claim, not a rule: plain support/resistance entries without the stack are expected to lose ~70%. *(heard twice, different contexts)*

### T163 — MINIMUM DISTANCE = AT LEAST THE BROKERAGE; DON'T SUB-DIVIDE TOO FINELY

*"If we attend a trade there must be at least enough distance for brokerage. Without even brokerage there is no need to take the trade — don't look too in-depth."* (012 · 00:36:44)

Consistent with (weaker than) T39/T41.

### T164 — A LOWER-TIMEFRAME BREAK CAN RUN TO A HIGHER-TIMEFRAME BOUNDARY IN ONE STRETCH

*"There is no compulsion that if it breaks the 5-minute it goes only to the 15-minute. In one stretch it can break the 5-minute boundary and go to the 4-hour boundary."* (012 · 00:43:20)
*"Wherever it stops fast, analyse that boundary's zone again."* (012 · 00:44:20)

No target is guaranteed; re-read at whatever boundary the move stops. *(heard once)*

### T165 — AFTER A WICK THROUGH THE LOW, THE BODY MUST FOLLOW INTO THE WICK WITHIN 3 CANDLES

*"It breaks the low and puts a wick. If it is going to go down, within the next three candles it must put a body into this wick area. Then again wick, then body, wick, body. Sometimes straight body. That is control of bear … the next three candles will be red."* (012 · 01:00:02–01:00:32)
*"It breaks aggressively here because it knows many put their stop loss here."* (012 · 01:00:32)

Continuation = alternating wick→body advances; three consecutive red candles = bear control. The aggressive break at the obvious stop location corroborates T101.

### T166 — RETURN REQUIREMENT FOR TRADING: 5–10× A BANK FIXED DEPOSIT

*"A lakh in the bank gives 6,000 a year … you must take nearly 10 times, 5 to 10 times at least … only then is there meaning in trading."* (012 · 00:51:26)

**Contradicts T82** (professional return 4–7%/yr): here trading must beat FD (~6%) by 5–10×. The caption's figures are garbled ("6000 means ₹60") *(unclear)*. *(heard once)*

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T154** | A candle's signal is judged over exactly the next 3 candles of its timeframe (1m→3 min, 5m→15 min, 1H→3h, 4H→12h, D→3d, W→3w, M→3mo) | **Yes** |
| **T155** | At a boundary, a body close beyond the prior candle's full range within 3 candles = continuation; wick-only for 3 candles = control passes to the other side | **Yes** |
| **T156** | After an outside close beyond the other side's last push, the push origin holds on closes (wicks allowed) | **Yes** |
| **T157** | After a control change the minimum expected move is 50% of the leg, often in one stretch; pause at 50% | **Yes** |
| **T158** | A leg that only wicks past its 50% for 3 candles reverses to its origin | **Yes** |
| **T159** | For continuation, none of the next 3 closes may be beyond the latest candle's 50% | **Yes** |
| **T160** | Price decides at the 50% line ~50–70% of the time, at 25/75 only ~25–50%; ignore 25/75 on 1m | **Yes** |
| **T161** | Stack: 5m short / 15m mid (base) / 1H long; enter off 5m; add 1m for the 7-hour Indian session | **Partly — a procedure, testable only via the rules it feeds** |
| **T162** | Entries at plain S/R without the 3-timeframe read lose ~7 of 10 | **Partly — needs a definition of the S/R entry** |
| **T163** | Skip any trade whose distance does not at least cover brokerage | **Yes** |
| **T164** | A 5m boundary break may run to a 4H boundary in one stretch; re-read where it stops | **No — no condition, just "may"** |
| **T165** | After a wick through the low, a body must enter the wick area within 3 candles for the fall to continue; 3 red in a row = bear control | **Yes** |
| **T166** | Trading must return 5–10× a bank FD (~6%/yr); contradicts T82 | **No — a goal, not a market rule; figures garbled** |


## Video 013 — Contraction between two 50% lines; how to draw a trendline and what its break means

He re-reads the 1H gold chart: a buyer fails at the 50%, the seller takes over, and price is squeezed between an upper and a lower 50% line. Most of the video is on trendlines: how he draws one (rotate from the extreme until it touches two bodies), the claim that price always returns to the line, and his main point that a trendline break is an exit/stop-adding signal, never an entry. Long stretches attack indicators, support/resistance teaching and options buying (not method).

### T167 — SQUEEZE BETWEEN TWO 50% LINES = CONTRACTION, THEN A ONE-CANDLE BLAST TO A BASE

*"This 50% stands as a barrier; it can't go up, it can't go down. Now the market starts to shrink … when it breaks, on one side it blasts badly — blast means in one candle. Wherever it breaks, up or down, the market goes as far as its base — here is a base, here is a base — in one candle it blasts."* (013 · 00:09:15–00:10:16)
*"If the market shrinks at the bottom, it is moving from the retailer's hand to the big player — he is buying and accumulating. If it shrinks at the top, the big rich man is transferring to the retailer at a high price."* (013 · 00:09:15–00:09:46)

When price is held between the 50% of the last up-leg (below) and the 50% of the larger range (above), expect a range break that runs to the base on that side in one candle. Corroborates and quantifies T104 (O pattern) by giving the target (the base) and the speed (one candle). *(heard once)*

### T168 — HOW FAR EACH ANALYSIS LOOKS AHEAD

*"A candle has only three: inside candle, outside candle, failed candle. Knowing that is enough to say how the next two candles will move. Likewise if you analyse a pattern, you can find how the market will be for the next one or two hours. Then if you analyse a trend, how the market will be for the next one or two days."* (013 · 00:13:50–00:14:22)
*"Reading on one candle works only for the next two or three candles; beyond that, if you read finished candles, it won't work."* (013 · 00:10:47)

Candle read → next 2–3 candles; pattern → next 1–2 hours; trend → next 1–2 days (timeframe not stated; the chart is 1H gold). Corroborates T154/T92.

### T169 — TRENDLINE DRAWING: START FLAT AT THE EXTREME, ROTATE UNTIL IT TOUCHES THE BODIES OF TWO CANDLES

*"Wherever the maximum high is … first put a straight line flat like this, bring it slowly, and wherever it hits the body of many candles, stop there. Simple."* (013 · 00:18:27)
*"Put it straight at this high, bring it slowly; where it hits the bodies of two candles, adjust and stop."* (013 · 00:27:36)
*"If it is nearby and we have only two candles, putting it at one candle's wick is enough; if the formation has more than one candle, keep it touching the body of one or two candles."* (013 · 00:42:19)

The anchor is the pattern's extreme (high for a falling line, low for a rising line); the line pivots until it touches ≥2 candle **bodies**. Refines T14 (T14 anchors on heads/highs); here the second touch point is on bodies, not wicks. He says the line "connects a pattern" (00:36:15).

### T170 — THE TRENDLINE IS A MAGNET: PRICE RETURNS TO IT

*"A trendline is like a magnet. However far the market goes below this trendline, the price is attracted back and the market meets the trendline again — the trendline exists only for this one concept."* (013 · 00:20:59–00:21:30)
*"However far it goes it will come back and touch the trendline. There is no history of the market not touching it."* (013 · 00:42:19)

Corroborates T73. He adds (00:23:34) that *when* it returns needs volume analysis, so it is "not for placing an order".

### T171 — TRENDLINE BREAK TARGET: THE TRENDLINE'S VALUE ABOVE THE EXTREME, ELSE THE BASE

*"When this trendline breaks, draw a vertical line at the candle where the ultimate high is and connect it to the line; it will definitely come back at least to that place."* (013 · 00:28:06)
*"There is a point here — I put it straight at the maximum low. The market will go to this point; going beyond it is somewhat difficult; if it goes, it goes to the previous point, its base."* (013 · 00:28:36–00:29:07)

The first target after a break is the point on the trendline vertically above/below the swing extreme; failing that, the base. The exact construction is only partly recoverable — he is pointing at the chart *(unclear)*.

### T172 — A TRENDLINE BREAK IS AN EXIT / STOP-ADDING SIGNAL, NEVER AN ENTRY

*"When a trendline breaks out it is not for taking an entry. The big rich man has reached that boundary, almost; from there we need not proceed far with the market. We can exit what we hold — don't keep it beyond this — that is what the trendline is for."* (013 · 00:31:08–00:31:38)
*"If it breaks the trendline upward, stop accumulating beyond this, the market is going to over value … If price comes down through the trendline, give away anything bought and come out; the market is going to under value."* (013 · 00:53:37)
*"If price breaks the trendline and comes down, the market is coming to under value — be ready to buy, analyse and buy."* (013 · 00:59:11–00:59:42)

Do not buy an upside break or sell a downside break. An upside break = the accumulation leg is done (stop adding, sell into it); a downside break = get ready to buy lower, after analysis. The captions contradict themselves on which side means what in a few places (00:43:22, 00:56:09) *(unclear)*; the consistent point is "break = not an entry".

### T173 — AFTER A BASE BREAK THE BIG PLAYER CARRIES PRICE 1.5–2× AND LETS IT RETRACE TO THE 50% FIRST

*"He won't lift it here … he will let it come to where your support is, let it come to 50%, and only then lift."* (013 · 00:39:47)
*"If it breaks the base and puts an outside candle … he will carry it about one-and-a-half to two times, test you in control of greed, dump it on you, and bring it down again."* (013 · 00:40:17)

Corroborates T108 (1.5–2× the last counter-move) and T97/T157 (pullback to 50% before continuation).

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T167** | Price trapped between two 50% lines is contracting; the break runs to the base on that side within one candle | **Yes** |
| **T168** | Candle read lasts 2–3 candles; pattern read 1–2 hours; trend read 1–2 days | **Partly — timeframe of the hours/days not stated** |
| **T169** | Trendline: start flat at the extreme, rotate until it touches ≥2 candle bodies (one wick allowed if only two candles) | **Yes** |
| **T170** | Price always returns to its trendline (magnet) | **Partly — no time limit given** |
| **T171** | After a trendline break, first target = trendline point vertically at the swing extreme, else the base | **Partly — construction shown on chart only** |
| **T172** | A trendline break is an exit/stop-adding signal, not an entry; downside break = prepare to buy lower after analysis | **Partly — side-mapping garbled in captions** |
| **T173** | After a base break the move runs ~1.5–2×; the big player allows a 50% retrace before lifting | **Yes — corroborates T108** |


## Video 014 — Failed vs pure outside candles, colour runs as bait, and trendline distance as under/over value

He replays the 1H gold chart with the 3-candle test at each 50% line (corroborating T154/T155/T167), then defines the "failed" candle and returns to trendlines: a trendline joins the heads of V patterns, and distance from the line measures under or over value. Roughly the second half of the video is a sermon on envy, prayer and religion (no method).

### T174 — FAILED CANDLE = CLOSES PAST THE PRIOR EXTREME WITH LESS THAN 50% OF ITS BODY OUTSIDE

*"Every two candles, look: inside candle? outside candle? failed candle? What is a failed candle? The close is beyond this candle's low. If this candle's body was not more than 50% beyond this price line and stayed inside, it is a failed candle. Here 100% of the body is outside — this is a pure outside candle. If price only peeped out and the body was fully inside, that is called a failed candle."* (014 · 00:22:54–00:23:25)
*"A candle has only three: inside candle, outside candle, failed candle."* (013 · 00:13:50)

Three candle types: inside, outside (≥50% of body beyond the prior high/low; "pure" if 100%), failed (it pokes or closes past but less than 50% of the body is beyond). This ties T95 (outside = close beyond) to T20 (≥50% body beyond) and names the in-between case.

### T175 — THREE SAME-COLOUR CANDLES IN A ROW ARE BAIT: BUY RED, NOT GREEN

*"If there are three continuous green candles like this, they are showing you greed to make you buy. If the market keeps coming as red candles continuously, they are showing you fear and snatching from you. What you must not do: don't fear when they show fear; don't move when they show greed."* (014 · 00:10:12–00:10:43)
*"Red candles belong to the seller, green to the buyer — correct. But you must not buy on green candles; you must buy on red candles. When ordinary people fear the market, it is available at a throwaway price; that is when the big player buys."* (014 · 00:08:10–00:08:41)
*"This is called a trap — that is why we must wait three candles."* (014 · 00:10:12)

A run of 3+ consecutive same-colour candles is a "control of fear/greed" leg meant to pull the crowd in; he buys into red and does not chase green. Consistent with T2 (buy only after 3+ red candles) and T53.

### T176 — A SLOW, SINGLE-LAYER APPROACH TO AN OBVIOUS SUPPORT PRECEDES AN AGGRESSIVE BREAK

*"You will think: the low has come, support is here, next it goes up. No — it breaks that support and goes aggressively, continuously putting more than three red candles. That is control of fear."* (014 · 00:25:26)
*"Where support is — ordinary people know it, and he knows that too. At that place he comes slowly, puts single layers — why doesn't he come fast? … As soon as it touches, the market goes aggressively, heavily red candles."* (014 · 00:25:57–00:26:28)
*"Single layer, single layer, double layer, double layer, triple layer, and only then does multiple layer start."* (014 · 00:25:26)

Layers usually build 1,1,2,2,3 before a multi-layer bounce. A slow drift of single layers into a well-known support is a set-up for a stop-run break (3+ red candles). Extends T98.

### T177 — A TRENDLINE JOINS THE TWO HEADS OF A V PATTERN

*"A trendline is connecting a V pattern. If you keep connecting each V pattern you get the trendline. … If there is one V pattern, connecting the two heads of that V pattern is a trend[line]."* (014 · 00:23:55–00:24:56)

Refines T169/T14: the anchor points are the heads of consecutive V (or A) patterns, not arbitrary candles.

### T178 — DISTANCE FROM THE TRENDLINE = UNDER / OVER VALUE

*"If price stands far below a trendline, it is under value; if price is far above a trendline, it is over value. That's all."* (014 · 00:32:33–00:33:03)
*"However far into under value, it will first come to the trendline and then go past the trendline into over value; likewise however over-valued, it will break the trendline and come down to under value."* (014 · 00:33:03)
*"When it is in over value, sell; when under value, buy — analyse and buy. Don't take it blindly."* (014 · 00:46:45)

An alternative to T38's "below the 50% on every timeframe" definition of under value: price far below its trendline. Buy only in under value, after checking big-player involvement. No distance threshold given *(unclear)*.

### T179 — ONCE THE BIG PLAYER HAS FINISHED BUILDING, THE TARGET COMES WITHIN ABOUT TWO CANDLES

*"Once the big player has come he won't delay there; immediately the market blasts … it goes fast and hits the target. You would have waited 10 candles, 20 candles to take that position; within the next two candles the target will go."* (014 · 00:35:03–00:35:33)

Falsifiable: after a completed accumulation (contraction plus outside break), the move to target takes far fewer candles than the build-up. Corroborates T107. *(heard once)*

### T180 — THE TIME TO RETURN TO A TRENDLINE SCALES WITH THE TIMEFRAME OF THE V THAT DREW IT

*"How to find how many days it will take? If you drew a 5-minute trendline V pattern, it takes that many 5-minutes — so that many hours. If you connect a one-hour V pattern, it takes that many days. … These are day candles — like this it takes that many weeks to come back here."* (014 · 00:48:45–00:50:20)

Gives T170 a time scale: a 5m line is revisited within hours, a 1H line within days, a daily line within weeks. Corroborates T74. The exact multiplier is not stated *(unclear)*.

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T174** | Candle types: inside; outside (≥50% of body beyond the prior extreme, "pure" at 100%); failed (poke/close past with <50% of body beyond) | **Yes** |
| **T175** | 3+ consecutive same-colour candles are a fear/greed bait leg; buy into red, never chase green | **Yes** |
| **T176** | Layers build 1,1,2,2,3 before a multi-layer bounce; a slow single-layer drift into obvious support is followed by an aggressive break (3+ red) | **Yes** |
| **T177** | A trendline joins the heads of consecutive V (or A) patterns | **Yes** |
| **T178** | Far below the trendline = under value (buy after analysis); far above = over value (sell) | **Partly — "far" not quantified** |
| **T179** | After accumulation completes, the target is hit within ~2 candles | **Yes** |
| **T180** | Return-to-trendline time scales with the timeframe: 5m → hours, 1H → days, D → weeks | **Partly — no multiplier** |


## Video 015 — Equal / Failed / Excess A and V; pattern validity by timeframe; win rate by boundary

He reads an up-move leg by leg: he classifies each retracement as an Equal, Failed or Excess A (and V), calls a push beyond the prior push an "outside pattern", and measures buyer strength by comparing leg sizes in points. He also gives the forward validity of a candle read and a pattern read per timeframe, and win/loss rates for trading at short-, mid- and long-term boundaries. The last ~40% is about his time, clients and why he won't answer chart questions (no method).

### T181 — READ ONLY THE CURRENT STRUCTURE: THE CURRENT MARKET WORKS ~70%, FINISHED MARKET ~30%

*"Always reset the chart … start reading from the visible high or visible low. If there is a high earlier here, don't consider it; it is already finished. Current-market analysis is always very effective. You can analyse finished market too, but that works only about 30%. Only the current market has a chance of working about 70%."* (015 · 00:00:31–00:01:02)

Anchor all measurements on the current visible swing, not older highs/lows. Consistent with T36 (re-mark the mother) and T154. *(heard once)*

### T182 — EQUAL / FAILED / EXCESS A (AND V)

*"An A can be 25, 50, 75 or up to 100 — this is an Equal A. … It doesn't come here, it breaks straight up — this is also an A; what is its name? It could not come to this base; it is Failed. … This A crossed 100 and came below — it is an XS [excess] A. Equal A, Failed A, XS A — only these three A's. The V pattern is the same."* (015 · 00:02:03–00:02:33)
*"Equal A: the seller brought it back the same distance the buyer carried it. The seller could not bring it back as far as the buyer carried it: Failed A — and the V goes up beyond this base. The seller brought it below the place the buyer started from: Excess — remember it as Extra A."* (015 · 00:03:33–00:04:04)

The retracement of a leg is classed by how much of it is retraced: Failed (<100%, the head then breaks the other way), Equal (~100%, back to the leg's origin), Excess (>100%, beyond the origin). This supplies the definitions behind T22's "Equal / Failed / Extra V are skips".

### T183 — OUTSIDE PATTERN / INSIDE PATTERN FOR EACH SIDE

*"Did they carry it beyond this high, or below this low, or could they not cross either? … The second time the buyer carried it beyond this high — this is called an outside pattern, like the outside candle."* (015 · 00:20:46–00:21:17)
*"The second time, could the seller bring it beyond this low? No — this is an inside pattern."* (015 · 00:22:48–00:23:18)
*"Only if the seller carries it beyond the area they already brought it to have they put an outside pattern; otherwise it is inside."* (015 · 00:23:49)

Corroborates T105 and applies it per side: a buyer push beyond the prior push's high is the buyer's outside pattern; a seller leg that fails to take out the prior seller low is an inside pattern for the seller (and seller weakness).

### T184 — BUYER STRENGTH = SIZE OF EACH PUSH IN POINTS; A SHRINKING PUSH WARNS OF A REVERSAL

*"If this is 100 points, this must also be 100 points. This is 80 points. They broke this head and put a new outside — true. But first the buyer carried 100 points, the second time only 80 points: the buyer's involvement is there, but the buyer's strength is decreasing."* (015 · 00:24:50–00:25:21)
*"If the strength has reduced, the buyer must go back to the resting point — near where they started — and set off aggressively again; then they go one to two times beyond what the buyer went overall."* (015 · 00:28:22–00:28:52)

Corroborates T91 with a worked number (100 → 80). After a weaker push, expect a return toward the start of the move before any new 1–2× leg (T108).

### T185 — AFTER THE SELLER BREAKS THE PATTERN LINE, THE FALL RUNS 1–1.5×, AT MOST 2×, THE PRIOR SELLER LEG

*"When they break that place, look — they break aggressively … when it comes beyond all that, the seller is going to bring it two times as far as they came before — at least one to one-and-a-half times, at most up to two times."* (015 · 00:32:26–00:32:56)
*"If the low comes below the pattern already carried and it cannot break the upper pattern, the market will aggressively come to an even stronger distance — not an equal distance but one to two times more."* (015 · 00:33:27–00:33:57)

Target after a reversal-pattern break: 1–1.5× the previous seller leg (minimum), 2× (maximum). Corroborates T108 and T173.

### T186 — NEVER ENTER OFF A PATTERN; ENTRIES ARE ALWAYS TAKEN ON CANDLE ANALYSIS

*"Should you take a trade after seeing the pattern? You must not. Always take the trade only after analysing the candle. The pattern is for seeing the market's control more strongly, with a bit more validity … it is not for taking a trade. Always take a trade by analysing the candle — only then can you take the trade early and profit comes early; otherwise you will trade in over value."* (015 · 00:31:24–00:31:55)

The pattern sets direction and validity; the entry trigger is always a candle signal (T155 / T20 / T21). *(heard once, stated firmly)*

### T187 — FORWARD VALIDITY OF A CANDLE READ AND A PATTERN READ, BY TIMEFRAME

*"Candle analysis on 5-minute: how it will be for the next 15 minutes; 1-hour candle: the next three hours; 4-hour candle, taking the last two candles: the next 12 hours."* (015 · 00:10:10–00:10:40)
*"A 5-minute pattern shows how the market will be for one hour, two hours. On 15 minutes it shows how the market will be for about 6 hours. On one hour, about one day — nearly 18 … just multiply."* (015 · 00:11:10–00:11:41)
*"A candle analysis validity is 10, 15 minutes … then its validity expires. If you do an option with a 15-minute-validity candle analysis and profit comes only after two or three hours, the option will give you a loss."* (015 · 00:12:42)

Quantifies T92: candle read ×3 bars (T154); 5m pattern ≈ 1–2 h, 15m pattern ≈ 6 h, 1H pattern ≈ 1 day. Pick the timeframe whose validity covers the time the trade needs (critical for option buyers because of time decay).

### T188 — WIN RATE BY BOUNDARY: SHORT-TERM 3/10, MID-TERM 5/10, LONG-TERM 7/10

*"If you approach the market at a short-term boundary, 7 trades out of 10 will lose. At a mid-term boundary, only about 5 trades go to profit directly; 5 come as losses and you must do the mechanism to take them to profit. If you approach at long-term, 3 trades out of 10 lose; the other seven have a chance to go directly. Those three you must handle with the mechanism."* (015 · 00:16:42–00:17:13)
*"Even if you do it perfectly, out of 10 trades two or three will be losses; you must bring them to profit with the mechanism."* (015 · 00:19:15–00:19:45)

A falsifiable claim of first-attempt success by boundary level (short = 5m, mid = 15m, long = 1H per T161). It matches T28's idea that the lower level needs more trades. Size to the boundary (T48, T77): over-sizing wipes the account, under-sizing cannot recover earlier losses (00:15:42–00:16:12).

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T181** | Measure only from the current visible swing; analysis of current structure works ~70%, finished structure ~30% | **Partly — "works" undefined** |
| **T182** | Retracement classes: Failed (<100% of the leg, head breaks the other way), Equal (~100%), Excess (>100%) — for A and V alike | **Yes** |
| **T183** | A push beyond the prior same-side extreme = outside pattern; failure to exceed it = inside pattern (per side) | **Yes — corroborates T105** |
| **T184** | Compare successive push sizes in points; a smaller push (e.g. 100 → 80) = weakening side, expect a return toward the start before any new 1–2× leg | **Yes** |
| **T185** | After a reversal break of the pattern line, the new leg runs 1–1.5× (min) to 2× (max) the prior opposite leg | **Yes** |
| **T186** | Never enter off a pattern; entry only on a candle signal | **Yes** |
| **T187** | Validity: candle read = next 3 bars; 5m pattern ≈ 1–2 h, 15m pattern ≈ 6 h, 1H pattern ≈ 1 day | **Yes** |
| **T188** | First-attempt success: short-term boundary ~3/10, mid-term ~5/10, long-term ~7/10 | **Yes** |


## Video 016 — Reading a single candle: wick ownership, body-vs-wick control score, and "settlement"

On a daily chart he shows how to read each just-closed candle on its own: draw its 25/50/75 levels, give the lower wick to the buyer and the upper wick to the seller, and weigh body against wicks in points to decide who controls the next 1–2 hours. "Settlement" is the point where a run of fear candles stops producing bodies beyond the latest candle's 50%. He also chains 50% targets up the timeframes and gives a profit-per-trade ceiling relative to capital. Long sales/biography segments are skipped.

### T189 — BREAKING A LOWER TIMEFRAME'S 50% TARGETS THE NEXT TIMEFRAME'S 50%

*"If the market is going beyond the base of the 5-minute, go to 15 minutes, upgrade and draw the visible high and low; only then will you know the market is targeting the 15-minute 50%. If it travels beyond that too, come to one hour, then 4 hour, day, week, month — upgrade everything. When the market is falling, downgrade each frame."* (016 · 00:01:00–00:01:31)
*"If the market has crossed the 5-minute's 50%, it means next it is targeting the 15-minute's 50%."* (016 · 00:02:01)
*"Knowing it goes to 50% — is there any use? None. … At that place you must do candle analysis again."* (016 · 00:02:32–00:03:03)

A chained target: 5m 50% → 15m 50% → 1H 50% → 4H → D. The target alone is not an entry (T186). Consistent with T23/T38; compare T164.

### T190 — LOWER WICK = BUYER, UPPER WICK = SELLER, WHATEVER THE BODY COLOUR

*"Instead of remembering 'opposite colour', how to remember it easily: the lower wick always belongs to the buyer. Look at this candle — it is a green candle; the lower one belongs to the buyer. The upper wick belongs to the seller. Upper wick — seller; lower wick — buyer."* (016 · 00:11:44)
*"If the upper wick and the body are red, 100% is in the seller's control. If the lower wick and body are green, 100% belongs to the buyer."* (016 · 00:30:01)

**Refines/contradicts T100** ("wick belongs to the side opposite the body colour"). The two agree for a green candle's upper wick and a red candle's lower wick. They disagree for a green candle's lower wick and a red candle's upper wick, which T100 gives to the opposite side and T190 gives by position (lower = buyer, upper = seller). Here he calls the position rule the simpler way to remember the same idea, so the coordinator should decide which one governs.

### T191 — CONTROL SCORE = BODY POINTS vs WICK POINTS; THE LARGER SIDE OWNS THE NEXT 1–2 HOURS

*"You must look at how many points the body is and how many points the wick is. Say the body is 30 points — just an example — and the wick is 70 points. Then 70 points are in the buyer's control, 30 in the seller's. Overall 40 points are in the buyer's control, so for the next one or two hours the market is likely to be in the buyer's control."* (016 · 00:19:51–00:20:23)
*"The wick is about 60%, the body only 40%. The body belongs to the buyer; an upper wick always belongs to the seller. So 60% is seller, 40% buyer; overall this candle is in the seller's control, so there is a probability of coming down."* (016 · 00:21:56–00:22:27)
*"90% is red body, 10% is the buyer's wick — it is in the seller's control."* (016 · 00:32:05)

Per candle: add the points owned by each side (body → its colour; lower wick → buyer; upper wick → seller, per T190); the side with more points controls the next bars (1–2 hours on the chart he uses). A mechanical candle score, easy to backtest.

### T192 — INDECISION CANDLE = EQUAL WICKS ON BOTH SIDES

*"If it is both below and above, the buyer is strong and the seller is strong — called an indecision candle, a candle that can't decide … equal wick above and equal wick below: the big buyer–seller fight is equal."* (016 · 00:16:50–00:17:20)
*"If both are there at the same time, it is called an equal fight; at such times the market shrinks a lot and starts putting O patterns."* (016 · 00:08:10)

Links a candle feature to T104 (O pattern / contraction).

### T193 — "SETTLEMENT": A FEAR RUN ENDS WHEN THE NEXT CANDLES CAN'T PUT A BODY BEYOND THE LATEST CANDLE'S 50%

*"If it is to go, it must never break this current candle's base, its 50%, and come below. This is called 'the market has settled.' Settled means: a market that came down fast as control of fear, as a sell trend, has settled — the money changes hands from one to another. The market in the seller's control has changed into the buyer's hand."* (016 · 00:35:09–00:35:40)
*"This is a day candle; on the day it puts control of fear; if it breaks this, it will put control of fear on the weekly — three or more red candles to scare you. Because it put those three red candles you can understand the market is about to settle."* (016 · 00:35:40–00:36:10)

Buy-side trigger: after 3+ red candles (T175/T2), the reversal is confirmed when subsequent candles fail to close a body below the last candle's 50% (and a candle then closes above it — T194).

### T194 — AT A SUPPORT, WAIT FOR THE CANDLE TO CLOSE; BUY ONLY IF A LATER BODY CLOSES ABOVE ITS 50%

*"You think there is support here, you want to buy — OK. A candle has come here. Don't take it immediately; let that candle finish. … Is the market settled? No — 100% of the body is in the seller's control and the close is below 50%. If you are buying here, at least the body must go above this 50% line — not the wick."* (016 · 00:43:20–00:43:51)

A concrete entry filter: at the level, the buy is valid only after a candle closes its body above the 50% of the prior (seller) candle. Consistent with T1/T20/T21 and T159.

### T195 — PROFIT PER TRADE ≈ CAPITAL WITH TWO ZEROS REMOVED: ₹10,000 → ₹100–999 PER TRADE

*"If you have ₹10,000, cut two zeros — your profit will be in multiples of ₹100. At most three digits — up to 999. If you invest ₹10,000 and even ₹1,000 comes, your account will be squared off. Write it down."* (016 · 00:55:34–00:56:34)
*"If ₹10,000 suddenly becomes one lakh, it means within the next two or three trades it will all go in the market."* (016 · 00:57:36–00:58:06)
*"Every third trade your account must keep growing … ₹10,000 … after the third trade +₹100 … again +₹100. Only then are you 100% fit for this business."* (016 · 01:12:27–01:12:58)

Per-trade profit target ≈ 1–9.99% of capital. A win of ≥10% of the account in one trade signals over-sizing. Health check: the equity curve should make a new high at least every third trade (also 012 · 00:46:53, 00:49:25). Consistent with T63/T83 on the sizing side.

### T196 — TARGET ANNUAL RETURN: 3–5× A BANK FD NORMALLY, 5–7× WITH ADVANCED FUND MANAGEMENT

*"If I put one lakh in the bank I get ₹6,000 a year; I can take about three to five times that. If I do advanced fund management I can take five to seven times."* (016 · 00:59:09)

Corroborates T57 (3–5×, 5–7×) and puts it against a bank FD (~6%/yr → ~18–42%/yr). This conflicts with T82 (4–7%/yr) and differs from T166 (5–10×).

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T189** | A break of one timeframe's 50% targets the next higher timeframe's 50% (5m → 15m → 1H → 4H → D); a target is not an entry | **Yes** |
| **T190** | Lower wick = buyer, upper wick = seller regardless of body colour (partly contradicts T100) | **Yes** |
| **T191** | Candle control score: points owned by each side (body + wick by position); the larger side controls the next 1–2 hours | **Yes** |
| **T192** | Equal upper and lower wicks = indecision / equal fight, leading to contraction (O pattern) | **Yes** |
| **T193** | A fear run "settles" when the following candles fail to close a body beyond the latest candle's 50%; control passes | **Yes** |
| **T194** | At a support, buy only after a later candle's body closes above the 50% of the prior seller candle (wicks don't count) | **Yes** |
| **T195** | Per-trade profit ≈ 1–9.99% of capital (₹10k → ₹100–999); ≥10% in one trade signals over-sizing; equity should make a new high every ~3 trades | **Partly — a sizing heuristic, testable on a trade log** |
| **T196** | Target annual return 3–5× bank FD (5–7× advanced) — corroborates T57, conflicts with T82 | **No — goal, not a market rule** |


## Video 017 — Fear runs climbing the timeframes; upgrade/downgrade in practice; no stop loss; scaling capital

He sketches how the market is built: a fear run on 5m settles and the buyer lifts; then a fear run on 15m, then 1H, then 4H, and each lift is larger. On 1H gold he shows a base break leading him to upgrade to 4H and find the target at the 4H 50%. He then argues against trading with a stop loss, and ends with a doubling ladder for adding capital. Much of the video is motivation, the "elephant chain" myth-busting and an anti-flattery segment (no method).

### T197 — FEAR RUNS ESCALATE THROUGH THE TIMEFRAMES; EACH BUYER LIFT IS LARGER THAN THE LAST

*"Say this is 5 minutes. On 5 minutes control of fear ends in the market … only after that does the market settle and go up in the buyer's control. Then on 15 minutes control of fear starts again. Then the buyer takes control and carries it up — each time more than the buyer carried it before."* (017 · 00:02:06)
*"Next the market takes control on one hour … after that the buyer carries it one-and-a-half to two times like this. Then on 4 hour … they carry it one or two times the highest the buyer carried before. That is how the market's full structure is."* (017 · 00:02:38–00:03:08)
*"Each time the buyer's involvement must keep increasing. If not, it means it is not fundamentally strong; it is going down to settle at zero."* (017 · 00:04:09–00:04:39)

A falsifiable model: successive pullbacks appear on rising timeframes (5m → 15m → 1H → 4H), and each subsequent up-leg is 1–2× the previous one (T108). If the up-legs stop growing, treat the instrument as weak (T91).

### T198 — WORKED EXAMPLE: A 1H BASE BREAK TARGETS THE 4H 50%, WHERE THE MARKET SETTLES

*"If on one hour the market broke this base, what happens? Next it will try to go to the 4-hour's 50%. That's the thing — it goes there. See, it went there and settled."* (017 · 00:16:21–00:16:51)
*"Each time the market keeps travelling to different boundaries. It need not travel slowly; in one candle it can go to 4 hour, to day."* (017 · 00:16:51)

Corroborates T189 (chained 50% targets) and T164.

### T199 — BREAKING THE HEAD OF THE PRIOR V/A IS AGGRESSIVE, AFTER A SLOW APPROACH

*"Whenever the head of the previous V pattern, the upper head, the A pattern's head is broken, the market comes aggressively. … On 5 minutes it would have come slowly; when it crosses that place the market comes aggressively."* (017 · 00:21:58–00:22:29)

Corroborates T99/T101 and T176.

### T200 — NO STOP LOSS WHILE TRADING; USE ONE ONLY "LIKE A BRAKE" WHERE CANDLE ANALYSIS SAYS SO

*"That is why we never put a stop loss anywhere when we trade. What does it mean not to put a stop loss? That carefulness comes to us — we must use our money correctly. If this car had no brake, how carefully would you drive?"* (017 · 00:32:13–00:32:44)
*"Those doing demo for a week in this session — nobody must put a stop loss. No, strictly."* (017 · 00:35:17)
*"Strictly put a stop loss, they say. You should put one — after analysing the market, analysing the candle, knowing whose control it really is — use a stop loss like using a car's brake, where needed."* (017 · 00:33:14–00:33:44)

Corroborates T11/T45 (no stop on the ladder; reason is psychological). It also softens T12/T13: a stop is allowed only as a deliberate, analysis-based exit (T13's "confirmed risk"), never as a pre-set safety net that justifies over-sizing.

### T201 — ADD CAPITAL ONLY AFTER THE ACCOUNT DOUBLES: 10k → 20k (+20k) → 40k → 80k (+1L) …

*"Do it small: make 10 into 20,000, then you can put another 20,000 — 20 plus 20 is 40. Make this 80, bring another one lakh … one plus one, 2 lakh; make it 4 lakh, bring another 4 lakh — now 8 lakh. Load the 10 lakh like this."* (017 · 01:09:45)
*"If ₹10,000 suddenly becomes one lakh…"* (016 · 00:57:36) — and, on scale, *"If you have thousands, earn in hundreds."* (017 · 00:42:55)

Capital policy: start small (~₹10k), and add new money only after the current account has doubled, roughly matching the account's size. Consistent with T195/T46.

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T197** | Pullbacks (fear runs) appear on rising timeframes (5m → 15m → 1H → 4H); each following up-leg is 1–2× the prior one; if legs stop growing the instrument is weak | **Yes** |
| **T198** | A 1H base break targets, and settles at, the 4H 50% (worked example of T189) | **Yes** |
| **T199** | Breaking the head of the prior V/A after a slow approach produces an aggressive move | **Yes — corroborates T99/T101** |
| **T200** | No pre-set stop loss; a stop is used only as an analysis-based "brake" (corroborates T11/T45; qualifies T12) | **Partly — the discretionary brake is not specified** |
| **T201** | Add new capital only after the account doubles (10k → 20k +20k → 40k → 80k +1L …) | **Partly — a capital policy, testable on an equity simulation** |


## Video 018 — "Upgrade" and "downgrade": stepping the range ×3, bases as zones, shrinking swings as contraction

On Bitcoin (5m up to monthly) he defines "upgrade" (step up to the next range about three times larger and find its 50%) and "downgrade" (step down, or re-measure successive swings within one frame, to see the range shrinking). A base is the body-to-wick zone of the origin candle, not a line. He shows how control can differ by timeframe and how a 5m base break cascades into the 15m and 1H bases. Long opening and closing talks on attitude, why he teaches, and religion (no method).

### T202 — UPGRADE = THE NEXT RANGE ABOUT 3× LARGER (5m → 15m → 1H → 4H → D → W → M), CHOSEN BY PRICE RANGE

*"Why not take 10 minutes? After short term we must look at mid term, and mid term is a frame about three times this. Five minutes ×3 and above is 15 minutes; 15 minutes ×3 is one hour; one hour ×3 is four hour; four hour ×3 and above is day; day ×3 or more is week; week ×3 is month. You must keep upgrading like this; the market follows the next boundaries like that."* (018 · 00:13:16–00:13:47)
*"We don't go for the standard frame. The price range must be three times: if ₹100, next in multiples of ₹300; if ₹300, next 900. We choose the frame with that price range."* (018 · 00:24:33–00:25:04)
*"This range is 100 points on 5 minutes; 300 on 15 minutes; 500 on one hour; 1,000 on day — that is upgrade."* (018 · 00:26:06)

The next boundary to watch is the range roughly three times the current one. The frame name matters less than the range. Pairs with T189 (each 50% chains to the next) and T154 (×3 validity).

### T203 — A BASE IS THE BODY-TO-WICK ZONE OF THE ORIGIN CANDLE; THINK IN BOUNDARIES (LINE TO LINE), NOT ONE LINE

*"A base must not be seen just as a base; its body and wick is its base. See, this candle's body is here, the wick here — this is what we call the boundary, this is the base: body–wick."* (018 · 00:15:18–00:15:49)
*"Never look at a base only; look at the boundary — this line to this line."* (018 · 00:22:29)

The base (target) is a zone from the origin candle's body edge to its wick tip. The seller must close beyond the whole zone to be "strong". Refines the "base" target used in T157/T158/T167.

### T204 — THREE 50% LINES STACKED CLOSE TOGETHER = CONTRACTION; LOW BIG-PLAYER VOLUME; A FAST BREAK EITHER WAY

*"Now there are three 50% lines: one here, two here, three here. It means the market is starting to shrink … a shrinking market is very dangerous. It means big volume is not involved; mostly retailers; FII, DII are there but very few. So it will break this upper 50% line and go fast, or break the lower 50% and come down."* (018 · 00:19:52–00:20:24)

Corroborates T167/T104 with a mechanical trigger: the 50% lines of successive timeframes lying close together.

### T205 — DOWNGRADE WITHIN A FRAME: SUCCESSIVE SWINGS SHRINKING (100 → 75 → 40 → 30 → 20) = SETTLING / CONTRACTION

*"This whole is 100 points; this is 75. From here it went 100, came back 75; went again about 40, came about 30; went about 30, came about 20. That's how we downgrade — look at the market closer and closer. You can do it within one hour itself."* (018 · 00:36:47–00:37:18)
*"A ball dropped from above: the first bounce goes a certain height, the second a little less … as the distance reduces the force reduces … finally it rolls and stops — it settles. That is how the market settles."* (018 · 00:28:07–00:28:37)

Operational definition of "settling": the alternating swing sizes on one timeframe decrease monotonically. Links T193 (settlement) and T104 (O pattern). The numbers are his illustration, not measurements *(illustrative)*.

### T206 — CONTINUATION NEEDS BOTH BODY-BEYOND-BODY AND WICK-BEYOND-WICK

*"If it is going down further: wick beyond wick, body beyond body. Here the body came beyond the body, but did the wick come beyond the wick? No. So the other side's involvement has increased."* (018 · 00:08:40)

Refines T165: a new body extreme without a new wick extreme is a warning, not a confirmation.

### T207 — CONTROL CAN DIFFER BY TIMEFRAME; A 5m BASE BREAK CASCADES TO THE 15m, THEN THE 1H BASE

*"Long term the market is in the seller's control. Mid term (15m) is in the buyer's control … if a 5-minute candle puts a body below, it comes into the seller's control; since long term is already seller's, they will come down aggressively … it can come to this 15-minute base, this 5-minute base; if they break this too the market goes aggressively to the 15-minute base; if that breaks too, to the one-hour base."* (018 · 00:39:21–00:40:22)

When the higher timeframe is already against the lower timeframe's trend, a lower-timeframe base break cascades aggressively through the bases above it. Consistent with T189/T23.

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T202** | Upgrade to the range ~3× larger (5m → 15m → 1H → 4H → D → W → M; chosen by price range) and watch its 50% | **Yes** |
| **T203** | A base is the body-to-wick zone of the origin candle; a side is strong only after closing beyond the whole zone | **Yes** |
| **T204** | 50% lines of successive timeframes stacked close together = contraction, followed by a fast break either way | **Partly — "close" not quantified** |
| **T205** | Successively shrinking swing sizes on one timeframe = settling / contraction | **Yes** |
| **T206** | Continuation requires a new body extreme and a new wick extreme; a body-only extension signals opposite involvement | **Yes** |
| **T207** | When the higher timeframe opposes the lower, a lower-timeframe base break cascades through the next bases (5m → 15m → 1H) | **Yes** |


## Video 019 — Recap: inside/outside/failed at candle, pattern and trend level; 0–50 / 50–75 / 75–100 zones; major vs minor patterns

A recap on Bitcoin (5m/15m/1H). He restates candle → pattern → trend (letter → word → sentence) and Equal/Failed/Extra V (T182). New points: the inside/outside/failed classification applies at the pattern and trend levels too, the body-vs-wick control score (T191) applies to whole patterns, and he splits any range into short-term (0–50), mid-term (50–75) and long-term (75–100) zones. Mostly recap plus the usual warnings and sales talk.

### T208 — INSIDE / OUTSIDE / FAILED APPLIES TO CANDLES, PATTERNS AND TRENDS ALIKE

*"A candle has only three: inside, outside, failed. Likewise a pattern: inside pattern, outside pattern, failed pattern. Likewise a trend: inside trend, outside trend, failed trend."* (019 · 00:17:19)
*"From here to here it came, and couldn't break this high or low in the next trend — so this is an inside trend. From here to here: it broke the high and the low and put many candles outside — this is called an outside trend."* (019 · 00:17:19–00:18:19)
