Extends T105/T174/T183 to the trend level: a trend (sequence of patterns) that exceeds the prior trend's extreme is an outside trend. Breaking out goes candle → pattern → trend (00:21:24: "outside candle, then outside pattern, then outside trend").

### T209 — CONTROL SCORE ALSO WORKS FOR A PATTERN: COMPARE THE SELLER LEG WITH THE BUYER LEG

*"From here the seller brought it this far; the buyer brought only this much. About 85–90 on the seller's side, only 5% on the buyer's side — 95% of this pattern is in the seller's control; then the market will only go down. What we saw for the candle, we must see for the trend too."* (019 · 00:27:34)
*"Here the current candle closed below 50%; the body belongs to the seller, only the lower wick to the buyer. Overall 70% seller, 30% buyer."* (019 · 00:03:33–00:04:05)

Applies T191's candle scoring to patterns: compare opposing leg lengths within the pattern. The captions contradict themselves on the 70/30 candle's conclusion ("overall in buyer's control") *(unclear — likely a slip for "seller")*.

### T210 — MAJOR vs MINOR PATTERNS: A PUSH THAT FAILS TO MATCH THE PRIOR PUSH'S DISTANCE IS MINOR ("FAILED")

*"Failed pattern — what does it mean? The buyer carried it this far before. Next the buyer didn't carry it this far, couldn't, couldn't — these are failed patterns. Micro patterns … these are macro patterns: the buyer must carry it as far as before."* (019 · 00:10:43–00:11:14)
*"Major means it went beyond how far the buyer carried before … it couldn't carry that distance, put only single layers — these are minor patterns."* (019 · 00:25:00)

Only patterns whose push meets or exceeds the previous push count as major. Corroborates T184/T91 and T106.

### T211 — ZONES OF ANY RANGE: 0–50% SHORT TERM, 50–75% MID TERM, 75–100% LONG TERM

*"If we divide this into four … in a market going up from the bottom, up to 50 is short term, 50–75 is mid term, 75–100 is long term."* (019 · 00:12:45–00:13:16)
*"Inside a candle too: from here to the 50% is short term, 50–75 mid term, 75–100 long term. We don't split micro; we understand it simply."* (019 · 00:33:42–00:34:13)
*"Short term of short term, mid term of short term … long term of long term"* (019 · 00:34:13–00:34:44) — nine combinations with the timeframe level.

A retracement's depth sets its "term": inside the first half is short-term, 50–75 mid-term, 75–100 long-term. Combined with the timeframe's own term (5m/15m/1H, T161) this gives a 3×3 grid used for sizing (T77/T188). How exactly the grid maps to size is not stated *(unclear)*.

### T212 — ONE STEP UP IN RANGE IS 2–3× (BTC EXAMPLE: 500 → 1,000 → 5,000 POINTS ON 5m / 15m / 1H)

*"We go to 15 minutes not for the 15-minute chart but because the price there is two or three times this range. See, 67,500 to 66,500 — 1,000; there it was 500."* (019 · 00:29:35–00:30:07)
*"69,000 to 64,000 — nearly 5,000 points difference. First 500, second 1,000, third 5,000. That is how the buyer's involvement and the movement are."* (019 · 00:40:48)

Worked numbers for T202 (he says 2–3× here, ×3 in 018). Also a warning: *"You'll think the market fell 1,000 points so it will rise this much — it won't; if it rises, check its boundary"* (00:40:48–00:41:19), which corroborates T76.

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T208** | Inside/outside/failed classification applies at candle, pattern and trend level; a breakout escalates outside candle → outside pattern → outside trend | **Yes** |
| **T209** | Pattern control score: compare the opposing legs' lengths within the pattern; the dominant side's share predicts direction | **Yes** |
| **T210** | A push that fails to match the prior push's distance is a minor/failed pattern; only pushes ≥ the prior one are major | **Yes** |
| **T211** | Range zones: 0–50% short term, 50–75% mid term, 75–100% long term (applies to a leg or a single candle) | **Partly — use of the 3×3 grid not specified** |
| **T212** | Each higher range is ~2–3× the prior (BTC: 500 → 1,000 → 5,000 points on 5m/15m/1H) | **Yes** |


## Video 020 — The 3×3 term grid with loss counts, the four-phase market cycle, volatile hours for NIFTY and Bitcoin

Live gold 5m→15m→1H read (ranges of about 200/300/500 points). He gives a loss table for the nine "term of term" combinations and says how tightening (technical/fundamental, then fund management) lowers those counts. He also gives the high-volume ("violent") hours for NIFTY and Bitcoin, a four-phase cycle (opening → greed → satisfaction → fear → attraction), and his client allocation of 70% US stocks, 20% gold, 10% Bitcoin. A long middle section is about helmets, sayings and religion (no method).

### T213 — THE 3×3 TERM GRID: FIRST-ATTEMPT LOSSES OUT OF 10 FALL AS THE ZONE DEEPENS

*"Within short term there are three: short term of short term, short term of mid term, short term of long term. In short-of-short, seven trades out of 10 lose, you have to do mechanism; in mid-term's short term about six lose; in long-term's short term about five."* (020 · 00:04:06–00:04:38)
*"Short term's mid term — five trades lose … mid of mid, only four; long term's mid term, only three. Short term's long term — only three lose."* (020 · 00:05:09–00:05:39)
*"If you tighten technically and fundamentally, these three losses reduce to one or two, five to three, seven to four or three. If you tighten further with fund management, four can become two or three."* (020 · 00:03:05–00:03:36)

Refines T188 into a 3×3 grid (timeframe term × zone term, T211). The pairing of Tamil "X-ோட Y" phrases to cells is partly garbled, so the exact mapping is *(unclear)*. Reading them as written:

| Zone ↓ / timeframe → | short | mid | long |
|---|---|---|---|
| short (0–50%) | 7 | 6 | 5 |
| mid (50–75%) | 5 | 4 | 3 |
| long (75–100%) | 3 | — | — |

*(table built from his spoken numbers; cells marked — were not stated)*

### T214 — SHORT-OF-SHORT ENTRIES ONLY GET A SINGLE LAYER; THE PROFIT MAY NOT EVEN COVER BROKERAGE

*"Straight at a short term's short term, if you buy there, the buyer's involvement is only that much — the market will put only a single layer. The profit that comes won't even cover brokerage. Then the market reverses; then you have to do it at the next boundary."* (020 · 00:04:38)

Links T213 to T96 (single layer = weak) and T39/T163 (cost floor): skip the shallowest zone on the lowest timeframe.

### T215 — HIGH-VOLUME ("VIOLENT") HOURS: NIFTY FIRST HOUR AND LAST PART OF THE SESSION; BITCOIN 18:00–22:00 IST

*"In NIFTY, morning 9 to 10 is very violent hours … after that the rest are silent hours … then the last, 3:15–3:30 … again violent hours for NIFTY."* (020 · 00:11:14–00:11:47)
*"Violent hours — you can call them volume hours … the first one hour and the last two hours, as far as NIFTY is concerned; the other hours are somewhat silent."* (020 · 00:11:47–00:12:18)
*"In Bitcoin, Indian-time evening from about 6 pm to night 10 pm is very violent … in 24 hours these four hours are very violent. Next, the European hours in the afternoon are somewhat less; the remaining Asian hours are silent."* (020 · 00:12:18–00:12:49)

**Corroborates T93** for NIFTY, but he gives the closing window inconsistently ("3:15–3:30" vs "last two hours") *(unclear)*. **New for Bitcoin:** 18:00–22:00 IST is the high-volume window, European afternoon is medium, Asian hours are quiet. Choose the frame for the hour (NIFTY: add the 1m, 00:10:12).

### T216 — FOUR-PHASE CYCLE ON EVERY CHART: OPENING → GREED → SATISFACTION → FEAR → ATTRACTION

*"First there is price of opening. If it goes up, it goes to price of satisfaction; before it ends there, it goes up in control of greed again and again … at some point, price of satisfaction — it is over value, those who bought cheap sell everything. Then it comes slowly … and from there it falls like from a hill — control of fear … and it settles at price of attraction, where big investors buy from you at throwaway prices. Again price of opening, again control of greed … Only these four things happen in the market, on every chart in the world."* (020 · 00:19:26–00:21:30)
*"First the market comes slowly like stairs (escalator), then falls vertically like falling off a staircase; going up, it goes like a rocket."* (020 · 00:14:50)

The cycle behind T175/T178/T193: slow drift into obvious levels, then a vertical 3+ candle run, then settlement at under or over value. Descriptive, not a trigger on its own.

### T217 — A RUN THAT REACHES 75% BUT CAN'T CLOSE A BODY BEYOND IT RETURNS TO THE LEG'S 50%

*"From here it came beyond 50 to about 75. Now if it can't put a candle body or wick beyond 75, the market will come to its 50% — this is trend analysis."* (020 · 01:00:31)

Extends T158 (failure at the 50%) to failure at the 75%, with the same target (the leg's 50%).

### T218 — SUCCESSIVE PUSHES: EACH LARGER = "MACRO LAYER" (BIG PLAYER IN); SMALLER = "MICRO LAYER"

*"From here the buyer carried it this far; the next time they could not carry it beyond — less — this is micro. Now they carried a bit more — macro … Macro means: the second push must be more than the first, the third more than that. Only then is it macro layering — the big player has jumped in. If this base doesn't break, the market will go one or two times like this."* (020 · 00:55:56–00:56:56)

Corroborates T184/T210 and T108.

### T219 — PORTFOLIO SPLIT HE USES FOR CLIENTS: 70% US STOCKS, 20% GOLD, 10% BITCOIN SPOT

*"If a client brings 100, 70 we invest in the US market, 20 in gold, the remaining 10 in Bitcoin — directly in spot … this 10% in Bitcoin will be in higher growth than the 70% in US and 20% in gold, because its volatility is different."* (020 · 00:30:08–00:30:38)

An allocation rule by volatility (spot, not leveraged, consistent with T40/T79). *(heard once)*

### T220 — 99% OF RETAIL FINISH THEIR INDIAN-MARKET TRADES WITHIN THE FIRST 10–15 MINUTES, AT A LOSS

*"From market opening, within 10 minutes, at most 15 minutes, 99% of retailers finish their trade in the Indian market — and finish at a loss."* (020 · 00:51:23)

A claim, not a rule; it supports avoiding the opening minutes unless the setup is at a proper boundary (compare T93, which calls the opening hour the violent hour to approach). *(heard once; source unstated)*

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T213** | First-attempt losses per 10 fall with zone depth and timeframe level (short-of-short 7 … down to 3); tightening lowers each further | **Partly — cell mapping garbled** |
| **T214** | Skip short-of-short entries on the lowest timeframe: single layer only; profit may not cover brokerage | **Yes** |
| **T215** | High-volume windows: NIFTY first hour and close; Bitcoin 18:00–22:00 IST (Europe medium, Asia quiet) | **Yes** |
| **T216** | Four-phase cycle (opening → greed → satisfaction → fear → attraction) on every chart and timeframe | **No — descriptive model** |
| **T217** | A leg that reaches 75% but can't close a body beyond it returns to the leg's 50% | **Yes** |
| **T218** | Macro layering = each successive push larger than the last (big player in, expect 1–2× continuation); otherwise micro | **Yes** |
| **T219** | Client allocation 70% US stocks / 20% gold / 10% Bitcoin spot | **Partly — allocation policy, testable as a portfolio** |
| **T220** | 99% of Indian retail finish trades within 10–15 min of the open, at a loss | **No — external statistic, unsourced** |

## Video 021 — Live review: boundaries, settling, greed/fear runs and the 20/30/50 time split

He continues the previous day's NIFTY 5-minute chart, re-deriving each move with candle → pattern → trend analysis, then recaps the vocabulary (equal/extra/failed V and A, O = opposite/inside pattern, major/minor layer, boundary = two price lines). New precise material: the definition of "control of greed/fear" as a run of 3+ same-colour candles with stepping bodies, the "3 candles held inside = the other side keeps control" rule, the escalation of break size by timeframe (3 red on 5m, then 15m, then 1H), the win-rate "nature" of short/mid/long boundaries, and a 20/30/50 capital split across short/mid/long term.

### T221 — CONTROL OF GREED / FEAR = 3+ STEPPING SAME-COLOUR CANDLES

*"What is control of greed? Continuously green — more than three green candles will go. And the next green body, compared to the previous green body, keeps closing strongly higher and higher."* (021 · 00:04:03)
*"Control of greed, what have I said: more than two green candles will print. At least three candles in a row will be green. The next body closes at a higher place than the previous body."* (021 · 00:10:08)
*"It will print continuously red candles. At least three candles… don't look at the green that comes in between — one body, the next body lower, the next body lower."* (021 · 00:50:21)

The operational form of a "blast" run: ≥3 consecutive candles of one colour, each body closing beyond the previous body's close (outside candles, T95). The mirror (red, lower closes) is "control of fear". He tolerates an interleaved small opposite candle *(00:50:21 — "don't look at the green in between")*, which makes "consecutive" slightly loose.

### T222 — THREE CANDLES HELD INSIDE AFTER A BOUNDARY TOUCH = ATTACKER HAS LOST CONTROL

*"If for one, two, three candles it maintains an inside close, then as I already said, the probability of the seller bringing it again past this body/wick is low — because the market has held here 15 minutes, 20 minutes."* (021 · 00:08:36)
*"If they put it inside and wait here 10 minutes, 20 minutes, the market has the maximum chance of going into the buyer's control."* (021 · 00:09:07)
*"For no reason must [price]… if for three candles it maintains body/wick beyond this base, the probability of the market going up is very, very low. It will go — it will take time."* (021 · 00:30:23)

After price reaches a 50% line/boundary, if the next 1–3 candles (≈15–20 min on 5m) close back inside the prior candle's body/wick rather than extending, the side that drove price there is spent and a turn is likely. Symmetric rule: 3 candles holding beyond a base makes a return through it unlikely. Complements T102 (30 min without a new outside close) with a shorter 3-candle window.

### T223 — REPEATED TESTS OF BOTH 50% LINES = SHRINKING MARKET THAT WILL BLAST

*"It hits here, hits here, hits here, hits here. It has happened four times — that is why I said yesterday the market is shrinking."* (021 · 00:03:02)
*"Up, down, up, down it hits… this stage is the settling stage… then the market will blast. When it blasts it goes very fast."* (021 · 00:03:32–00:04:03)

When price alternately touches the seller-leg 50% line above and the buyer-leg 50% line below (he counts four touches), the range is compressing and a fast break follows. Corroborates T104 (O pattern) with a touch count.

### T224 — SETTLING = CHAIN OF INSIDE CANDLES DRIFTING (NO BUYER STRENGTH)

*"It does not have to hit up and down. Inside candle, inside candle, inside candle inside each candle's body… it will seem to go up, lightly… If it moves slowly like that, it means the buyer's strength is not there. It is a symptom that it is about to stop completely."* (021 · 00:19:17–00:19:47)

A drift made of successive inside candles is exhaustion, not a trend. Consistent with T105 (inside = no-volume noise).

### T225 — MAJOR vs MINOR LAYER (BOUNCE SIZE vs PREVIOUS BOUNCE)

*"Major means: if they take it bigger than the size they took before, it is major… Is this more than this? No. So the buyer's control… should have increased, it has not, so we call this minor. Then the market will come down aggressively."* (021 · 00:22:49)
*"It turns at 25 itself… it means the buyer is not there at all… control is fully going to the seller."* (021 · 00:22:19)
*"If more than what was carried before, major layer; otherwise minor layer."* (021 · 00:33:25–00:33:56)

A counter-move is **major** if its height exceeds the previous counter-move in the same direction, **minor** otherwise; a minor bounce while price is making lower lows = aggressive continuation. Refines T91 (shrinking pushes) and T97 (25% bounce = seller in control) with a named test.

### T226 — BOUNCES CONFINED TO 0–25% → THE V LOW BREAKS AGGRESSIVELY

*"Zero, 25, 50, 75, 100 — it is only inside 0 to 25. It could not even reach 50. Then the market will break this low, break this V pattern and print a new V pattern… it goes down aggressively."* (021 · 00:51:53–00:52:25)

Repeated bounces off a support that never reach 50% of the range (each smaller than the last) → the support low breaks. Corroborates T97/T98.

### T227 — A SUPPORT BOUNCE MUST BE ~2x TO BE A REAL PROFIT

*"The profit coming here won't be big. It should be like two times — only then is it actual profit. Otherwise, what is this? Minor. This is only a layer, not a major layer."* (021 · 00:49:50–00:50:21)

*(heard once)* He dismisses buying repeated support touches: unless the bounce is ~2× (the previous bounce/the risk — *unclear which*), it is a minor layer and not worth it.

### T228 — BREAK SIZE ESCALATES BY BOUNDARY: 3 RED ON 5m, THEN 15m, THEN 1H

*"The first time it comes down, it will come printing three red candles on 5 minutes… The second time, it will come printing three red candles on 15 minutes — it will certainly be that far… Third time — you cannot take 'third time' [on the 5m]. You must draw each boundary: 5 minute, 15 minute, 1 hour… third time means third boundary: it will print three 1-hour red candles."* (021 · 00:27:51–00:28:21)
*"Whichever frame's boundary it breaks, that many candles — at least three red candles."* (021 · 00:30:53)
*"If its boundary is on 1 hour and it breaks, the market will go three 1-hour candles without stopping."* (021 · 01:00:33)

The size of a breakdown is measured in candles of the timeframe whose boundary broke: ≥3 consecutive red candles of THAT timeframe. Successive boundary failures escalate 5m → 15m → 1H. Links T23 (escalation) and T74 (speed scales with clock). He adds that after such a fall "you'll think it will rise as much — it certainly won't" (00:28:21), corroborating T76.

### T229 — BOUNDARY = TWO PRICE LINES; SHORT/MID/LONG = 0–50 / 50–75 / 75–100

*"Two price lines together are one boundary."* (021 · 00:16:14)
*"0 to 50 is the short-term boundary… 50 to 75 is the mid-term boundary, 75 to 100 the long-term boundary."* (021 · 00:38:34–00:39:05)
*"The 75 of this 15 minute has come here… it is the long-term boundary of the 5 minute."* (021 · 00:31:53)

Definitions: any two adjacent price lines (0/25/50/75/100) form a boundary; the retracement depth 0–50 is short-term, 50–75 mid-term, 75–100 long-term. Extends T97.

### T230 — FOR CANDLE AND PATTERN ANALYSIS DRAW ONLY THE 50%; 75 ONLY IN A TREND

*"When drawing for candle and pattern, 25 and 75 are not needed. Taking only the 50% price line as the major is enough."* (021 · 00:38:04)
*"I said: always draw the 75 when in a trend. Normally you need not draw 0.75."* (021 · 00:24:50)

### T231 — NATURE OF A BOUNDARY: 7/10, 5/10, 3/10 TRADES GO NEGATIVE

*"The nature of short term: 7 out of 10 trades will go negative… only with mechanism can you bring profit. Mid term: 5 out of 10 go negative. Long term: only 3 out of 10 go down and need mechanism; the other seven have a chance of natural profit."* (021 · 00:39:05–00:39:35)

A falsifiable base rate by entry depth: entries in the short-term boundary (0–50) go into drawdown 70% of the time, mid-term (50–75) 50%, long-term (75–100) 30%. Supports T38/T67-style depth preference.

### T232 — CAPITAL 20% SHORT / 30% MID / 50% LONG, EACH SUB-SPLIT INTO NINE

*"If you approach the market in the short-term boundary, you must not risk more than 20% of your total capital. Overall 20% for the short-term boundary."* (021 · 00:41:09)
*"Split the boundary into nine: short of short, short of mid… long of long."* (021 · 00:41:41–00:42:11)
*"20 30 50: overall 20% for all short terms… 30% for mid-term… 50% for long-term."* (021 · 00:43:45–00:44:16)

Allocation across entry depth (not across legs): 20% of capital to the short-term boundary, 30% mid, 50% long; each is distributed across the 5m/15m/1H versions. Relates to T3 (20/30/50 per boundary) — here the 20/30/50 is by depth term; T66 says the per-leg 20/30/50 is superseded by 30/70, so this needs reconciling.

### T233 — TRENDLINE TOUCH PRICE = OVER/UNDER-VALUE LINE

*"Where the trendline hits, put a price line straight across. That price line is over value: if the market goes above it, over value; only if below it is the market under value."* (021 · 00:47:50)
*"The probability of crossing it is low; even if it crosses, selling pressure increases there."* (021 · 00:48:20)

Horizontal line through the latest trendline touch defines over/under value. Complements T14/T38.

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T221** | Control of greed/fear = ≥3 consecutive same-colour candles, each body closing beyond the previous close | **Yes** |
| **T222** | After reaching a 50%/boundary, 1–3 candles (15–20 min on 5m) closing back inside = attacker spent, expect reversal; 3 candles held beyond a base = return unlikely | **Yes** |
| **T223** | ~4 alternating touches of the two 50% lines = compression; fast break follows | **Partly — break direction not specified** |
| **T224** | A drift of successive inside candles = no strength, move about to stop | **Yes** |
| **T225** | Bounce smaller than the previous bounce = minor layer → aggressive continuation; larger = major | **Yes** |
| **T226** | Repeated bounces staying within 0–25% of the range → the low breaks | **Yes** |
| **T227** | A support bounce is only worth trading if ~2x (reference unclear) | **Partly — reference of 2x unclear; heard once** |
| **T228** | A boundary break runs ≥3 consecutive candles of the broken boundary's timeframe; successive breaks escalate 5m→15m→1H | **Yes** |
| **T229** | Boundary = two adjacent price lines; 0–50 short, 50–75 mid, 75–100 long term | **Yes (definition)** |
| **T230** | Candle/pattern analysis uses only the 50% line; draw 75 only in a trend | **Yes (procedure)** |
| **T231** | Entries go negative 7/10 in short-term, 5/10 mid-term, 3/10 long-term boundary | **Yes** |
| **T232** | Capital 20% short / 30% mid / 50% long-term boundary, each split over 3 timeframes | **Partly — sub-split amounts not given; conflicts with T66** |
| **T233** | Horizontal line through the latest trendline touch divides over value (above) and under value (below) | **Partly — anchoring by chart, values shown only** |


## Video 022 — Upgrading the boundary through 15m/1H/4H; forecast horizons of candle vs pattern vs trend

He continues the NIFTY chart, following a break of the 5m base by upgrading to the 15m, 1H and 4H boundaries to find where price may settle. New precise material: a three-candle confirmation wait scaled to the timeframe (3 hours on 1H), explicit forecast horizons for candle/pattern/trend analysis, "micro short term" (0–25) bounces that do not pay brokerage, a 1.5x threshold for a major push, and a percentage split of candle ownership. The second half is sales talk (his seven income streams) — skipped.

### T234 — CONFIRMATION WAIT = 3 CANDLES OF THE TIMEFRAME (1H → 3 HOURS)

*"If the market is settling on the one hour, to see whether this control of greed has ended you must give three hours of the one hour. Three candles means give three hours. Only if you give it can you see whether they are setting traps here. Otherwise the market suddenly goes fast up in the next candle."* (022 · 00:13:43)
*"That is why we say wait three hours at that place. You must wait for confirmation."* (022 · 00:15:46–00:16:16)
*"Whatever frame it is — it is common to all candles — in the next three candles the body must not be put outside this boundary; a wick may."* (022 · 00:32:35–00:33:05)

Generalises T222: the three-candle hold test applies on any timeframe and the wait is measured in that timeframe's candles. A single-candle "breakout" that leaves a long upper wick with the body back inside is the retail trap.

### T235 — FORECAST HORIZON: CANDLE ≈ 2–3 BARS, PATTERN ≈ 1–3 HOURS, TREND ≈ 1–2 DAYS (ON 5m)

*"If you do candle analysis on 5 minutes, then for the next five, ten minutes — two, three times the 5 minute, maximum 10 minutes, maximum 15 minutes — what the market will be."* (022 · 00:26:28)
*"Candle analysis on five minutes means the market is likely to stay in that trend for the next 15 minutes. Pattern analysis: for the next one hour to two hours, maximum three hours."* (022 · 00:28:31)
*"One, two… thirteen — more than one hour, as I said, the next one or two hours the market continued in that trend."* (022 · 00:29:02)
*"Trend analysis: whose control the market is in for the next one or two days."* (022 · 00:29:33)
*"For taking the trade it is completely candle analysis. To see how the market may be over one or two hours, pattern analysis; for the next one or two days, trend analysis."* (022 · 00:37:20)

Quantifies T92: on the 5m, a candle read is valid ~2–3 bars (10–15 min), a pattern read (outside close beyond an A/V head) ~1–3 hours, a trend read ~1–2 days. He adds these are ~50% expectations to be rechecked continuously (00:30:34). Entry timing itself is candle analysis only.

### T236 — MICRO SHORT TERM (0–25) BOUNCES DO NOT PAY BROKERAGE

*"0 to 25 is micro short term, 0 to 50 short term, then 50–75 mid term, 75 to 100 long term. If it cannot even go to 50, they call it micro short term — no use going there. It will print only single layer, double layer; the buyer's involvement there will not be big… even if you take a trade there the profit won't be big — it won't even cover brokerage."* (022 · 00:35:47–00:36:19)

Adds a fourth band below T229: a counter-move confined to 0–25% of the leg is "micro short term" and not tradeable. Consistent with T39/T41 (sub-1% targets are not trades).

### T237 — A MAJOR PUSH MUST BE ≥1.5x THE PREVIOUS ONE, ELSE THE LOW BREAKS

*"First the buyer took it this far. Second, one to one-and-a-half times that. Third, the same size… Here it must go at least one-and-a-half times this, more than this. Only if it goes more is the buyer's contribution major. If it cannot go more and the market turns, it will break this aggressively and go down hard."* (022 · 00:38:51–00:39:22)

Puts a number on T225: a buyer push counts as major only if ≥1.5× the largest earlier push; a failed push → aggressive breakdown. Matches the 1.5–2× alert in T106.

### T238 — CANDLE OWNERSHIP AS A PERCENTAGE (UPPER WICK + RED BODY = SELLER)

*"The upper wick belongs to the seller; the body below, being red, also belongs to the seller. So overall this is 90% in the seller's control. Only that little wick below belongs to the buyer — only 10%."* (022 · 00:08:42)
*"Current candle analysis: almost 100% — as per this candle it is in the buyer's control. If for the next three candles the market does not come down, the chance of breaking this base is low."* (022 · 00:37:50–00:38:21)

Extends T100: sum the lengths each side owns (lower wick + green body = buyer; upper wick + red body = seller) to get a control percentage; a ~90% candle predicts the next 2–3 bars (T235) and a move toward the 50% of the prior range.

### T239 — UPGRADE LADDER 5m → 15m → 1H → 4H ("THREE TIMES")

*"Upgrade: which is three times the one hour? Four hour. The 4-hour's visible high, low…"* (022 · 00:09:12)
*"The market has come into the long term of the one hour and the mid term of the four hour. As it goes up, the seller's presence increases — price of satisfaction."* (022 · 00:09:42–00:10:13)

When a lower frame's base breaks, redraw the boundary on the next frame (5m→15m→1H→4H) to find the next settling zone; deeper into a higher frame's long-term band = more profit-taking against the move. Extends T23.

Corroborates T38/T68: "don't buy as it goes up; if you hold something, sell it in over value; buy in under value" (022 · 00:17:49).

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T234** | Confirmation = 3 candles of the same timeframe (1H → 3 h) with bodies not closing outside the boundary; wick-only probes are traps | **Yes** |
| **T235** | On 5m: candle read valid ~10–15 min, pattern read ~1–3 h, trend read ~1–2 days | **Yes** |
| **T236** | Counter-moves confined to 0–25% (micro short term) are not tradeable — profit below brokerage | **Yes** |
| **T237** | A push is major only if ≥1.5x the largest earlier push; failure → aggressive break of the low | **Yes** |
| **T238** | Candle control % = (owned wick + owned body) / range; ~90% one side predicts the next 2–3 bars | **Partly — "90%" was eyeballed, no threshold stated** |
| **T239** | On a base break, redraw on the next frame up: 5m→15m→1H→4H | **Yes (procedure)** |

## Video 023 — Gold 1H walk-down to 15m/5m/1m, then NIFTY: gaps, frames, violent hours and a 15-minute trade clock

He continues the previous day's chart on the 1-hour, reads candle ownership and the three-hour hold, then down-grades to 15m, 5m and 1m to show the same boundaries. The second half switches to NIFTY: how to treat gaps, which frames to use for a 6–7 hour session, when the session is "violent", and an explicit time limit for an intraday target. Ends with an analogy for equity/futures/options (skipped as colour, the claim is noted).

### T240 — FORECAST HORIZON SCALES WITH THE FRAME: 1H CANDLE ≈ 2–3 h, 1H PATTERN ≈ 1–2 DAYS, 1H TREND ≈ 1–2 WEEKS

*"If you take one candle and do candle analysis [on 5 minutes], you can see how the market will be for the next 10 to 15 minutes — three times. If you do it on one hour, the next 2 to 3 hours… If you do pattern analysis for the same one hour, how the market will be for the next one or two days. If you do trend analysis for it, the next one week to two weeks."* (023 · 00:31:42–00:32:12)

Extends T235/T92 into a scaling rule: a candle read lasts ~2–3 candles; a pattern read lasts roughly one frame up (5m pattern ≈ 1–3 h; 1H pattern ≈ 1–2 days); a trend read one frame further (1H trend ≈ 1–2 weeks). The 5m-trend figure given in 022 (1–2 days) and this 1H-pattern figure coincide.

### T241 — CONTROL OF GREED ESCALATES 5m → 15m → 1H (RISING SIDE)

*"First, for 5 minutes, control of greed will go — consistently three green… Next, on 15 minutes three green candles will go up. That's how the market's speed is. After it settles somewhat, it comes down a bit, refuels, and goes fast again breaking that head; then on one hour itself three green candles will come. Look — has it come? Three green candles on one hour."* (023 · 00:10:17–00:10:48)

Mirror of T228 on the upside: successive legs of a run print ≥3 same-colour candles on 5m, then 15m, then 1H, each after a settle-and-refuel pause. "After the one-hour [run], the market has a chance to settle" (00:10:48).

### T242 — 50% SPLITS OVER/UNDER VALUE; 25 IS A MINOR (SUB) BOUNDARY, 50 THE MAJOR

*"25 is a sub-boundary, a minor boundary; 50 is the major boundary. If you split the market in two, 0 to 50 to 100… above 50 is over value, below 50 is under value."* (023 · 00:24:01)
*"Always the maximum decision happens on the 50% line."* (023 · 00:23:01)

Corroborates T38 and T230; names 25 as "minor".

### T243 — NIFTY: CONNECT THE GAPS; ANALYSE ON 1m, 5m, 15m (1H IF NEEDED)

*"Because NIFTY's floating hours are few — not 24 hours — you must take 1 minute, 5 minute, 15 minute, and if needed one hour. Only if you analyse these four frames can you identify NIFTY's boundaries."* (023 · 00:41:21)
*"Bulk deal, block deal… pre-open… that's the gap. Connect all this. If there's a gap up or gap down, just draw across it… don't let the gap confuse you."* (023 · 00:43:22, 00:47:55–00:48:25)
*"As far as I know NIFTY moves only within the 1 minute, 5 minute, 15 minute boundary each day — micro short term, short term, mid term. Only occasionally does it go to the one-hour size."* (023 · 01:00:03)

Procedure for a session-limited index: treat overnight gaps as continuous price (draw across them), and expect a normal day's range to fit within a 1m–15m boundary. Relates to T92/T93.

### T244 — NIFTY VIOLENT HOURS: FIRST HOUR, ~12:00–13:30 SOMETIMES, LAST ~2 HOURS

*"The first one hour the market will be violent in NIFTY — from 9:15 to 10:15; after that the market lies down. Then ~12 to 1:30 it will be somewhat violent, medium… Then 2 to 3:30 — here it's terrible; say 1:30 to 3:30. First one hour and last two hours are violent… 12 to 1:30 only sometimes. So of the six hours, the market is actually violent only three hours."* (023 · 01:06:39–01:07:12)

Refines T93 (09:15–10:00 and 14:45–15:30): he now gives 09:15–10:15 and ~13:30/14:00–15:30, with an occasional 12:00–13:30 medium window.

### T245 — INTRADAY NIFTY: TARGET MUST HIT WITHIN 5–15 MINUTES OR THE TRADE IS A LOSS

*"In these three hours, if you take a trade, within the next five to ten minutes, maximum 15 minutes, your target must go. If it doesn't, that trade is in loss. You must do fund management accordingly and choose the first place accordingly."* (023 · 01:07:12)
*"If you trade intraday NIFTY on 15 [minutes], you'll just take the position and there's no more time; the market expires. You can only do it on 1 minute — 1 minute, 5 minute is your [frame]."* (023 · 01:05:38)

A time stop: an intraday NIFTY entry that has not reached target in 15 minutes is treated as a losing trade. Consistent with T235 (a 5m candle read lasts 10–15 min).

### T246 — TAKE A 5m SETUP'S ENTRY ON THE 1m; ON 1m GIVE 3 MINUTES BEFORE BELIEVING A BREAK

*"If you must take a trade on this five minute, break it down and look on one minute… the moment it reaches this place, put a vertical line and go straight to one minute."* (023 · 00:56:30–00:57:01)
*"Give three candles' time… one minute means give the next three minutes. In the next three minutes the next candle's body must not go above this 50%."* (023 · 00:51:26)
*"When doing one minute, don't put 25/75; for candle and pattern use only the 50%."* (023 · 00:49:25–00:49:56)

Execution refinement: the setup is read on 5m, the fill is timed on 1m, with the same 3-candle confirmation (T234) scaled to 3 minutes.

### T247 — PREFER 24-HOUR INSTRUMENTS: MORE "FLOATING" HOURS = MORE TRADES PER DAY

*"The Indian market floats only six to seven hours a day. In gold it's 24 hours; in Bitcoin 24 hours, 365 days… where it does one [trade] a day here, you can do three a day there."* (023 · 00:36:15–00:36:47)

A venue-choice claim (with tax as a second reason). Complements T40.

He also restates "buying a fast fall because a boundary arrived is catching a falling knife with your tongue" (023 · 01:09:12) — corroborates T68 (wait for buyer involvement).

| **T241** | A rising run escalates ≥3 green on 5m, then 15m, then 1H, with settle pauses between | **Yes** |
| **T242** | Above 50% of range = over value, below = under value; 25 is a minor sub-boundary | **Yes (definition)** |
| **T243** | NIFTY: draw across gaps; use 1m/5m/15m (1H if needed); a normal day stays within a 15m boundary | **Partly — "normal day" range claim testable, frame choice is procedure** |
| **T244** | NIFTY violent windows: 09:15–10:15, ~13:30/14:00–15:30, sometimes 12:00–13:30 | **Yes** |
| **T245** | Intraday NIFTY: if target not hit within 15 min (ideally 5–10), treat as a loss | **Yes** |
| **T246** | Read the setup on 5m, time the fill on 1m; on 1m wait 3 bars before trusting a break | **Yes** |
| **T247** | Prefer 24h instruments (gold, BTC) over a 6–7h session — ~3x the trade opportunities | **Partly — the 3x is a rough claim** |

## Video 024 — Gold 1H review, the trendline's own boundary, and a capital-scaling ladder

Mostly a re-read of the gold 1H chart with the same vocabulary (90% candle control, 3-hour hold, O pattern compressing before a blast) and a brief NIFTY 1H→15m→5m pass. New material: how to draw a trendline and derive a "trendline boundary" from the distances of successive heads, and a staged way to add capital only after the account doubles. A long section on the 10/10/20/60 "marks" (technical/fundamental/trading system/psychology+fund management) is framing, not method.

### T248 — TRENDLINE BOUNDARY = DISTANCES OF EACH NEW HEAD FROM THE TRENDLINE; PRICE RETURNS TO THEM

*"Place it straight from a high, drag it from there; where it hits more than one candle, stop there."* (024 · 00:14:41)
*"Minimum wherever two candles hit, stop there."* (024 · 00:18:47)
*"Wherever it went far from the trendline, at each of those places draw a line straight to the trendline… First it came this far from the trendline, second this far, third this far. Beyond this it could not go far from the trendline… To all those boundaries where it went far from the trendline, the market will come back."* (024 · 00:16:14–00:17:47)
*"If the trendline breaks and the market goes up, if you hold something, sell it in that boundary."* (024 · 00:18:17)

Procedure: anchor on a high, rotate until ≥2 candles touch (refines T14); for each pattern head that breaks the previous one, mark its perpendicular distance from the line; the band of those distances is the trendline's boundary and price is expected to revisit it (corroborates T73). A move beyond the line into the boundary = over value → sell holdings there.

### T249 — SCALE CAPITAL IN ONLY AFTER THE ACCOUNT DOUBLES

*"Start with an amount whose loss won't hurt — even 10,000, even 1,000… Let ₹10,000 become 20,000. Then bring another ₹20,000 of your own money; now 40,000 — let it become 80. Now bring one lakh; it becomes 2 lakh. Put money into the market step by step like this."* (024 · 00:43:46–00:44:46)

A capital-deployment rule: new own-money is added only after the current stake has doubled, roughly matching the stake each time. Complements T46 (own capital only).

Corroborates T240: "a one-hour candle analysis tells the next two hours — maximum three times" (024 · 00:10:37) and T104/T223: "if the market jams within one boundary more than once, up–down, without breaking either side, it will blast one way" (024 · 00:04:36).

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T248** | Trendline from a high touching ≥2 candles; the distances of successive new heads from it form a boundary price revisits; above it = sell holdings | **Partly — "revisits" has no time limit** |
| **T249** | Add own capital only after the current stake doubles (10k→20k, +20k→80k, +1L→2L) | **Yes (procedure)** |

## Video 025 — Gold 1H tug-of-war read and a short NIFTY 15m/5m/1m outline

A shorter session (he says he had ~30 minutes). He reads the gold 1H chart as a "tug of war": body beyond the other side's last body = confidence shift, wick only = reaction. Then NIFTY 15m → 5m → 1m boundaries for the next session. Almost all material restates existing rules; the new items are the weighting of past vs current analysis, the one-candle-gives-half-the-confirmation claim, and a performance benchmark (capital should grow every third trade).

### T250 — ONE CANDLE GIVES ~50% CONFIRMATION; THE NEXT TWO GIVE THE REST

*"Should all this be decided on one candle? From one candle we get a 50% confirmation. The remaining two candles — on 15 minutes, within half an hour of today's open — you'll know."* (025 · 00:26:18–00:26:49)
*"I must see another two candles like this here; only then can I find whether the market has settled, whether it's a trap."* (025 · 00:24:48)

Refines T234: the first candle's close is half the evidence; candles 2–3 complete it.

### T251 — WEIGHT CURRENT PRICE ACTION ~70%, THE COMPLETED MARKET ~30%

*"The finished market works about 30%; the current market works about 70%."* (025 · 00:12:39)
*"The finished market is useful — about 30% useful. Current market analysis is very, very important."* (021 · 00:21:18)

A weighting of evidence: the prior structure frames expectations, but live candle behaviour at the level dominates. Consistent with T30.

### T252 — BENCHMARK: THE ACCOUNT SHOULD GROW EVERY THIRD TRADE (~+5% PER 3 TRADES AT THE START)

*"Every third trade your capital will keep growing. If you start with 10,000, when three trades finish — that doesn't mean two are losses, I'm saying on average — it will have become 10,500. The next three trades, 11,000. The next three, 11,200 at least."* (025 · 00:34:26–00:34:57)
*"As I said, every third trade you must keep seeing profit in the market — your overall profit must keep growing."* (025 · 00:38:32–00:39:02)

A falsifiable performance claim for the full method (including fund management), usable as a backtest acceptance criterion: equity higher after each block of three trades.

Corroborates T100: "the body must be considered, because the body creates confidence; the wick only means the market reacted there" (025 · 00:14:11); T225/T91: seller pushes shrinking while buyer pushes grow = buyer taking control (025 · 00:17:14–00:17:44); T223: "two or three 50% lines very close together = the market is shrinking, it will blast one way" (025 · 00:20:48); and T38: NIFTY "above four boundaries is over value — only try sells, by no means buys" (025 · 00:32:22).

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T250** | The first candle after a level gives ~half the confirmation; candles 2–3 complete it | **Partly — "50%" is a figure of speech** |
| **T251** | Weight current price action ~70%, prior structure ~30% | **No — a judgement weighting, not a signal** |
| **T252** | Equity should be higher after every block of 3 trades (≈+5% per block at the start) | **Yes (acceptance benchmark)** |

## Video 026 — Pattern analysis in depth (Bitcoin): equal/extra/failed V, inside/outside pattern, "failed outside" pattern, and net pattern control

On a Bitcoin chart he formalises pattern analysis the way candle analysis was done earlier: the three V/A types, inside vs outside pattern (T105), and a new compound — the "failed outside pattern". He also scores a pattern's control as simple arithmetic (seller leg 100, buyer retrace X → net seller control 100 − X), and tracks how that number changes pattern to pattern. The last third is sales talk.

### T253 — V/A TYPE BY HOW FAR THE COUNTER-LEG REACHES THE PRIOR BASE (EQUAL / EXTRA / FAILED)

*"Where the seller brought it from, if the buyer takes it back to that same place — equal V pattern. If the buyer takes it beyond that — extra V pattern. If the buyer cannot take it to 25, 50, 75, 100 of that base, takes it to 25 or 50 and then breaks this head and comes down — failed V pattern."* (026 · 00:10:14–00:11:16)
*"V pattern: seller brings it down, buyer takes it back up. If it reaches the base, equal; beyond the base, extra; can't reach the base — goes somewhere in 25/50/75/100 and turns — failed. Only three."* (026 · 00:11:16–00:11:46; 00:19:59)

Definitions tied to the 0/25/50/75/100 grid of the prior leg: counter-leg reaches 100 = equal, >100 = extra, <100 and then the pattern's own low (V) / high (A) broken by an outside candle = failed. Formalises the Equal/Failed/Extra names in T22.

### T254 — NET PATTERN CONTROL = COUNTER-LEG% − 100 (TRACK IT PATTERN TO PATTERN)

*"Think of it like this: what the seller brought from here is 100; what the buyer took back is half of it, 50. So 100 − 50… −50. Overall whose control? Failed V pattern means the market is in the seller's control."* (026 · 00:14:19–00:14:54)
*"The pattern is now about 95% in the seller's control, 5% in the buyer's. So overall −90%, the seller's control."* (026 · 00:16:26)
*"Again the buyer took it only 25%; the seller is at about 100%. So −75% seller control. It was 90 here, now it has reduced to 75 — still seller's control, but the seller went from 90 to 75. You must read the market like that."* (026 · 00:16:26–00:16:56)

A numeric pattern score: net control = retracement% of the prior leg − 100 (negative = seller). A rising score across successive patterns (−90 → −75 → …) while still negative is the early sign of a shift. Extends T91 (shrinking pushes) and T106.

### T255 — "FAILED OUTSIDE" PATTERN = FAILED, THEN THE RETEST CANNOT EVEN REACH THE BROKEN LOW → ~100% ONE-SIDED

*"Failed pattern means it couldn't go to the high above. Failed outside pattern means it couldn't even go to the [broken] low below. Then the market is 100% in the seller's control."* (026 · 00:32:12)
*"It came out failed, then tries again — now it can't even take it to this low. Then it's an outside pattern… failed outside pattern: failed as well as closed outside."* (026 · 00:31:41; 00:33:13)
*"Inside the old high–low, if the buyer keeps trying again and again, those are inside patterns… If they cannot take it back inside this high and low — that is an outside pattern."* (026 · 00:22:34–00:23:04; 00:30:10)

Sequence: a V fails (breaks its low without reaching the prior high); the next bounce stays entirely below the broken low (cannot re-enter the prior pattern's range) → strongest continuation signal. Refines T105 (inside/outside pattern) and T99.

### T256 — READ THE LAST TWO PATTERNS THE WAY YOU READ THE LAST TWO CANDLES

*"Just as, however many candles there are, you take the last two candles and look, take the last two patterns and look."* (026 · 00:20:30)
*"The pattern itself: 25% bounce = the market is shrinking, it will blast; about 50% = an equal fight."* (026 · 00:15:24)

Procedure plus a restatement of the 25/50 thresholds at pattern level (T97, T226).

He also defines a "failed candle" by analogy — "the next candle forms, can't reach this high, turns and breaks this low; its body won't even be at the 50%, it'll be outside" (026 · 00:33:45) — but the distinction from an outside candle is *(unclear)* in the caption.

Corroborates T231 ("7 out of 10 trades go negative"; 026 · 00:35:47) and T11/T45 ("we don't use stop loss in our trades; if a trade goes negative we tighten it technically, fundamentally, then by fund management into overall profit"; 026 · 00:45:58–00:46:29).

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T253** | Counter-leg reaches 100% of prior leg = equal; >100% = extra; <100% then own extreme broken = failed | **Yes** |
| **T254** | Net pattern control = retrace% − 100; a rising score across patterns (still negative) signals the other side gaining | **Yes** |
| **T255** | Failed pattern + retest unable to reach the broken extreme ("failed outside") = ~100% one-sided, strongest continuation | **Yes** |
| **T256** | Compare the last two patterns like the last two candles; 25% pattern bounce = compression, ~50% = equal fight | **Partly — "equal fight" has no stated outcome** |

## Video 027 — Bitcoin/gold/NIFTY boundary stacking, and a profit cap for "money investment" trades

A multi-instrument walk (Bitcoin 1H→15m→5m→1m, gold 1H, NIFTY) that mostly restates T234/T239/T253. New material: the observation that 1m/5m/15m/1H boundaries stack like layers, that 25/75 "minor" lines react only at trend level, and — most important — a profit cap tied to the risk amount when a trade is a "money investment" (taken in over value) rather than a "time investment" (under value). The second half is a warning story and sales talk.

### T257 — MONEY-INVESTMENT TRADES: PROFIT CAPPED AT 1–10% OF THE RISK AMOUNT ("CUT TWO ZEROS")

*"If you risk ₹10,000 in a trade, cut the two zeros: your profit should be at most from ₹100 up to 999 — when it is money investment. Money investment is mostly in over value. If you do money investment and take 2,000, 3,000 profit, your account will become zero one day — write it down."* (027 · 00:20:24–00:20:54)
*"With the same 10,000 you can take 2,000, 3,000 too — then you break that rule — because that is [approached as] time investment… If you go into money investment and take the return that time investment gives, your account will certainly become zero, however much you grow it."* (027 · 00:21:24–00:21:54)
*"Where to take that ₹2,000–3,000 on the same ₹10,000 risk? Approach the market in under value — [time] investment."* (027 · 00:20:54)

Target sizing depends on where the entry sits: in over value ("money investment", T232/021 definitions), take ≤ risk ÷ 100 up to risk ÷ 10 (₹100–999 on ₹10,000); only an under-value ("time investment") entry may aim for 20–30% of the risk. Refines T39/T41 (minimum target) with a maximum, and links to T38 (under value).

### T258 — BOUNDARIES STACK BY TIMEFRAME: 1m = MICRO SHORT, 5m = SHORT, 15m = MID, 1H = LONG

*"Here's the one minute — in this area; 5 minute here, 15 here, one hour here. Layer upon layer… this is micro short term, this short-term boundary, this mid-term boundary, below it the long-term boundary. That is how the market moves step by step, inch by inch."* (027 · 00:15:50–00:16:20)

Maps the depth bands (T229/T236) onto frames: a fall that breaks the 1m base heads to the 5m 50%, then 15m, then 1H. Consistent with T228/T239.

### T259 — 25/75 (MINOR) LINES REACT ONLY AT TREND LEVEL, NOT AT CANDLE/PATTERN LEVEL

*"Always don't mark the minor boundary, mark only the major… 25, 75 are minor boundaries; the market reacts there if it's at a big trend level. At candle level and pattern level it won't react."* (027 · 00:16:50–00:17:20)

Tightens T230: don't expect reactions at 25/75 except on trend-scale legs.

Corroborates T234 ("an outside candle — give it three candles' time"; 027 · 00:06:09), T252 ("every third trade your total — brought capital plus profit — must be higher than the last check; if not, you lack knowledge"; 027 · 00:24:27–00:24:58), and T73/T76 via "control of fear — you'll think it fell so much, won't it rise as much? It won't" (027 · 00:45:20).

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T257** | Over-value ("money investment") trades take profit of risk/100 to risk/10 (₹100–999 on ₹10k); only under-value ("time investment") entries may target 20–30% of risk | **Yes** |
| **T258** | 1m/5m/15m/1H boundaries stack as micro-short/short/mid/long; a break of one frame's base targets the next frame's 50% | **Yes** |
| **T259** | 25/75 lines react only on trend-scale legs, not at candle/pattern level | **Yes** |

## Video 028 — Bitcoin 4H/1H top, gold and NIFTY frames; NIFTY's 80/20 intraday split; base rates for random vs technical entries; volume needed at deeper boundaries

He confirms the previous day's Bitcoin call (a 1H outside candle that failed to sustain for 3 candles, then ~10 hours of down-trend into the 4H body–wick zone) and walks gold and NIFTY down the frames. New precise content: the time-scaled confirmation restated, NIFTY session statistics, win-rate base rates, a rule that deeper boundaries need more volume before a reversal, and when boundary analysis stops being usable.

### T260 — NIFTY: FIRST HOUR + 13:30–15:30 CARRY ~80% OF THE DAY'S MOVE; 10:30–13:30 COMPRESSES

*"In it, the first one hour is very violent; the last two hours, 1:30 to 3:30, very violent. Within that, 80% of the market's movement will be — on most days. The rest of the time the market is only 20%… Mostly from 10:30 to 1:30 the market will shrink. On expiry days, after 12 there is big volume… FII presence after about 12… 11:30–12:30, European session time, big volatility in the Indian market."* (028 · 00:21:48–00:22:49)

Quantifies T244/T93: ~80% of NIFTY's daily range in 09:15–10:15 and 13:30–15:30; 10:30–13:30 is the compression window, except expiry days and the ~11:30–12:30 European-open overlap.

### T261 — BASE RATES: RANDOM ENTRIES WIN ~3/10, TECHNICAL READING ~5/10; THE REST MUST BE FIXED BY FUNDAMENTAL (VOLUME) THEN FUND MANAGEMENT

*"If you analyse technically, about five out of ten trades will be profit. If you approach the market flat, three out of ten trades come in profit naturally. You needn't do it — call a small child at home, ask 'buy or sell?' — overall result: three out of ten profit."* (028 · 00:50:46–00:51:17)
*"With fundamental analysis you can make one or two more trades [profit]… does it still lose? It does. Then what corrects it? … your knowledge — fund management."* (028 · 00:53:18)

A falsifiable claim for backtests: unconditional entries ≈30% win rate, his technical reading ≈50%, volume ("fundamental") adds 1–2 more per 10, and the remainder is managed by the averaging/fund-management mechanism (T11). Consistent with T231 (short-term boundary 7/10 go negative).

### T262 — A DEEPER BOUNDARY NEEDS PROPORTIONALLY MORE VOLUME BEFORE IT CAN REVERSE; WITHOUT IT, EXPECT A FAILED V AND THE NEXT BASE

*"A car in a 2-foot pit, two of us can push. The same car in a 20-foot pit — can two push it? No. At least 20 people must come… Taking an entry because the market reached this boundary is the two people pushing the car."* (028 · 00:47:44–00:48:45)
*"If price has come from this boundary to this [deeper] one, the volume here must be totally different… otherwise it goes as if going up, 25 or 50, and turns into a failed V pattern… goes further down."* (028 · 00:49:46–00:50:16)
*"Price came to the boundary — the volume for that place must come too."* (028 · 00:52:18)

The further price has travelled (larger boundary), the more accumulation volume is required at the level before a bounce is real; a bounce without it reaches only 25–50 and fails (T253). Operationalises T68/T94 (buyer involvement, takeover needs volume).

### T263 — CONTROL OF FEAR CAN CASCADE ACROSS FRAMES (1H → 4H → DAY); SETTLING IS ONLY CONSIDERED AFTER ≥3 SAME-COLOUR CANDLES

*"When it turns control of fear it prints three red candles in a row; only after that does it think about settling. That doesn't mean after three candles it must come back. If one hour's control of fear finishes, four hour's control of fear can continue. Some days, starting at five minutes, control of fear has gone all the way to the day."* (028 · 00:28:26)
*"When control of fear finishes, it settles at price of attraction."* (028 · 00:38:34)

Extends T228/T241: the ≥3-candle run is a minimum, not a reversal signal; the next frame's run may follow immediately.

### T264 — NO 50% LINE NEARBY (ONLY DISTANT BASES) → BOUNDARY ANALYSIS DOESN'T WORK; USE CANDLE/TREND ONLY

*"Above this there's no 50% — only the base… If the base itself is very high, you must do candle analysis, trend analysis — no other way. Boundary analysis can't be done any more; even if done, it won't work."* (028 · 00:41:07–00:41:39)

A validity condition for the boundary method: when price is outside all drawn ranges (e.g. above the 1-day high), stop projecting levels.

Corroborates T234 ("candle analysis needs at least three times the time: 5 minute → 15 minutes, one hour → three hours"; 028 · 00:09:11), T222 ("if the market sustains three hours, the lower body is hard to break"; 028 · 00:09:41), T258 ("for a trader the long-term boundary is one hour, mid-term 15 minutes, short-term 5 minutes"; 028 · 00:20:17), and T73 ("the market goes 100% to where it must go — sometimes slowly"; 028 · 00:37:33).

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T260** | NIFTY: ~80% of the daily range occurs 09:15–10:15 and 13:30–15:30; 10:30–13:30 compresses (except expiry/11:30–12:30) | **Yes** |
| **T261** | Random entries win ~30%, his technical reading ~50%, adding volume +10–20 points; the rest is fixed by fund management | **Yes** |
| **T262** | A reversal at a deeper boundary requires more accumulation volume; without it the bounce reaches 25–50% and fails | **Partly — volume threshold not quantified** |
| **T263** | ≥3-candle fear/greed run is a minimum; the next frame's run (1H→4H→D) may follow without settling | **Yes** |
| **T264** | When no 50% line lies between price and distant bases, boundary projection is invalid — use candle/trend only | **Partly — "distant" not defined** |

## Video 029 — Pattern analysis continued: wick-only probes below a V head, "failed low" marking, converging 50% lines and the 70% range claim

He extends pattern analysis on Bitcoin (inside vs outside vs failed, wicks below a V head with bodies closing back inside), defines the "failed candle" by body percentage, gives a procedure for marking failed lows, shows compression via converging 50% lines, and restates that ~70–80% of the time the market is compressing. Gold and NIFTY 1H/15m/5m/1m walk-throughs repeat earlier rules.

### T265 — REPEATED SELL PUSHES THAT ONLY WICK BELOW THE V HEAD, BODIES CLOSING BACK INSIDE = BUYER ACCUMULATION; DOWNSIDE LIMITED

*"Beyond this low only the wick has come outside; the body closed inside. Again… the wick comes outside beyond the low and again it's come this far… that means the next pattern keeps forming as an inside pattern within that V pattern's head."* (029 · 00:00:30–00:01:01)
*"In this pattern the buyer's force is equal; the buyer is tightening the market hard — the buyer's volume is developing, accumulating heavily… If the buyer's movement stays inside, inside, inside that low, the chance of the market going far down is low."* (029 · 00:01:32–00:02:03)

Operational form of T100/T72 at pattern scale: several successive probes below the prior V low that close their bodies back above it → accumulation; expect the V head to hold and a move toward the 50%.

### T266 — "FAILED CANDLE" = LESS THAN HALF THE BODY BEYOND THE LINE

*"It put a body beyond the 50% — but that is also a failed candle. Failed candle means 50% of the body must be above this price line; here only about 20% of the whole body is above, 80% is below. So — failed candle."* (029 · 00:20:29–00:21:00)

Names the complement of T20: a close that pokes past a level with <50% of its body beyond it is a "failed candle" and does not count as a break.

### T267 — MARK THE LOW BEFORE EACH GREEN CANDLE; A LOW THAT HOLDS = NO "FAILED LOW" = BUYER IN CONTROL

*"Put each low — each low before a green candle. Failed low. Next, the green is here, put one for this; it broke this and came down — failed low. Next put one here; now it couldn't break this and come down — that means it hasn't become a failed low; the buyer is in control. Then quickly do candle analysis."* (029 · 00:38:50)
*"For the first time the buyer has reached the distance the seller brought. First time couldn't, second time couldn't… third time touched with a wick… then the seller couldn't make a failed low — so the buyer becomes strong again; the market can go to its 50% level."* (029 · 00:04:08–00:05:08)

Procedure: walk the down-leg marking the low preceding each green candle; the first such low that is not broken ends the sequence of failed lows and flips control. Combines with T237 (buyer push reaching 100% of the seller's leg).

### T268 — CONVERGING 50% LINES (BUYER-LEG 50% AND SELLER-LEG 50% CLOSE TOGETHER) = COMPRESSION; BREAK GOES FAST TO THAT SIDE'S BASE

*"There's a 50% for what the buyer took, a 50% line for what the seller took… they are near each other — that means they are starting to shrink… You can find the market shrinking with these 50% lines."* (029 · 00:25:36–00:26:08)
*"If it breaks below and prints an outside candle, the market comes here fast; above with an outside candle, fast to this place… A shrinking market is always dangerous — stay away from the crowd."* (029 · 00:26:08)

A measurable compression test: distance between the latest buyer-leg 50% and seller-leg 50% shrinking. Refines T223/T104.

### T269 — ~70–80% OF THE TIME THE MARKET IS COMPRESSING (RANGE); THAT IS WHERE 7/10 LOSING TRADES COME FROM; THE EXIT FROM COMPRESSION IS ONE BIG CANDLE

*"70 to 80% of the time the market is in range — what you call range, I call shrinking."* (029 · 00:27:40)
*"Every time it shrinks and goes out, look — it comes with one big candle… Why are seven out of ten of your trades losses? Because you approach the market where it's shrinking like this."* (029 · 00:28:10)
*"The market going silent means it's going to become violent."* (029 · 00:29:10)

Corroborates T49 (~70% ranging) with a causal claim tying it to T231's 7/10 short-term loss rate, and adds that the breakout bar is a single large candle.

Corroborates T236 ("a buyer bounce within 25 is micro short term; 50 at least short term"; 029 · 00:02:33) and T260 ("NIFTY first hour and last two hours violent; in between it lies flat — head and tail both unbroken… profit in the silent hours won't even cover brokerage"; 029 · 00:43:24–00:44:26, 00:49:01–00:49:31).

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T265** | Successive probes below a V low that close their bodies back inside = accumulation; low holds, target the 50% | **Yes** |
| **T266** | A close past a level with <50% of the body beyond it is a "failed candle" (not a break) | **Yes** |
| **T267** | Mark the low before each green candle; the first one not broken ends the failed-low chain and flips control to buyers | **Yes** |
| **T268** | Buyer-leg 50% and seller-leg 50% converging = compression; break with an outside candle runs fast to that side's base | **Partly — "close" not quantified** |
| **T269** | ~70–80% of bars are compression; the breakout comes as one large candle | **Partly — "large" not quantified** |

## Video 030 — Refuelling pullbacks, trendline "super value", a step-by-step loss-rate reduction, and ≤10% of capital in trading

On Bitcoin 1H/4H and gold/1m he re-reads settling (wicks only into the zone, repeatedly), converging 50% lines, and draws the 4H trendline with vertical "head" lines. New items: every leg must pull back ≥25% to "refuel"; the band below the trendline boundary is "super value" where accumulation starts; a quantified ladder of how under-value entry, volume and fund management cut the losing-trade count; and a capital rule that contradicts T6.

### T270 — EVERY LEG PULLS BACK AT LEAST ~25% ("REFUELS") BEFORE THE NEXT HIGH; IN A HEALTHY TREND PULLBACKS STAY IN THE 0–25 BAND

*"If the market must make a high, it comes back, fills fuel, and only then goes to the next high. It comes back at least about 25% and then refuels and goes up again… Coming down from above is the same — it goes up, refuels, then comes down. 25, 50… again 25… only like that can the market come down. This is the market's cycle."* (030 · 00:14:46–00:15:47)
*"The market that went — where does it come back into? Into the short-term boundary… Everywhere the market keeps coming back again and again into that 25."* (030 · 00:30:36–00:31:38)
*"However many days or months later it comes back, it must not put an outside candle beyond this body–wick."* (030 · 00:31:38)

Falsifiable structure claim: continuation legs are separated by counter-moves of ≥~25% of the prior leg, and in a strong trend those counter-moves stay near 25% and never close a body beyond the prior leg's body–wick zone. Complements T97/T226 (a 25% bounce leaves the other side in control).

### T271 — BELOW THE TRENDLINE BOUNDARY IS "SUPER VALUE" — WHERE ACCUMULATION STARTS; DON'T BUY THE MOMENT THE LINE BREAKS

*"Must you buy right at the place where the trendline breaks? No — that's why we put these lines… Where the lines at each head reach the trendline — when [price] comes into that line area, under value fully starts. Below this is super value; from here accumulation will start."* (030 · 00:12:45–00:13:16)
*"Start the trendline as a horizontal price line, lift it slowly; where it hits the bodies of more than one candle, connect it."* (030 · 00:10:43)

Refines T248/T233: crossing the trendline only starts under value; the buy zone is the band marked by the head-distance lines, and below it is "super value".

### T272 — LOSING-TRADE COUNT BY STEP: SHORT-TERM TECHNICAL 7/10 → UNDER VALUE 6 → +VOLUME 5 → +FUND-MANAGEMENT MECHANISM 3; MID-TERM UNDER-VALUE START 5 → 3–4 → 1

*"Approaching short term, seven out of ten go negative. To tighten it, we approach it in under value — then about six have a chance of going negative. Next, volume analysis — fundamentally tighten, confirm the volume — about five. Then fund management — you can't tighten it, you can do mechanism — that can reduce it to about three."* (030 · 00:39:47–00:40:47)
*"If at the start we take mid-term, in under value, about five trades have a chance to go to loss; tighten technically — four or three; with fund management — one."* (030 · 00:41:17)

A quantified hypothesis for what each filter contributes (entries per 10 that go into drawdown / loss). Directly testable layer by layer; consistent with T231 and T261.

### T273 — NEVER PUT MORE THAN ~10% OF TOTAL CAPITAL INTO TRADING; ~80–90% STAYS IN INVESTMENT

*"Even today our capital is only 10%, 20% in trading; the remaining 80% we keep in investment. Ask anyone called a big trader — 90%, 80% of their money is in investment — assets, gold, stocks, bonds."* (030 · 01:01:13–01:01:44)
*"Always, don't put more than 10% of your total capital into trading. Trading is 100% risk — in a fraction of a second that account can become zero."* (030 · 01:02:15)

**Contradicts T6** (cap trading capital at 50%): here the trading allocation is 10% (at most 10–20%), with the rest in investment assets. Also restates the risk-money rule (start only with money you can lose; 030 · 00:36:45, 00:38:47) — corroborates T249.

He also says support/resistance "works 3 times out of 10 — to trap you" (030 · 00:26:33) — consistent with T261's 3/10 random base rate.

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T270** | Continuation legs are separated by ≥~25% pullbacks; in strong trends pullbacks stay ≈0–25% and never close beyond the prior body–wick zone | **Yes** |
| **T271** | Crossing the trendline only begins under value; buy zone = band of head-distance lines; below it "super value" | **Partly — band construction is visual** |
| **T272** | Losing trades per 10: short-term technical 7 → under value 6 → +volume 5 → +mechanism 3; mid-term under value 5 → 3–4 → 1 | **Yes (layer by layer)** |
| **T273** | Trading capital ≤10% (max 10–20%) of total; the rest in investment assets — contradicts T6's 50% | **Yes (allocation rule)** |

## Video 031 — Greed/fear endpoints, drilling a higher-timeframe test down to lower frames, and the operator's volume cycle

A long chart walk-through (1H/4H/day) that restates control of greed/fear and names where each ends (price of satisfaction / price of attraction), shows how to resolve a 4H three-candle test on the 1H and 5m, extends the candle-horizon scale to day/week/month, attacks fixed 1:2 risk-reward, and describes the large player's accumulation cycle (each push 1.5–2× the last, single → double → triple layer). The last ~30 minutes (01:10 onward) are disclaimers, services and life talk — no method.

### T274 — CONTROL OF GREED ENDS AT THE PRICE OF SATISFACTION; CONTROL OF FEAR ENDS AT THE PRICE OF ATTRACTION

*"On the one-hour, look, it has put four green candles continuously. If green candles come more than three, what does it mean? There is control of greed. Where does control of greed end? It ends at the price of satisfaction."* (031 · 00:12:43)
*"Price of satisfaction — many who bought in under value, the rich, say 'this is enough' and exit; that is called the price of satisfaction… Where control of greed ends, price of satisfaction applies."* (031 · 00:13:14)
*"Always, if the market has put three green or three red continuously, it means the market has a chance of settling at that place… Price of satisfaction — lots of selling happens here."* (031 · 00:33:44)
*"If control of greed comes as red candles, that is control of fear — many retailers sell out of fear, sell at throwaway prices. Where does it end? At the price of attraction… it tempts people to buy… the market has come to under value."* (031 · 00:34:16)

Extends T221 (≥3 same-colour stepping candles = control of greed/fear): after such a run, expect a settling/reversal zone — heavy selling after a greed run (over value), buying after a fear run (under value, corroborates T38). Where exactly the "price" sits is not given numerically *(unclear)*.

### T275 — RESOLVE A HIGHER-TIMEFRAME 3-CANDLE TEST ON A LOWER FRAME: 4H (12 h) → 1H (3 h) → 5m/15m

*"If control of greed has gone on for 4 hours… instead of giving three candles on the 4-hour and waiting 12 hours, if we downgrade to one hour, three candles means we can know in three hours."* (031 · 00:34:47)
*"The four-hour's boundary — what happens inside after 12 hours — the decision became known in the fifth minute… in the next 15 minutes it became clear: the body closed inside, beyond the wick."* (031 · 00:56:42–00:57:12)

The reverse direction of T239/T23: instead of escalating up, drop down to read the higher frame's 3-candle confirmation early. Consistent with T234 (3-candle wait scaled by timeframe). A body back inside past the prior wick on the lower frame = the test failed.

### T276 — HORIZON OF A CANDLE READ = ~3 OF ITS OWN BARS AT EVERY SCALE: 5m → 15 min, 1H → 3 h, day → 3 days, week → 3 weeks, month → 3 months

*"Candle analysis — take five minutes, it tells you what is likely in the next 15 minutes; take one hour, how the next three hours are likely to be; take the day candle, the next three days; weekly candle, three weeks; monthly candle, three months."* (031 · 00:50:05)

Extends T235/T240 to daily/weekly/monthly and corroborates T92 (a read is valid for a few bars of its own timeframe). Precise statement: forecast horizon = 3 × bar length.

### T277 — NO FIXED RISK:REWARD (e.g. 1:2); SIZE THE TARGET TO THE BOUNDARY BEING APPROACHED

*"They'll say 1:2 — does the market work exactly everywhere? Keep loss at 10 points, profit at 20 points — and the market goes 200 points. Then you just take 10, 20 points?"* (031 · 00:53:09)
*"The profit won't be uniform — ₹100, ₹500, ₹1000, ₹2000, ₹3000, ₹60, ₹40 — varying. You must know how to set the profit according to how the market reacts and in which boundary you approach the market."* (031 · 00:53:39)

Rejects fixed-ratio exits; the target depends on the boundary (short/mid/long). Consistent with T26/T31 (targets in quarters of the structure) rather than a new number.

### T278 — OPERATOR CYCLE: EACH NEW PUSH IS 1.5–2× THE PREVIOUS; LAYERS GROW SINGLE → DOUBLE → TRIPLE; UNMET 50% TARGETS DOUBLE (100 → 200 → 400)

*"He will take it like two times — one-and-a-half to two times… again they sell here, again it falls big… it becomes control of fear on the 5-minute, then on the 15, then on the one-hour."* (031 · 01:00:48)
*"More than a single layer — a V pattern… putting like this is single layer; when the market breaks this and comes, it puts double layer."* (031 · 01:01:18)
*"When it breaks this again it puts a triple layer… it will put one-and-a-half to two times this. Take it anywhere, this is what will have happened."* (031 · 01:01:49)
*"Each time the market will be at least 50% of it… if 50% doesn't happen overall, individual goes; wait for overall… if not, at the next boundary it goes properly."* (031 · 01:02:19)
*"Here it has gone 100 points. At the next boundary it has to go 200. If not, when it goes here they'll make it 400."* (031 · 01:02:49)

Corroborates T108 and T106 (1.5–2× the last counter-move) and T237 (major push ≥1.5×), and adds the layer escalation (T96) and a doubling of the unmet target per boundary. The 100/200/400 figures are one chart example *(heard once)*.

Other points in 031 (corroborations only):
- Failed candle: a green body that crosses the 50% line but closes with less than 50% of its body beyond it, even with ~85% buyer control, is a failed candle (031 · 00:16:47) — corroborates T266.
- Don't buy because a green candle appeared after crossing a boundary; green is for selling what you hold, buy on red in under value (031 · 00:15:47) — corroborates T38.
- A day-chart fear run of ~10 consecutive red days took out many supports (031 · 01:08:28); don't trade off a 5-minute glance — corroborates T92.
- Training benchmark: out of 10 demo trades at least 7 must be profitable, or overall in profit (031 · 01:07:27) *(heard once)*.
- Investment-style trading has no stop loss; stop-loss trading is high-margin/high-risk — trade only with money you can afford to lose (031 · 01:09:28–01:09:58) — corroborates T11, T249, T273.

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T274** | After ≥3 consecutive green candles (greed), expect settling/selling at over value; after ≥3 red (fear), expect buying at under value | **Partly — the end "price" is not given numerically** |
| **T275** | Read a 4H three-candle test (12 h) early on the 1H (3 h) or 5m/15m; a lower-frame body closing back inside past the wick = test failed | **Yes** |
| **T276** | A candle read's horizon ≈ 3 bars of its own timeframe (5m→15 min, 1H→3 h, D→3 days, W→3 weeks, M→3 months) | **Yes** |
| **T277** | No fixed R:R; target is set by the boundary being approached | **Partly — the target rule is only "by boundary"** |
| **T278** | Each new push ≈1.5–2× the previous; layers go single→double→triple; an unmet 50% target doubles at the next boundary (100→200→400) | **Partly — the 1.5–2× is testable; 100/200/400 is one example** |

## Video 032 — Current-candle control split, body/wick band, failed candles at the 25

Bitcoin (1H, then 15m) and a finished gold session. He reads each candle as a split of buyer/seller control (body = its colour, wick = the other side), uses that split to forecast the next few candles, and walks through the "wick beyond wick, then body beyond wick" sequence and the "failed candle" that closes past the 25 line with too little body. Roughly two-thirds of the video is talk about training, "tools vs trade", and psychology — dropped.

### T279 — CANDLE CONTROL SPLIT FORECASTS THE NEXT ~3 CANDLES

*"If you do current-candle analysis, then for the next three candles — if this is one hour, the next three hours; if 5 minute, the next 15 minutes — you can know in detail what the market will be like."* (032 · 00:06:39)

*"This is green body, there is wick above. The green body is more, some 80%; 20% is the upper wick. The upper wick belongs to the seller. So 100 − 20 … sorry, it is in the buyer's control."* (032 · 00:08:14)

*"If 15 minutes, for the next half hour to three-quarter hour there is a chance the market goes up … The body is some 30%, the wick is 70%. So the market is 70% in the seller's control."* (032 · 00:08:44)

*"For the next 15 to 30 minutes there is a chance the market comes down … this closed below the 50%, likely in the seller's control; so for the next 15, 20 minutes there is a chance the market comes down."* (032 · 00:09:14)

He applies T100's ownership rule as a number. Take a candle's range. The body's share goes to its colour and each wick's share goes to the opposite side. The side with the larger share is expected to drive the next ~2–3 candles of that timeframe. A close under the candle's own 50% gives the seller control. He does this check with ~30 s left on the candle (032 · 00:06:09: high/low, upper and lower wick, close above/below the 50% line). He adds that these are only "chances", not 100% (00:09:14).

### T280 — THE PRIOR SWING'S BODY–WICK BAND IS THE TEST ZONE

*"If it is coming down today, it must break this body-wick and put a body outside … Inside this area it can even put a body. On this boundary it can even put a body. But if it puts a body beyond this, the seller becomes aggressive; this base means there is a chance it comes to the current 50% and this low."* (032 · 00:05:06 – 00:05:37)

*"Only if a green outside body goes beyond this base is there a chance the market comes to this base again."* (032 · 00:06:09)

*"First wick must go beyond wick. Next, body must go beyond wick … otherwise all this is inside."* (032 · 00:12:47) *"If the body did not go, what does it mean? The market is negative. Only the wick has gone — and the upper wick belongs to the seller."* (032 · 00:11:46)

The zone to test is the band between the prior extreme's furthest body close and its wick tip, not one price. The counter side may close bodies inside the band. A body closing beyond the band flips control, and he gives targets of the current structure's 50% and then its low. A continuation needs two steps: first a wick beyond the prior wick, then a body beyond that wick. A wick alone = the other side holds. This refines T100/T101.

### T281 — FAILED CANDLE AT A QUARTER LINE; REPEATED FAILURE → AGGRESSIVE BREAK

*"It has closed beyond this 25 line, but its name is failed candle. Meaning, of the whole body at least 50% must close above this price line — strongly; that is a quality move. If it had, strong, OK confirmation. Otherwise its name is failed candle."* (032 · 00:12:17)

*"Failed candle means there is a chance the market goes up, but whether it goes far constantly is not known."* (032 · 00:12:47)

*"Could not even go to 25 — failed. Again could not even go to 25 — failed, failed, failed, coming down fast … when the market starts breaking…"* (032 · 00:44:30 – 00:45:00) *"Consistently it comes back without even reaching 25 — then the market comes aggressively."* (032 · 00:48:02)

This applies T20's 50%-body test to the quarter lines (T97) and gives the result a name: a close past a quarter line with less than half its body beyond it is a "failed candle". On its own it only lets a weak continuation happen. Bounces that keep failing at the 25 are his signal for an aggressive break of the low (the fall then goes "vertical", 00:48:34).

### T282 — THREE-CANDLE CLOSE BEYOND A BOUNDARY ≈ 90% HARD TO RECLAIM

*"If it closes outside beyond this boundary — and that too takes three candles' time to settle — then for the buyer to break this boundary, this body, this wick again is very difficult. Almost 90% difficult. That does not mean it must not go."* (032 · 00:57:13)

This is a persistence rule: a boundary that has held closes beyond it for three candles is expected to hold against a return about 90% of the time. *(heard once)* — he gives no count behind the 90%.

### T283 — NEVER BUY INTO A GREEN CANDLE

*"If what you buy is green colour, no champion can stop your loss. I say it as a challenge."* (032 · 00:45:00)

The entry candle must not be green: buy into red/falling candles. This is consistent with T2 (buy after red candles) and T70 (resting limits). *(heard once in this video, stated as absolute)*.

### T284 — AFTER A TRENDLINE BREAK, DISTANCE FROM THE LINE = OVER VALUE

*"After it breaks the low, the market falls vertically … it goes very far from the trend line … the points straight in line with this trend line are over-value points again. Those who bought in under value keep exiting as it reaches this area."* (032 · 00:48:34 – 00:49:05)

*"If it goes to over value the big player is going to sell; if there is a V, or under pressure it is available beyond all the boundaries, look for a way to buy."* (032 · 00:49:35)

When price runs far from its trendline after a break, he calls the far side "over value": expect exits and selling there, and buy only on a V or deep beyond the boundaries. This pairs with T73 (magnetic return to the trendline). The exact distance is not given, so it is *(unclear)*.

### T285 — CHART TIMEFRAME BY SESSION LENGTH

*"For a 24-hour market, 5 minute is enough; for something like NIFTY that is six, seven hours, you can take one minute."* (032 · 00:58:15)

The base timeframe shrinks as the session shortens: 5m for 24h markets (BTC) and 1m for NIFTY. *(heard once)*.

### T286 — MINIMUM BUSINESS RETURN 2–5% A MONTH

*"If one lakh in the bank gives me 600 rupees a month, I must take at least 10 times that a month … if you do trading as a business, you must take at least 2% to 5% a month."* (032 · 00:23:00)

He gives a monthly floor. This **conflicts with T82** (professional return 4–7% a *year*), unless T82 was meant for investment and this for trading. The coordinator should reconcile the two. *(heard once)*.

Corroborates T29: 20% of the work comes before the trade and 80% after (032 · 00:19:25, 00:25:03). Corroborates T91: successive buyer pushes that fall short of the earlier high ("second time force reduced, third time completely reduced — it will break the low", 032 · 00:17:54).

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
