| **T279** | Split each candle's range: body share → its colour, wick shares → opposite side (close under own 50% → seller). The majority side is expected to lead the next ~2–3 candles (e.g. 80% buyer on 15m → up for 30–45 min; 70% seller → down 15–30 min) | **Yes** |
| **T280** | Test zone = band between prior extreme's body close and wick tip. A counter-side body inside the band is allowed; a body beyond it flips control (target: 50% then low). Continuation needs wick-beyond-wick, then body-beyond-wick | **Partly — "body" beyond band needs a threshold (T20 50%?)** |
| **T281** | A close past a quarter line with <50% of body beyond = failed candle (weak continuation). Consecutive failures to reach the 25 → aggressive break of the low | **Yes** |
| **T282** | 3 candle closes beyond a boundary → ~90% the other side cannot reclaim it | **Yes** |
| **T283** | Never enter a buy on a green candle | **Yes** |
| **T284** | After a trendline break, price far from the line = over value (exits/selling); buy only on a V or beyond the boundaries | **Partly — "far" is not quantified** |
| **T285** | Base timeframe 5m for 24h markets, 1m for NIFTY | **Yes** |
| **T286** | Business-grade trading must return ≥2–5% per month (conflicts with T82) | **Partly — a performance target, not a signal** |

## Video 033 — Quarter "terms", multi-timeframe confluence, the 50% tug-of-war, stacked bases

Bitcoin 1H/4H/15m/5m/1m, gold 1H→1m, and a short NIFTY look. He names the four quarters of a leg as time horizons and treats the 50% as the main decision line. The core claim is that levels from different timeframes (a 1H base next to a 4H 50%) stack into one boundary. He also restates the "must not retrace more than half the opposing leg" test as a tug-of-war, and describes stacked bases as vessels that fill and overflow. The second half is about religion, luxury cars and "dead rats and incense sticks" — dropped.

### T287 — THE FOUR QUARTERS ARE NAMED TIME HORIZONS

*"This is zero; 25 is micro-short-term. 25 to 50 is short term. 50 to 75 is mid term. 75 to 100 is long term. This is only for the boundary the buyer carried."* (033 · 00:02:01 – 00:02:33)

*"If the buyer carried it from below, then at its 25 — micro-short-term — its 50 short term, its 75 mid term, its base 100 long term — there is a chance [buyers come back] layer by layer."* (033 · 00:06:38)

This names T97's quarters. A retracement that stops at the 25 is a "micro-short-term" pullback, and one that reaches 100 (the base) is a "long-term" one. Each quarter is a place where the original side may re-enter. The same ladder applies to a seller leg measured upward (00:05:36 – 00:06:08). His "short/mid/long" words in T107 likely refer to this ladder *(unclear — he does not link them explicitly here)*.

### T288 — THE 50% IS THE DECISION LINE; ABOVE IT OVER VALUE, BELOW IT UNDER VALUE

*"The 50% line is very, very important. That 0, 100, 50 is very, very important. Most decisions in the market are taken at the 50% boundary — whether the market is coming down from above or going up from below."* (033 · 00:03:03)

*"Just split a boundary in two and analyse — what is above is over value, what is below is under value; looking like that is enough."* (033 · 00:27:30 – 00:28:01)

*"So you don't get confused I split the boundary into just two lines: 0, 50, 100. That's all."* (033 · 00:40:44)

The working grid is 0/50/100. The 25/75 are optional "if needed" (00:45:49: *"this 75 is not needed; draw it only if required"*). This supports T38 (below 50% = under value).

### T289 — CROSS-TIMEFRAME LEVELS THAT COINCIDE FORM ONE BOUNDARY

*"It has a chance to come to the 4-hour's 50%. Right next to it is the one hour's base. So these two together are one boundary."* (033 · 00:04:04)

*"There is the one-hour base, then the four-hour base — all side by side as a boundary. If the market comes here, the chance of it breaking all these bases is very low."* (033 · 00:07:09)

*"Per 5 minute the 50% is here, and per 15 minute and per one hour — all three are here."* (033 · 00:11:45) *"At the one-minute base is the 5-minute's 50%. One is interconnected with another."* (033 · 00:27:30)

When one timeframe's boundary breaks, the next target is the adjacent higher timeframe's 50% or base. Where two or more timeframes' 50%/base levels fall close together, treat them as a single zone that is unlikely to break. He does not give a numeric "close together" tolerance *(unclear)*.

### T290 — THE HALF-LEG TUG-OF-WAR

*"For it to go up strongly, the buyer must pull back at least beyond 50% of however far the seller dragged it."* (033 · 00:10:43)

*"From wherever the buyer took it, the seller must not bring it down beyond its 50%. If he does, he becomes confident … If one pulls the rope 10 feet forward and the other pulls it back 5 feet, his energy and confidence go up."* (033 · 00:15:19 – 00:15:49)

*"If it comes down beyond its 50%, the probability of it going up again is low — low meaning it can only go after taking a lot of time, because the seller becomes strong again."* (033 · 00:20:55 – 00:21:25)

*"If the buyer puts an outside close beyond this [seller-leg 50%], it means there is a chance of coming to this base itself."* (033 · 00:25:28)

Two mirror tests on the last leg. (a) The counter-move must stay within 50% of that leg, or the trend is delayed and the counter side gains. (b) A counter-move that closes outside beyond the 50% projects to the full base (100%). This adds the 100% target and the "time delay" consequence to T106.

### T291 — THREE CANDLES HELD BEYOND A LEVEL: ~95%, AND IT HOLDS ~3× AS LONG

*"If they maintain one, two, three — three candles — breaking this again is some 95% difficult. I don't say they will never break."* (033 · 00:13:17)

*"If three candles pass … they tied the market for 15 minutes; another three times that is three-quarters of an hour — there is a chance it maintains above. 95% — the probability of it turning back without going to at least its basic target is low."* (033 · 00:13:48)

*"If three candles came beyond this current candle's body-wick — three hours on the one hour — the probability of coming back down is low."* (033 · 00:24:27)

This restates T282 (video 032 said ~90%; here ~95%). It adds a duration: a level held for 3 candles is expected to hold ~3× that time (15 min on 5m → ~45 min), and price should reach at least the next "basic target" before returning.

### T292 — START AT THE 1H FOR TRADING (MONTHLY FOR INVESTMENT), THEN MOVE DOWN

*"Whenever you begin a chart, come from long term to short term … for investment, long term is monthly. For trade, long term is one hour."* (033 · 00:21:55) *"Look first at the one hour, then downgrade if needed, upgrade if needed. Don't decide from the 5 minute straight away."* (033 · 00:22:25)

*"Depending on what kind of trade you do — scalper, intraday, futures-options, weekly expiry, monthly expiry — you choose the boundary."* (033 · 00:12:16 – 00:12:46)

Procedure: read the 1H range and its 50% first, then 15m/5m/1m for entry detail. This fits T92 (choose the analysis timeframe by trade life).

### T293 — BOUNDARIES CAN BE BODY-TO-BODY OR WICK-TO-WICK; TRENDLINES WICK-TO-WICK AND WICK-TO-BODY FOR DOUBLE CONFIRMATION

*"On 5 minute you can draw body-to-body as well as wick-to-wick … Trend lines too — you can draw wick to wick and also wick to body. You can draw both and take double confirmation."* (033 · 00:20:24 – 00:20:55)

*"Draw any one; enough. Body to body, or wick to wick."* (033 · 00:41:46)

This relaxes T14, which anchors trendlines on heads only. A body-anchored line is an accepted alternative, and agreement between the two is a "double confirmation".

### T294 — STACKED BASES HOLD; ONCE BROKEN, EMPTY SPACE IS CROSSED FAST ("VESSELS")

*"This area has one, two, three, four, five, six — about this many bases. Breaking it is no ordinary thing; if it breaks, there is this much empty space — it will come here swiftly."* (033 · 00:47:20)

*"If water falls from here — volume accumulated at the 5-minute level — it will flood to this area … it overflows and fills this. When this fills and the market empties this boundary, next it comes here; it fills this vessel. This is called accumulation."* (033 · 00:50:53 – 00:51:23)

Count the bases in a zone. Many bases = strong zone. Below the zone, a gap with no bases means fast travel to the next cluster once the zone breaks. This pairs with T72 (accumulation).

### T295 — CONTRARIAN CROWD SENTIMENT

*"When it goes to over value they talk positively … if analysts everywhere are positive, sell anything you hold and stay away. If everyone talks negatively about it, analyse it and look to buy."* (033 · 00:36:11)

This is a sentiment filter that pairs with T288's over/under value.

Corroborates T91 (033 · 00:16:50 – 00:17:51: six successive buyer pushes each failing to reach the prior one, "then it breaks and comes down"). Corroborates T29 (10–20% before the trade, 80% after, 00:19:22).

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T287** | Quarters of a leg: 0–25 micro-short, 25–50 short, 50–75 mid, 75–100 long; the original side may re-enter at each | **Partly — a labelling; test re-entry frequency per quarter** |
| **T288** | 0/50/100 is the working grid; the 50% is where most turns happen; above = over value, below = under value | **Yes** |
| **T289** | Where 50%/base levels of adjacent timeframes coincide, treat as one zone; low break probability; a break targets the next TF's 50/base | **Partly — "coincide" tolerance not given** |
| **T290** | Counter-move > 50% of the last leg → trend delayed; a counter-side outside close beyond the 50% → target the full base | **Yes** |
| **T291** | 3 candles held beyond a level → ~95% it holds, for ~3× that duration, and reaches at least the next target | **Yes** |
| **T292** | Trading analysis starts at 1H (investment at monthly), then down/upgrades; timeframe per trade type | **Partly — procedural** |
| **T293** | Boundaries body-to-body or wick-to-wick; trendline wick-to-wick and wick-to-body = double confirmation | **Partly — needs rule for which to use** |
| **T294** | Zones with many stacked bases rarely break; a zone with empty space below travels fast after a break | **Partly — "many" ≈6 in his example; "empty" undefined** |
| **T295** | Unanimous positive talk at over value → sell/avoid; unanimous negative → analyse to buy | **No — needs sentiment data** |

## Video 034 — Over value vs under value: the odds, the three-candle continuation test, the distribution trap

Bitcoin 1H and gold 1H. He follows up on the previous day's 50% call and walks candle by candle through inside candles and the "every three candles" test for trend continuation. His main lesson is a worked comparison: buying a green candle at over value versus buying at under value, with win rates and reward:risk. He ends with how the big player distributes at over value and shakes retail out on a fast drop into under value.

### T296 — THE TREND CONTINUES ONLY IF WICK-BEYOND-WICK AND BODY-BEYOND-BODY HAPPEN EVERY THREE CANDLES

*"Wick must go beyond wick, then body beyond body. Only if the market keeps doing this — within every three candles this process must happen … only then is there a chance it continues in the same trend. If every three candles it reverses opposite, the same trend becomes weak and the opposite becomes strong."* (034 · 00:05:36 – 00:06:07)

*"Count from this green: one, two, three — within the next three candles the buyer could not put a new body. Then the market has come into the seller's control."* (034 · 00:13:12)

This is a rolling three-candle window. A new extreme must print, first by wick and then by body, within every 3 candles. If a window passes without one, control passes to the other side, which is then expected to retrace ~50% of the leg (00:06:37). It **tightens T102**, which gives ~6 × 5m (30 min) without a new outside close. This is stated per candle count on any timeframe.

### T297 — BUYING GREEN AT OVER VALUE ≈ 30% WIN AT 1:2; BUYING AT UNDER VALUE ≈ 70% WIN AT 2:1

*"Buying at 76,000 and it going to 77,000 has only a 30% chance. That is why we say only three of ten trades go into profit in over value — whenever you buy seeing green, in over value, there is only a 30% chance."* (034 · 00:15:12)

*"When 77,000 comes to 75,000 — buying there and selling at 77,000 has a 70% chance. That is why I say always buy when buyer involvement comes in under value; don't chase and buy the market in over value."* (034 · 00:15:43)

*"Here the risk is 2,000 and the reward only 1,000 … in under value 70% chance of profit, profit 2,000, risk only 1,000 — exactly upside down."* (034 · 00:16:45 – 00:17:16)

*"Draw the 50%; below it is under value. To buy, focus below it; above it, under no circumstances."* (034 · 00:28:01) *"Above 50% I will not buy for any reason. If anything, I'll try to sell."* (034 · 00:47:15)

The under/over line is the 50% of the most recent buyer leg (low → high). Above it: no buys. Below it: buy only once buyer involvement appears (T68). The numbers are his round illustrations on BTC (76k/77k/75k). *(the 30/70 and 1:2 / 2:1 figures are stated as illustration, heard once)*. This reinforces T38 with an explicit win-rate claim.

### T298 — INSIDE CANDLE: WAIT ONE MORE CANDLE

*"This put its body lower, but could not put body beyond the wick. What does it mean? It is an inside candle … body beyond body, but it must put body beyond the wick. So this is an inside candle. Inside candle means if we wait one more candle, the market's reality will be known."* (034 · 00:04:36 – 00:05:06)

This applies T95 as a procedure: after an inside candle, make no decision until the next candle closes. In his example the next candle closed above the inside candle's 50% and beyond the seller's bodies, which was his buyer-involvement signal.

### T299 — CONTROL OF GREED / CONTROL OF FEAR = THREE CONSECUTIVE SAME-COLOUR CANDLES

*"It is called control of greed: consistently three greens keep going up. Likewise consistently three reds keep coming down. This is control of fear, control of greed."* (034 · 00:08:07 – 00:08:37)

This gives the vocabulary a count: 3 consecutive green = "control of greed" (a chasing zone, do not buy at over value); 3 consecutive red = "control of fear". It fits T96's "multiple layer" (3+).

### T300 — DISTRIBUTION AT OVER VALUE, THEN A FAST DROP INTO UNDER VALUE THAT REVERSES WITHIN TWO CANDLES

*"At the start red, then a bit of speed, a bit of speed, aggressive, aggressive. That is how the market will be … going up and down equally slowly, we think buyers won't let it come down — this is their trap."* (034 · 00:39:40)

*"Take the 5 minute — there will be many greens … this body was never allowed to break … up, down, up, down until the whole quantity is dumped. Of however many candles are in this, in one-third of those candles he brought it to under value."* (034 · 00:40:11 – 00:40:42)

*"If it came slowly, retail would enter … so he brings it fast; you sell in fear … the moment he gets the quantity, in the next two candles he puts it in profit. He will not come back to that area."* (034 · 00:49:46 – 00:50:16)

*"When it is in over value and the market shrinks — danger; run away."* (034 · 00:51:17)

Four measurable claims. (1) Distribution at over value = many green candles in a narrowing chop where the key body is never broken. (2) The drop to under value takes ~⅓ as many candles as the distribution. (3) After the fast drop, price is back in profit within ~2 candles and does not revisit the low area. (4) Contraction at over value is an exit signal. Claim (2) matches the "⅓" in T107. *(heard once each)*.

Corroborates T279: a candle with a 70% red body and 30% lower wick is "70% seller control" and forecasts 1–2 more candles down, unless following candles trade above its 50% (034 · 00:03:35 – 00:04:06). Corroborates T280/T100 (a green body beyond the seller's bodies = buyer involvement, 00:05:06).

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T296** | Trend continues only if a new wick extreme and then a new body extreme prints within every 3 candles; a 3-candle window without one hands control to the other side (→ ~50% retrace of the leg) | **Yes** |
| **T297** | Never buy above the 50% of the last buyer leg; buys below it (with buyer involvement) win ~70% at ~2:1 vs ~30% at ~1:2 above | **Yes** |
| **T298** | After an inside candle, wait for the next candle's close before acting | **Yes** |
| **T299** | 3 consecutive green = control of greed; 3 consecutive red = control of fear | **Yes** (definition) |
| **T300** | Over-value distribution (narrowing green chop) → drop to under value in ~⅓ the candles → back in profit within ~2 candles, low not revisited. Contraction at over value = exit | **Partly — "narrowing" and "fast" need thresholds** |

## Video 035 — Run-invalidation, how to drag a trendline, the "A vs V head-touch game", stacked 50% compression

Bitcoin 1H/Day, gold, NIFTY 1H/4H. He sets the invalidation test for a candle run and the procedure for drawing and using trendlines (a break sends price to the next 50% "price line"). He then reframes pattern reading as a game: each side tries to "touch the other's head", and the 50% is the "resting point" or half-point. Where several 50% lines stack, he expects a fierce, fast resolution. Long stretches on storytelling/chess/Bahubali — dropped.

### T301 — A RUN IS BROKEN BY A CLOSE UNDER THE PREVIOUS BODY OR UNDER THE CURRENT CANDLE'S 50%

*"It must keep putting body, body, body. For no reason must it close breaking the previous candle's body. It must not close below the current candle's 50%. If it does, the market is reversing — the market is settling."* (035 · 00:01:32)

*"Once it puts a red body below like this, the wick may go beyond the upper wick, but only if the body comes back above this can it go up. Otherwise it settles completely and comes down … and if that holds for three candles — one, two, three — the chance of breaking this body-wick is also low."* (035 · 00:01:32 – 00:02:02)

This is a concrete stop signal for a trending run. During a run every close must stay above the prior candle's body and above the current candle's own 50%. The first close that breaks either marks the run as settling. If the next ~3 candles stay below, the run's top body/wick is unlikely to be exceeded. This complements T296 and T291.

### T302 — TRENDLINES: DRAG UNTIL TWO CANDLE BODIES TOUCH; A BREAK GOES TO THE 50% "PRICE LINE"

*"Where the second one touches — minimum two candles' body touch — set it there."* (035 · 00:10:07) *"Hold one point, drag toward whichever low you are focusing on. Let it touch at least two candles' bodies; once it touches, stop and extend it fully. If the market breaks it again, it goes to the 50% of the high-to-low you drew, and then beyond to wherever the base is."* (035 · 00:12:12 – 00:12:43)

*"If a trend line is broken coming down, it is coming into under value … but can we buy right here as under value? No. For that the price line is this 50%."* (035 · 00:10:38)

*"If the first trend line breaks, it comes to the bigger trend line; only if that breaks does it go to the next wider one. So each trend line break → price line, next trend line, next price line … alternating."* (035 · 00:15:16 – 00:15:46)

*"Whatever trend line you draw, it connects one pattern — a V or an A."* (035 · 00:15:46 – 00:16:17)

This is a drawing procedure: the line must touch at least 2 candle **bodies**, which refines T14's head-anchoring. A trendline break is not an entry. Its target is the 50% of the swing the line spans, then the base, with the next larger trendline as the next stop. This supports T73.

### T303 — THE "HEAD-TOUCH" GAME: THE 50% IS THE HALF-POINT / RESTING POINT

*"The one going up with A patterns must not come and touch this head [the V's low]. If he touches it, there is a chance he breaks down. Only while he never touches it does he have the chance to go up and touch the A's head."* (035 · 00:24:27)

*"That break is this 50% line … this 50% line is the breaking point [rest point]."* (035 · 00:24:57 – 00:25:28) *"If he touches it, that's like half a point — that is why 50. If he touches the A's head itself, we call that 100 — the base."* (035 · 00:30:37)

*"If the buyer puts an outside close beyond this 50%, he has taken half a point; then the full point comes next, aggressively — that is where they decide … when finishing, the market comes to the base, they touch the head."* (035 · 00:36:09 – 00:36:39)

This restates T99/T101 as scoring. A = the upper head (high), V = the lower head (low). A side "wins" only by an outside close beyond the opponent's head. A failed pattern is one that stalls at the 25/50 without taking the head (00:31:08 – 00:31:38, the origin of the "Failed" name). An outside close beyond the 50% = half-point, and the rest of the move to the head follows aggressively.

### T304 — WHEN SEVERAL 50% LINES STACK, THE BREAK IS FIERCE AND FINISHES IN ~¼ OF THE STRUCTURE'S CANDLES

*"When the market shrinks like this, an equal fight is happening. Why? All three 50% lines are here … now it must be won; the competition will be very aggressive."* (035 · 00:33:38)

*"However many candles this whole thing is — within a quarter of those candles the next match will be over. That is why I said the market is very dangerous."* (035 · 00:34:08)

*"Here there is one, two, three 50%s. This place is a big match … whether it breaks with an aggressive candle, up or down, and the next two, three candles maintain it — that side is likely to win and touch the head."* (035 · 00:38:10 – 00:38:40)

This is measurable. Count the 50% lines (of recent buyer and seller legs) that cluster in a narrow zone: 1 = "local match", 3 = "big match". The side whose aggressive candle breaks the cluster and holds for 2–3 candles is expected to reach the opposing head, within ~25% of the candle count of the structure. This complements T289 and T294. *(the ¼ figure heard once)*.

### T305 — TERMS BY TIMEFRAME: 5–15m SHORT, 1H–4H MID, DAY–MONTH LONG

*"The match at the 5-minute, 15-minute level is a short-term match. At one hour, four hour, a mid-term match. At day, week, month, a long-term match."* (035 · 00:37:09)

This assigns horizon labels to timeframes. It **conflicts with T292** (video 033: "for trade, long term is one hour"). The two likely differ in frame of reference (033 was the trader's own horizon, 035 the market's), but he does not say so — *(unclear)*.

Corroborates T281 (a close beyond the line with <50% body = failed, 035 · 00:33:08). Corroborates T103 (uptrend = each A breaking the previous A's head, 00:18:19 – 00:18:50) and T105 (the first inside pattern after a run = "market starting to settle", 00:19:20).

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T301** | During a run, a close below the prior candle's body or below the current candle's 50% = run settling; ~3 candles thereafter below → run's top unlikely to be exceeded | **Yes** |
| **T302** | Trendline must touch ≥2 candle bodies; a break targets the 50% of the swing it spans, then the base, then the next larger trendline; a break alone is not an entry | **Yes** |
| **T303** | The side whose opposing head (A high / V low) is taken by an outside close wins; outside close beyond the 50% = half-point → move to head follows | **Yes** |
| **T304** | ≥2–3 coincident 50% lines = compression; an aggressive break held 2–3 candles reaches the opposing head within ~¼ of the structure's candle count | **Partly — "coincident" tolerance not given** |
| **T305** | 5–15m = short, 1H–4H = mid, D/W/M = long (conflicts with T292) | **Partly — a labelling** |

## Video 036 — Recap of the half-leg rules; why indicators are dropped; NIFTY gaps; 3-in-10 base rate

Bitcoin 1H/4H/15m/5m/1m, gold 1H, NIFTY 1H. The chart walk mostly restates T290 (a counter-move beyond 50% of the last leg → the base) and T289 (the 1H base sits at the 4H 50%). He adds trader/investor frames, gap handling on NIFTY and a long argument against indicators. There is also a claim that ~3 in 10 entries win on their own and 7 go negative, so the edge is in management.

### T306 — PURE PRICE CHART ONLY; NO INDICATORS

*"99% of indicators are repaint. Currently it shows buy, but if the market changes slightly the indicator's colour itself changes."* (036 · 00:30:08 – 00:30:38)

*"Take one indicator: on 5 minute it shows buy, 15 minute sell, one hour buy, 4 hour sell, day buy — which way will the market go?"* (036 · 00:50:03)

*"Overall seven of ten trades, every indicator will give only loss."* (036 · 00:49:01)

Method constraint: analysis uses raw OHLC only (plus volume as a fact, 00:33:11). He gives three reasons: repainting, contradictory signals across timeframes, and ~70% losers.

### T307 — BASE RATE: ~3 OF 10 ENTRIES WIN BY THEMSELVES; ~7 GO NEGATIVE

*"Even if you approach short term and read price, seven of ten trades will go to loss."* (036 · 00:34:44)

*"Three of ten trades will naturally come into profit — even if a small child places the order, even if a monkey places the order. The remaining seven will go negative. Then tightening those … I have said in old videos."* (036 · 00:43:22)

This is a falsifiable claim about entries. Unmanaged entries win ~30%, and management (T29) is meant to rescue the other ~70%. It matches the 30% in T297 (green buys at over value), but here he applies it to entries in general. It gives a baseline to beat when backtesting any entry rule.

### T308 — NIFTY GAPS: JOIN THEM; A GAP IS NOT AN ACTIVE PRICE AREA

*"Where there is gap-up/gap-down, just fill the chart. Wherever the gap is, fill it in. At gap-up/gap-down the market was not active … it may be pre-open volume, or block deals, bulk deals, or corporate trades outside trading hours."* (036 · 00:23:27 – 00:23:58)

Chart preparation: connect across the gap and treat the gap as untraded. Boundaries (high/low/50%) are then drawn on the joined chart. *(the effect on levels is not stated beyond "connect it")*.

Corroborates T292: *"For a trader, 5 minute, 15 minute, one hour — at least on these boundaries you must analyse; an investor day, week, month."* (036 · 00:11:44). He also says *"long term is one hour, mid term is 15 minute, short term is 5 minute"* (00:13:17), which **agrees with T292 and conflicts with T305's** 1H = mid. Corroborates T290 (00:02:35 – 00:05:07), T289 (1H base = 4H 50%, 00:07:10), T304 (two 50%s coinciding → "shrinks terribly … blasts up or down", 00:28:06 – 00:28:37), and T300 (price approaches the expected support slowly, then breaks it fast, 00:21:26 – 00:21:57).

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T306** | Use raw price (and volume) only; no indicators | **Partly — a method constraint, not a signal** |
| **T307** | Unmanaged entries win ~30% of the time; ~70% go negative first | **Yes** |
| **T308** | On gapped charts (NIFTY), join the gap and treat it as untraded before drawing boundaries | **Partly — handling of the joined level unspecified** |

## Video 037 — Equal-V confirmation, "every depth gets its 50%", the two 50% lines of a V, the profit band, and escalating the horizon after a loss

Bitcoin 1H/4H/15m/5m and gold 1H/4H. On the chart he covers the equal V pattern, the claim that every leg eventually gets at least a 50% counter-move, how to read a V with two 50% lines (one per leg), and how often a 50% line holds. In the fund-management digression he gives a per-trade profit band (₹100–999 on ₹10,000) and the "intraday loss → weekly expiry → monthly expiry" escalation ("chicken dies → goat").

### T309 — AN EQUAL V NEEDS AN OUTSIDE CANDLE BEYOND IT AND 2–3 CANDLES OF HOLD

*"It made an equal V pattern. But after an equal pattern it should have put an outside candle beyond this. Then the market should have sustained for the next two, three candles — if this is one hour, sustained it for the next three hours … it must not put a V again; it must not put an inside pattern or inside candle — inside meaning a red candle."* (037 · 00:04:36 – 00:05:37)

This extends T22's "Equal V = skip" with the condition that turns it into a valid V. The next move beyond the equal head must be an outside close, followed by 2–3 candles holding outside with no red inside candle. Otherwise the pattern fails, and in his example the low then broke.

### T310 — EVERY LEG ("DEPTH") GETS AT LEAST A 50% COUNTER-MOVE ("STRUCTURE"); A BOUNCE STALLING AT ≤25 MEANS THE LEG EXTENDS FIRST

*"However far the buyer brings it or the seller brings it down, at least a bounce to its 50% will definitely be there. Again, however far it goes, there will be one at the overall's 50%."* (037 · 00:07:11 – 00:07:42)

*"If it goes to 25 and can't go to 50, it goes down even faster. It will definitely come to the overall high's 50%; the market cannot go very far without it … only if it turns back without even reaching the 25 can it go down fast, because there buyer involvement is completely absent. Once its 50 is reached, the market retests that 50 again and again."* (037 · 00:07:42 – 00:08:42)

*"The seller bringing it down is depth. The buyer taking it back is structure. If there is such a depth, the structure will definitely follow."* (037 · 00:08:42 – 00:09:13)

This is a falsifiable claim. Every impulse leg is eventually retraced by ≥50%. If the first bounce stalls at or below its 25%, the leg extends aggressively before that 50% retrace comes. A reached 50% becomes a level that gets retested. It builds on T97/T98 (25% bounce = seller in control) and T108.

### T311 — A V HAS TWO 50% LINES; AN OUTSIDE CLOSE THROUGH EITHER AGAINST THE MOVE = "25% CONFIRMED", THE LOW WILL BREAK

*"Draw for what the seller brought down, draw for what the buyer took up. Where are both 50%s? If it is going up, it must not put an outside candle breaking this 50% line or this 50% line."* (037 · 00:37:29)

*"Just breaking this 50 coming down, the market is 25% confirmed. If it breaks this 50, it means it will definitely break the low. It comes down aggressively."* (037 · 00:37:59)

*"If it crosses this 50 it will break the base — definitely break, definitely break."* (037 · 00:39:30)

This is the V-specific form of T290. The seller leg into the V and the buyer leg out of it each have a 50%. For the up-move to continue, neither may be broken by an outside close. Breaking the upper one is a partial warning; breaking the buyer leg's 50% means the V's low (base) will break.

### T312 — A 50% LINE HOLDS ~7 TIMES IN 10; WHEN IT FAILS, PRICE REACHES THE NEXT BOUNDARY IN 3–4 CANDLES

*"This won't work everywhere. It works in about seven of ten trades; in three it won't. But when it doesn't, a big loss comes … because when it breaks, it goes aggressively — to wherever the next boundary is, swish, in just three, four candles."* (037 · 00:49:42)

This gives a hold rate and a failure speed for the 50% line (T288). About 70% of first tests hold. The ~30% that fail reach the next boundary within 3–4 candles. This is the direct numeric claim behind his warnings about "traps". *(heard once)*.

### T313 — ANALYSE CANDLES ONLY AT BOUNDARIES; THE BIG PLAYER ACTS ONLY THERE, MOSTLY IN THE PRIOR BODY-TO-WICK BAND AT A 50%

*"It is not necessary to do it everywhere. But when it reaches a boundary, candle analysis is high priority there."* (037 · 00:27:09)

*"Analyse at a boundary; that's enough. If you skip the boundary and analyse everywhere else, you'll be fooled … the big player comes only at the boundary. Selling what he has — at the boundary; buying new — at the boundary."* (037 · 00:40:31 – 00:41:02)

*"At each 50% line most selling happens … within the previous body-to-wick. If you have a position here, look to exit; otherwise start candle analysis to try a new sell."* (037 · 00:41:02 – 00:41:32)

This is a workflow and exit rule. Do the T279/T280 candle work only when price is at a boundary (0/50/100 or a TF-confluence zone). On a rise into a 50% line, the exit zone for existing longs is the prior swing's body-to-wick band at that level. This complements T61 (partial-close at the 50% if it stalls).

### T314 — FAILED OUTSIDE CANDLE CAN BE RESCUED ONLY BY A BODY BEYOND IT IN THE NEXT 1–2 CANDLES

*"It is an outside candle, but 50% of the body is not outside, so it is a failed candle … at least 50% of its body should be there, otherwise within the next one or two candles a body must go beyond that body. A wick going is no use."* (037 · 00:24:36 – 00:25:07)

This refines T281 with a time limit. A breakout candle with less than half its body beyond the level is a failure unless one of the next 1–2 candles closes its body beyond the breakout candle's body.

### T315 — PER-TRADE PROFIT BAND ≈ 1–10% OF CAPITAL; MORE IS A DANGER SIGN

*"If you take 10,000, cut two zeros. Your profit must be in that range — the biggest three-digit number is ₹999. So your trade profit must be ₹100 to ₹999. If some day that trade gives 2,000, 3,000, 4,000, 5,000, it will surely strip you entirely one day."* (037 · 00:57:50 – 00:58:20)

*"With 10,000, we use about 3,000 at most. But our return … we too get the same ₹100 to ₹999. That is the secret of time investment."* (037 · 00:59:52 – 01:00:23)

*"More time investment, less money investment = high reward; more money, less time = low reward."* (037 · 01:00:53)

This is a risk-sizing test. A single trade's profit should be about 1% to <10% of account capital. A 20–50% win means the position was oversized ("the foundation of ruin"). Deploy ~30% of capital and hold longer ("time investment") rather than deploy all of it briefly. This fits T6 (cap trading capital) and T39/T41 (the ~1% floor).

### T316 — ESCALATE THE HORIZON AFTER A LOSS: INTRADAY → WEEKLY EXPIRY → MONTHLY EXPIRY

*"If you failed in an intraday, you must approach the market at least at a weekly-expiry level; only then can you earn profit beyond that loss."* (037 · 01:04:58)

*"From here to here took five days; coming back will take at least two days. Only if you give it these two days can you take all this loss back."* (037 · 01:05:28) *"Chicken dies, then goat; goat dies, then cow."* (037 · 01:05:28 – 01:06:00) *"Likewise if loss came in weekly expiry, it can only be fixed in monthly expiry."* (037 · 01:10:35)

*"Only if you give time equal to at least half of however many days it has been since here … can you come to overall profit."* (037 · 01:07:33)

This is a recovery procedure and an extension of T23/T48 to option horizons. After a losing trade at one horizon, the recovery trade goes on the next longer one. It should be given at least ~½ the time the adverse move took. He treats intraday-for-intraday recovery as impossible (01:10:35). *(the "half the time" figure heard once)*.

Corroborates T97 (a V whose bounce reached only 25% = "25% buyer, 75% seller control", 037 · 00:09:43 – 00:10:15). Corroborates T91 (the second push's wick falls short of the first, "wicks forming slanting", 00:26:07). Corroborates T108 (after a V breaks, the next leg is longer, 01:03:26). Corroborates T307 (money-only investment: 7 of 10 lose, 01:11:05).

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T309** | An equal V becomes valid only after an outside close beyond its head and 2–3 candles holding outside with no red inside candle | **Yes** |
| **T310** | Every leg is eventually retraced ≥50%; a first bounce stalling at ≤25% means the leg extends aggressively first; a reached 50% gets retested | **Yes** |
| **T311** | In a V, an outside close through the buyer leg's 50% (against the up-move) → the V's low will break | **Yes** |
| **T312** | A 50% line holds on ~70% of tests; the ~30% that fail reach the next boundary within 3–4 candles | **Yes** |
| **T313** | Do candle analysis only at boundaries; exit longs / look for sells in the prior body-to-wick band at a 50% line | **Partly — "boundary" set needs fixing** |
| **T314** | A breakout candle with <50% body beyond the level fails unless the next 1–2 candles put a body beyond it | **Yes** |
| **T315** | Target per-trade profit ≈1–10% of capital (₹100–999 per ₹10k), deploying ~30% of capital; bigger wins signal oversizing | **Partly — a sizing norm; testable as a filter** |
| **T316** | After a loss, the recovery trade goes on the next longer horizon (intraday→weekly→monthly) and gets ≥½ the adverse move's duration | **Partly — horizon mapping to charts not given** |

## Video 038 — Failed/Equal/Extra defined; ten-candle "settled"; capital ≤ 2× monthly income; breakout vs boundary entries; trend channels

Bitcoin Day/4H/1H/15m/5m/1m. He defines "failed/equal/extra" once, for candle, pattern and trend alike. He repeats T290/T311 and adds a ten-candle rule for when a side has "settled". He warns against breakout entries (3 of 10 win) in favour of entering at the boundary, gives a starting-capital cap, and draws a trend channel with its "opposite side" target.

### T317 — FAILED / EQUAL / EXTRA = WHERE THE COUNTER-MOVE ENDS RELATIVE TO THE PRIOR BASE

*"It went to 50% and turned back without going further — what do we call it? Failed V pattern. Why? It could not go to its base — the place from where the seller brought it."* (038 · 00:07:34)

*"If the pattern turns back without going, failed pattern. If it goes slowly and reaches this base, equal V pattern. If it goes beyond the base, extra V pattern."* (038 · 00:12:12)

*"Failed candle, whatever it is, the same is failed pattern; failed pattern, the same is failed trend … equal trend, failed trend, extra trend; equal pattern, failed pattern, extra pattern; equal candle, failed candle, extra candle."* (038 · 00:09:08 – 00:09:39)

These are the missing definitions behind T22's "Equal / Failed / Extra V are skips". Take the leg into the pattern (e.g. the seller's leg into a V) and its base, the point where that leg started. The counter-move is **Failed** if it stalls before the base (typically at 25–75) and then breaks the V's own 50%. It is **Equal** if it reaches the base. It is **Extra** if it closes beyond the base. The same scale applies to a single candle (vs the prior candle's range) and to a trend.

### T318 — ~10 CANDLES WITHOUT BREAKING THE BOUNDARY = THAT SIDE HAS "SETTLED" (GIVEN UP)

*"One, two … ten candles and more. So the market has settled at most. For ten minutes the buyer couldn't break this boundary. On one hour that would be waiting ten hours; on 5 minute, one hour. That is why I said each place has its own validity."* (038 · 00:48:55 – 00:49:25)

*"Nearly ten hours the market has only put wick beyond wick … favourable to the seller."* (038 · 00:19:22) *"Settled means they have totally fed up … lost confidence totally. Then the chance is only coming down."* (038 · 00:22:53)

This gives the count behind "settled". If a side fails to put a body beyond the boundary for ~10 consecutive candles (on any timeframe), it has given up and the move goes the other way. It is a longer window than T296 (3 candles) and T102 (6 × 5m), and it matches T92's "validity ≈ a few bars". *(the exact 10 is his count in one example)*.

### T319 — TRADING CAPITAL ≤ 2× MONTHLY INCOME; GROW IT BY DOUBLING

*"At most, whatever your monthly income is, twice that — you must not bring more than that to trade. Develop it, and if you really are a skilled trader, make two into four, four into eight."* (038 · 00:38:41)

*"Always, beyond your monthly income, don't take twice the risk. Trade is 100% risk."* (038 · 00:39:42)

This is a hard cap on the starting account, heard twice. It sits alongside T46 (own capital only) and T6 (cap trading capital at 50%). Add money only by compounding results.

### T320 — ENTER AT THE BOUNDARY, NOT ON THE BREAKOUT; BREAKOUT + STOP + 2R WINS ~3 IN 10

*"Don't jump saying it broke — breakout! Jumping itself is wrong. If you're selling, you must do it here [at the boundary] … you'd have taken a breakout sell here, put the stop loss here, target twice the stop loss … it comes three times; seven times it will tear away. Losing seven and profiting three, how will you make it?"* (038 · 00:52:31 – 00:53:02)

*"This outside candle — 75 to 80% of its body is inside. So it is a failed candle. Don't see it as 'it came, it came' at once. Wait and look for the next couple of minutes."* (038 · 00:52:00)

*"Everyone will have put their stop loss here … one candle comes, harvests the stop losses, and goes again."* (038 · 00:40:13)

Entry location: sell (or buy) at the tested boundary with candle confirmation, not after the breakout candle. He makes the negative claim testable: standard breakout entries with a stop and a 2:1 target win about 30% (compare T307). Stops sitting just past the prior extreme get "harvested" by a single candle that then reverses. This corroborates T314.

### T321 — TREND CHANNEL: FAILURE AT ONE LINE SENDS PRICE TO THE OPPOSITE LINE; A CHANNEL BREAK MOVES YOU DOWN A TIMEFRAME

*"Joining the two heads of a V pattern is a trend line. Join these two trend lines and pull it down to where this V pattern's low body is — that is a channel. If it can't go beyond the upper line, it will definitely come to the channel's ultimate low, the opposite side, straight across from here."* (038 · 00:54:04 – 00:54:35)

*"It will touch this channel, touch, touch and bounce back like a ball. If it breaks out and puts an outside, it must break back into the channel and put an inside to go the other way."* (038 · 00:57:08)

*"If it completely breaks the channel, the market has finished this boundary; then you must go to the 5 minute."* (038 · 00:56:07)

This is a construction plus two predictions. The parallel line goes through the pattern's opposite extreme (a body). A rejection at one rail targets the other rail. A clean break of the channel ends that timeframe's structure, so analysis moves to the next lower timeframe. He says he does not draw these when trading live (01:01:39), so treat it as illustration. This complements T73 and T302.

Corroborates T297 (buying at over value sells at profit only 3 of 10; at under value 7 of 10, 038 · 00:33:05 – 00:33:37). Corroborates T100 (repeated large lower wicks below the boundary = buyers strong → price reaches the seller leg's 50%, 00:23:54). Corroborates T108/T310 (each successive A is larger; the structure grows from 15m–1H to 1H–4H to Day, 00:13:12).

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T317** | Counter-move stops before the prior base = Failed; reaches the base = Equal; closes beyond it = Extra (same scale for candle, pattern, trend) | **Yes** (definition) |
| **T318** | ~10 consecutive candles with no body beyond the boundary = that side has settled; expect the opposite move | **Yes** |
| **T319** | Starting trading capital ≤ 2× monthly income; grow only by compounding | **Partly — a sizing norm** |
| **T320** | Enter at the boundary, not on the breakout; breakout entries with a stop and 2R win ~30%; stops just past the prior extreme get hunted by one candle | **Yes** |
| **T321** | Channel = trendline through two V heads plus a parallel through the opposite body extreme; rejection at one rail → the other rail; a channel break → drop a timeframe | **Partly — construction is loose** |

## Video 039 — "Support" fails 7 in 10; the 80% split restated; growth and return norms

Bitcoin Day/4H/1H/15m/5m/1m and gold 1H/15m/4H. The chart walk restates the half-leg rules (T290/T311) and nested boundaries (T289). The new content is in the asides: a claim that a classic "support" level holds only 3 times in 10, a breakdown of the 80% after-entry work, and the growth path of a small account.

### T322 — A CLASSIC "SUPPORT" HOLDS ONLY ~3 IN 10; 7 IN 10 IT BREAKS INTO CONTROL OF FEAR

*"Whether the market goes up there because there is support — I don't want to say it goes or doesn't. Three of ten times it goes, that's all. Seven of ten times you get control of fear, it scares you — because if he has started accumulation here, he dumps the market to squeeze you, and only after that lifts it."* (039 · 00:17:25 – 00:17:56)

*"Seven of ten times the market breaks the support and comes in control of fear. I am telling you plainly: if you know there is support, won't the big player know?"* (039 · 00:21:30)

*"Where you think there is support, exactly there the market goes aggressively into control of fear."* (039 · 00:16:24)

This is a falsifiable claim, separate from T312. A visible prior-low "support" gets swept roughly 70% of the time before any rally. That is different from the 50% price line, which he says holds ~70% (T312). The accumulation signature (T72) begins with that sweep. It also explains his preference for 50% lines over support/resistance (*"dummy support-resistance, trendline broken — these are things that don't work"*, 00:33:15).

### T323 — GROWTH NORMS: ~10% A MONTH ON A SMALL ACCOUNT; EACH DOUBLING TAKES ABOUT HALF THE TIME OF THE LAST

*"If you take only ₹10,000 of capital, see whether you can earn ₹1,000 a month."* (039 · 00:49:09)

*"However long 10,000 to 20,000 takes, 20,000 to 40,000 won't take that long; it takes half of it. 40,000 to 80,000 takes half of that."* (039 · 00:29:11 – 00:29:41; restated 00:48:06 – 00:48:39)

*"Invest only twice your monthly income and do it steadily. In the next three years this business will give you 10 to 15 times your monthly income every month."* (039 · 00:50:09)

These are aspirational performance norms, not signals. The ~10%/month figure **conflicts with T286** (2–5%/month, video 032) and with **T82** (4–7%/year). The "halving doubling time" claim follows from compounding plus experience. *(each heard once in this form)*.

Corroborates T319: *"twice your monthly income, your own money"* (039 · 00:27:08, 00:43:59). Corroborates T46: 90% borrowed margin = slavery to the lender (00:43:29). Corroborates T29, with a split he gives here: *"fund management 30, psychology 30, connecting it as a trading system 20 — 80% of things are there"* (00:35:19). Corroborates T307: support/resistance/trendline-break trades lose 7 in 10 (00:33:15). Corroborates T280: *"first wick beyond wick is a symptom, then body beyond wick; if wick cannot even pass the wick, there is no force"* (00:13:51 – 00:14:22).

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T322** | A classic prior-low "support" holds only ~30% on first test; ~70% it is swept first (control of fear) before any rally | **Yes** |
| **T323** | Growth norms: ~10%/month on a small account; each capital doubling takes ~half the time of the previous; aim for monthly profit 10–15× salary within ~3 years (conflicts T286/T82) | **Partly — performance targets, not signals** |

## Video 040 — Depth and structure defined; expanding V/A; the phase vocabulary; nested targets

Gold 1H/5m and Bitcoin. He defines "depth" (the move against the trendline) and "structure" (the return toward it), gives the structure a size relative to the depth, and explains how a V becomes a "bigger V". He also sets out the named phase sequence (price of opening → control of greed/fear → price of satisfaction/attraction) and the candle → pattern → trend target hierarchy. The last third is biography and business model — dropped.

### T324 — DEPTH = THE MOVE AWAY FROM THE TRENDLINE; STRUCTURE = THE RETURN; STRUCTURE ≈ 1–1.5× DEPTH

*"If it went against a trend line, its name is depth … if it came toward it, its name is structure."* (040 · 00:18:27)

*"However far it comes, however much depth, the matching structure will form in the market … like this, one to one-and-a-half times definitely. This cannot be avoided in the market. This is the fuel point."* (040 · 00:19:29 – 00:19:59)

*"For the V to become a bigger V, the seller's distance must become bigger, and the buyer's distance must also become bigger — to what extent? At least to the extent of touching this trend line. That is a bigger V."* (040 · 00:15:55 – 00:16:26)

*"At some point it cannot become a bigger V, cannot break down, does not become a failed V pattern — it just maintains. That is settling."* (040 · 00:15:55)

This gives the terms precise meaning and a size. The return leg is ≥1× and ≲1.5× the preceding leg's excursion from the trendline. That **differs from T108** (1.5–2× the last counter-move) and from his "one or two times" in 037 · 00:07:11. The coordinator should reconcile the ranges. "Expanding" patterns (each V/A bigger than the last, both legs growing to at least the trendline) mean trend continuation. When a bigger V can't form and the low doesn't break, the market has settled (cf. T318).

### T325 — LAYER COUNT SHOULD GROW WITH DEPTH

*"As the market's depth increases, the number of layers must also keep increasing. Layer — single layer, double layer, multiple layer, it's in old videos … how much seller involvement there is, that is depth. How the buyer reacted to it, that is structure."* (040 · 00:10:46 – 00:11:17)

This links T96 (layers) to depth. A deeper sell-off should be answered by a bounce with more consecutive green candles. A deep leg answered by a single layer is a weak structure (cf. T98). *(heard once; no numeric mapping given)*.

### T326 — NESTED TARGETS: A CANDLE BREAK AIMS AT THE PATTERN LEVEL; A PATTERN BREAK AIMS AT THE TREND LEVEL

*"If it breaks the candle, where is the pattern's [level]? Here. So it's going to come here. If it breaks upward, where is the pattern's? There — it will go there. Then where is the overall trend's? Here. First it breaks the candle; next it must break the pattern. Breaking a candle → to the pattern; breaking the pattern → to the trend."* (040 · 00:33:49 – 00:34:20)

This is the targeting ladder. The first target after a candle-level break (T280/T301) is the current pattern's head or 50%. After that pattern level breaks, the target becomes the trend's level. This sits alongside T289 (timeframe confluence) as the within-timeframe version.

### T327 — THE PHASE SEQUENCE, AND WHAT ENDS A RUN OF FAILURES AT THE 25

*"I have told you: going up — price of opening, control of greed, then price of satisfaction. Coming down — price of opening, control of fear, price of attraction. At some point it came down, couldn't go to 25, but couldn't go lower either. Then the market breaks the 25 and goes at least to 50."* (040 · 00:07:12)

*"Once the price of attraction comes, the market cannot keep going in control of fear."* (040 · 00:07:12)

The named phases, with T299's count for control of greed/fear:
1. **Price of opening**: the turn.
2. **Control of greed / fear**: ≥3 same-colour candles.
3. **Price of satisfaction / attraction**: the over/under-value zone where the big player exits or accumulates (T288, T297).

This refines T281. Repeated failures at the 25 give an aggressive break *if* new lows keep printing. If bounces fail at the 25 but the low stops breaking, the next move breaks the 25 and reaches at least the 50.

Corroborates T290, with a tolerance: *"it may even go to the 50%, and even if it puts one or two candles outside, the market should settle and come back — then it becomes a failed pattern"* (040 · 00:10:15). So 1–2 candles poking past the 50% do not by themselves flip control. Corroborates T310: *"however high the market goes, it must come to its average price"* (040 · 00:12:17). Corroborates T294: bigger vessels overflow into the next (00:22:01 – 00:22:31). Corroborates T302: price above the trendline = over value, below = under value, and price keeps returning to the line (00:20:30, 00:25:06).

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T324** | Depth = excursion away from the trendline; structure = return toward it, ≈1–1.5× the depth (conflicts T108's 1.5–2×); both legs growing to at least the trendline = bigger V (trend continues); no bigger V and no break = settled | **Yes** |
| **T325** | Deeper legs should be answered by bounces with more layers (T96); a single layer after a deep leg = weak structure | **Partly — no mapping from depth to layer count** |
| **T326** | Candle-level break → target the current pattern's level; pattern-level break → target the trend's level | **Partly — "level" = head or 50%, not fixed** |
| **T327** | Phase order: opening → control of greed/fear (≥3 same-colour candles) → satisfaction/attraction. Repeated failures at 25 without new lows → break of 25 to ≥50 | **Yes** (the 25→50 claim) |

## Video 041 — Three 50% lines and the "blast"; volume at under value; trendline channel mid-line; over value as one-sided expansion

Bitcoin 1H. He draws the three 50% lines of buyer→seller→buyer legs and explains how they compress and then "blast". He adds a volume test for under value and derives a "price line" as the midpoint between an upper and a lower trendline. Over/under value are redefined as the side on which patterns keep expanding. The fund-management digression repeats the intraday → weekly-expiry recovery with numbers.

### T328 — DO NOT TRADE INSIDE A CONTRACTION; AFTER THE BLAST, THE 50% CLUSTER MUST NOT BE RE-ENTERED

*"If the market starts shrinking, it will blast somewhere — explode and scatter on one side … when the market is shrinking, be careful; don't approach the market close to such a shrinking market. It's like pulling a dog's tail."* (041 · 00:11:40 – 00:12:42)

*"Now the market going up must not come back below these three 50% lines. If it comes below … it will come into this base."* (041 · 00:12:42 – 00:13:14)

This turns T304 into an entry rule. While ≥2–3 of the recent legs' 50% lines cluster and price oscillates among them, take no position. After the breakout candle ("blast"), a return inside the cluster invalidates it, and the target becomes the base of the structure.

### T329 — VOLUME AT UNDER VALUE: FALLING PRICE + RISING VOLUME = ACCUMULATION; FALLING PRICE + FALLING VOLUME = KEEP OUT

*"When it comes to under value, the price falls and the volume increases. Price getting lower, volume getting higher — that shows accumulation."* (041 · 00:37:08)

*"Price falls and volume also falls — that is not a good sign for the market to go up … price falling but volume not rising means the market goes further down; the big player isn't holding up this low price."* (041 · 00:37:39 – 00:38:10)

*"Don't buy while it's going up. Let it come down; buy when it comes down and then goes up again. If it keeps coming down, coming down, don't buy."* (041 · 00:43:46 – 00:44:16)

This is a falsifiable volume filter for T68 (buyer involvement), and the volume counterpart to T94. In the under-value zone, require rising volume on the decline, and enter only once price turns back up. A decline on shrinking volume is not accumulation, so expect more downside. His example: volume 10,000 at ₹10 should be 11–18,000 as price drops to ₹8 (00:38:10).

### T330 — THE MIDLINE BETWEEN AN UPPER AND A LOWER TRENDLINE IS A "PRICE LINE"

*"Draw only trend lines. Where the two trend lines meet, put a line at that mid point. That is the trend line's price line. You don't even take the 50% line."* (041 · 00:48:52 – 00:49:23)

*"If it cannot go above this trend line and puts an outside candle breaking the price line, it will hit this [lower] trend line. That is the calculation."* (041 · 00:50:25)

*"If it's coming down, it must not break this price line and come back up; if it does, it comes to the trend line's base again."* (041 · 00:51:57)

This is an alternative to the leg-50%. Draw the upper line (joining A-pattern bodies) and the lower line (joining V-pattern bodies), each touching ≥2 bodies (T302). Their midpoint line acts as the 50%. Rejection at the upper line plus an outside close through the midline targets the lower line. It extends T321.

### T331 — OVER VALUE = PATTERNS EXPAND ONLY UPWARD (TRENDLINES KEEP GETTING PUSHED HIGHER); AN EXTRA PATTERN = THE EXTREME TO FADE

*"The bottom does not get bigger, only the top keeps getting bigger — that is over value. Likewise under value: the top doesn't get bigger, only the bottom does."* (041 · 00:20:50)

*"The trend line that was here came here, the one here came here … pushing the trend line up, up, up — this is called over value. The first trend line you draw is the base; if it breaks that base, the market is in over value … push a bit more and it becomes equal; a bit more and it becomes extra."* (041 · 00:21:20 – 00:21:51)

*"If it has gone to over value, it has gone to the extreme — a terrific opportunity, for a sell. Likewise at the bottom extreme, for a buy … this is zero; above it +10, +20 … +50 over value; below −10 … −50 under value."* (041 · 00:53:27 – 00:54:27)

This is a structural definition of over/under value that complements the 50% definition (T288). Count successive trendline breaks in one direction with no expansion on the other side. Once the latest pattern becomes an Extra (T317) beyond the first trendline, treat it as the extreme and look for the opposite trade. Fund management is still required (00:54:27).

Corroborates T316 with a time budget: *"if you took a loss in over value this morning, it won't come to under value today; it takes two days to come; then it takes a day, a day and a half to go to profit; altogether four, five days — that's weekly expiry"* (041 · 00:38:40 – 00:39:10). Corroborates T288: with a single boundary use 25/50/75/100; with multiple boundaries use only the 50% lines (00:04:01 – 00:04:32). Corroborates T297 (00:27:28 – 00:28:59, 7 of 10 lose at over value). Corroborates T292/T285: for BTC 1H = long, 15m = mid, 5m = short; for NIFTY's 6–7h session treat 1m as short term (00:01:00 – 00:01:30). This **again contradicts T305**.

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T328** | No entries while price oscillates inside a cluster of 2–3 coincident 50% lines; after the breakout, a return inside the cluster → target the base | **Partly — cluster tolerance** |
| **T329** | In under value: falling price with rising volume = accumulation (buy after the turn up); falling price with falling volume = more downside | **Yes** |
| **T330** | The midpoint between an upper (A-body) and lower (V-body) trendline is a price line; rejection at one rail plus an outside close through the midline → the other rail | **Yes** |
| **T331** | Over value = successive trendlines pushed one way with no expansion on the other; an Extra pattern beyond the first trendline = the extreme → look to fade (with fund management) | **Partly — "extreme" needs a count** |


## Video 042 — Weekly outlook from three 50% lines; forecast horizon per timeframe

He resets the Day, 4H and 1H charts, joins each visible low→high as that timeframe's "trend", and reads the three 50% lines as a map of the next one to three weeks. He then does the same on 15m/5m/1m for the short term. He gives an explicit table of how far ahead a candle, a pattern and a trend analysis "see" on each timeframe. The rest covers return expectations, compounding claims and the no-stop-loss stance.

### T332 — FORECAST HORIZON BY TIMEFRAME AND ANALYSIS LEVEL

*"…only if you take a one-hour candle and analyse it can you see how the market will be for the next two or three hours. If you do the one-hour pattern, you can see how the next 5 to 10 hours will be… [trend] for the next two or three days"* (042 · 00:18:54–00:19:24; 00:18:54)
*"if you analyse just one four-hour candle, you can see how the market will be for the next three candles, about 12 hours"* (042 · 00:20:56)
*"if you analyse the last V pattern or last A pattern [on 4H]… about 24 hours, how one day will be… a 4-hour trend… two to three days. Likewise take the Day and analyse a single candle — the next two to three days… a pattern — three to five days"* (042 · 00:21:26)
*"a trend — what movement is likely from one week to three weeks"* (042 · 00:21:56)
*"a single 15-minute candle — how the next 45 minutes to 1 hour will be. A 15-minute pattern — from 1 hour to 3 hours… a 15-minute trend — the next 5 hours to 10 hours"* (042 · 00:45:34–00:46:05)

This is a horizon table that extends T92 ("validity ≈ a few bars") and puts numbers on it:

| TF | Candle | Pattern | Trend |
|---|---|---|---|
| 15m | 45 min–1 h | 1–3 h | 5–10 h |
| 1H | 2–3 h | 5–10 h | 2–3 days |
| 4H | 3 candles ≈ 12 h | ≈ 24 h | 2–3 days |
| Day | 2–3 days | 3–5 days | 1–3 weeks |

He is inconsistent in one place: a few seconds after saying 5–10 h, he restates the 15m trend as *"within the next 3 to 5 hours"* (042 · 00:46:05). The 1H and 4H trend horizons are identical (2–3 days) as spoken.

### T333 — WEEKLY OUTLOOK = THREE 50% LINES (DAY / 4H / 1H)

*"I take one chart, reset it like this, draw the visible low to high. The same for one hour… the same for the Day. If you draw these three, for the next one week minimum, three weeks maximum…"* (042 · 00:08:07)
*"…you can clearly understand what the market will be like… everything above all the 50% lines is over value; in over value the sellers' contribution is higher"* (042 · 00:08:38)
*"is the market below, above or in between these three 50% lines? If it is below, it is in a down trend"* (042 · 00:48:10)
*"all three lines — one hour, 4 hour, Day — it is below them, so the overall market is in a down trend, the overall market is in under value… under value means get ready to buy"* (042 · 00:48:40)
*"become the person who buys when it is available at a low price; do not become the one who sells there"* (042 · 00:49:10)

Procedure: on each of Day, 4H and 1H, connect the single visible extreme low and high. Price below all three midlines = down trend + under value, so prepare to BUY (never sell into it). Above all three = over value. The same is done on 15m/5m/1m for the short term (042 · 00:45:34–00:46:05). This corroborates T38 and T297. It adds that "down trend" and "under value" are the same reading, and that the correct action in a down trend is to buy.

### T334 — PATTERN BIAS: THE COUNTER-SIDE'S 50% MUST HOLD

*"[on the 1H] the 50% of the fall from the top is here, the 50% of the rise from the bottom is here; the market is now between the two. If it goes above this it will go aggressively fast, because the market is contracting. If it comes down it will break this low fast… if it is going up, it must not come below the 50% line the buyer carried"* (042 · 00:19:55)
*"if it is to go up, it must not break the 50% line of what the buyer carried"* (042 · 00:20:25)
*"a rising market must not break this base line; if it comes below, it will go down strongly"* (042 · 00:20:56)

A pattern (last down leg + last up leg) has two 50% lines. Price squeezed between them = contraction, and the break goes fast in the break direction. An up bias stays valid only while price holds above the buyer leg's 50%, and a down bias only while price stays below the seller leg's 50%. This corroborates T312 and T304 (contraction → blast). The same "three 50% lines, contraction → one side fast" reading is repeated at 01:13:42–01:14:12.

### T335 — RETURN NORM AND COMPOUNDING CLAIMS

*"whatever you get as bank interest, 5 to 7 times that should come in a month… if you put one lakh in the bank and take 6,000, here 5 to 7 times, so ₹4,200. If 3,000 to 4,000 is not coming here, you are not doing it right in this industry"* (042 · 00:39:24)
*"we keep ₹30,000 of it [out of ₹1 lakh] and earn 3 to 5 times what you earn with the full ₹1 lakh"* (042 · 00:23:27)
*"[10,000 →] 10,100… the next third trade 10,200. Like this every third trade can keep growing your capital"* (042 · 00:28:35)
*"only until you do 25 or 30 trades do you take risk on your capital. After that your floating loss will be inside your profit"* (042 · 00:29:06)
*"20,000 → another 10,000 won't take a year, only six months… three months… one and a half months… 15 days… a week… every day"* (042 · 01:09:02)

His numbers are internally muddled. "5–7× bank interest" is spoken per month, but ₹4,200 on ₹1 lakh is 5–7× of 6,000/year spread over 12 months, i.e. 4.2%/month. Read literally, the norm is **~3–4.2% per month on capital**. This agrees with T286 (2–5%/month) and contradicts T82 (4–7%/year) and T323 (10%/month). "Every third trade +1%" and the halving time-to-next-10k corroborate T323. Deploying 30% of capital corroborates T315 and T57 (3–5×).

**Corroborations in 042 (no new rule):**
- Control of fear = the market shows 1–2 tests of the support that retail can see, as if going up, then falls fast through it (042 · 00:10:08) → T322/T299.
- Bring capital of at most ~2× your monthly salary, after training (042 · 00:43:33) → T319.
- The work is to rest orders at the levels; sitting and watching does not bring price there (042 · 00:30:39) → T70.
- Without a stop loss the profit is ~2× what it is with one (042 · 00:52:42) → T11/T45. *(heard once)*

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T332** | A candle/pattern/trend reading on 15m, 1H, 4H and Day stays valid for the horizons in the table (e.g. 4H candle ≈ 3 bars/12 h; Day trend 1–3 weeks) | **Yes** — measure how long a directional read holds per TF/level (15m trend quoted as both 5–10 h and 3–5 h) |
| **T333** | Price below the Day, 4H and 1H visible-range 50% lines = down trend + under value → prepare to buy, never sell; above all three = over value | **Yes** |
| **T334** | Within a pattern, an up bias holds only above the buyer leg's 50%, a down bias only below the seller leg's 50%; price between the two = contraction → fast break | **Yes** |
| **T335** | Target ~3–4.2%/month on capital (5–7× bank interest); +1% every third trade; 30% deployment earns 3–5× full deployment | **Partly** — the return figure is a benchmark that can be checked against backtest P&L; the conversion is ambiguous and it conflicts with T82/T323 |

## Video 043 — Top-down 50%-boundary read on BTC, gold, NIFTY (Day → 4H → 1H → 15m → 5m)

He resets each chart, connects the visible high and low, and reads whether price sits above, below or inside the 50% line on each timeframe, stepping down from Day to 5m (and 1m). He then reads the successive buyer/seller pushes at the 50% line to call the next base. The last ~25 minutes are about Fibonacci "wonders", "unchanging things" (price, volume, high/low) versus indicators, and philosophy — no further method.

### T336 — THE 50% BOUNDARY READ (ABOVE / BELOW / INSIDE)

*"Normally take a chart, reset it, connect wherever the maximum visible low is and wherever the visible high is, and see whether it is below or above that."* (043 · 00:01:34)
*"We have already said: is it below, above or inside a 50% boundary — these three things are all we must look at in a trend analysis. If it is below, the market is in the seller's control."* (043 · 00:08:11)

The trend-level version of the pattern boundary: per timeframe, the midpoint of the visible (reset-chart) high–low range is the line; price location relative to it is the state. Same measuring idea as T38 / T97 but applied as a trend state.

### T337 — TIMEFRAME TRIADS BY HORIZON

*"For short-term we use three timeframes … 1 hour, 15 minute, 5 minute — this is for analysing the trend short term. After that 1 hour, 4 hour, day are fairly long term … for an investment zone, analyse day, week, month."* (043 · 00:04:06 – 00:04:38)
*"5 minute, 15 minute, 1 hour is for analysing the market short term; 1 hour, 4 hour, day for somewhat long term."* (043 · 00:17:20)

Fixes which three charts are stacked for each horizon. Complements T92 (choose the analysis timeframe by trade life).

### T338 — NEXT BASE ONLY AFTER AN OUTSIDE CANDLE BEYOND THE WHOLE BOUNDARY

*"Now we have two 50% lines … only if it puts an outside candle beyond this — outside the whole boundary — can the market come to this base. Otherwise … it will break the base below, and below that there are successive bases."* (043 · 00:05:10)
*"This is 1 hour's boundary, this is the day-and-4-hour boundary. If the market is going up, only if it puts an outside beyond both these boundaries can it reach this base."* (043 · 00:24:31)

When several timeframes' 50% lines stack, the band between them is one boundary; the move to the opposite base (0/100) needs an outside close (T95) beyond the whole band, else price reverts to the base on the other side. Links T99/T101.

### T339 — BELOW 50% ON EVERY TIMEFRAME = UNDER VALUE: PREPARE TO BUY, DO NOT SELL

*"If it has come below 50%, it is in the seller's control — correct — but you must be ready to buy; don't forget that, that is what rich people do. Having said it is in the seller's control you must not sell; it is under value, available at a lower price."* (043 · 00:08:11 – 00:08:41)
*"1 hour, 4 hour, day — the market is below all of them … below 50% means the market has come to under value."* (043 · 00:09:41)

Corroborates T38 (under value on every timeframe) and adds the explicit prohibition on shorting it.

### T340 — SHRINKING BUYER PUSHES AT THE 50% LINE → LOWER BASE

*"First time the buyer took it this far, second time this, third time this — all three times the price keeps getting less. But the seller brought it this much first, more the second time … so they will take it below this."* (043 · 00:12:14 – 00:12:45)
*"First it could not cross; second time it came equal; third time also the same place … only if it breaks this will there be buyer involvement."* (043 · 00:14:18)
*"First went this far; second could not cross this line; third time only put a wick; fourth time didn't even put a wick. Now it's coming down — it can come to this base."* (043 · 00:18:22)

Counts successive reactions at the line: when each buyer push is smaller than the last and each seller push larger, expect the prior low and the next base below. Operational form of T91 (shrinking pushes = weakening side).

### T341 — BETWEEN TWO TIMEFRAMES' 50% LINES = NEUTRAL

*"The 4 hour and day's 50% line is here, the 1 hour's 50% line is here … the market is inside this boundary … when it is inside the boundary the market is likely to be neutral."* (043 · 00:15:48)

A third state for the multi-timeframe read in T336.

### T342 — A 50% BREAK MUST HOLD AND ITS BODY MUST CLEAR THE PRIOR WICK

*"It came below the 50% but could not sustain below it for the next one hour … it put a wick beyond the wick, more body than body, but could not bring body beyond the previous wick, because the lower wick belongs to the buyer."* (043 · 00:26:02 – 00:26:34)
*"Body beyond body, wick beyond wick — it must put body beyond wick. Only if this happens can it come here."* (043 · 00:28:39)

Corroborates T100 and T20 with a concrete requirement: the body must close beyond the previous candle's wick, and the break must still hold one bar later (on his 1H example). *(sustain duration heard once)*

### T343 — THE 0 / 50 / 100 LINES ARE WHERE VOLUME CHANGES HANDS

*"The place where the most volume is exchanged is this 50% line and those base lines — 0, 100 — at these lines it happens the most. That is where the market changes."* (043 · 00:27:36)

Claims reversals cluster at 0/50/100 of the visible range.

### T344 — THE COMPLETED TREND IS FOLLOWED ~70%

*"There will be a completed trend and a current trend. The market will follow the completed one 70%; the current trend will always have 70% accuracy. You must read the current trend using the completed trend."* (043 · 00:29:42) *(heard once)*

No precise definition of "follow" given.

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T336** | Per timeframe, connect reset-chart visible high/low; state = above / below / inside the 50% line | **Yes** |
| **T337** | Short term: 5m/15m/1H; long term: 1H/4H/Day; investment: Day/Week/Month | **Yes** — a parameter choice |
| **T338** | With stacked 50% lines, the far base (0/100) is reached only after an outside close beyond the whole band; else price goes to the near base | **Yes** |
| **T339** | Below 50% on all timeframes → no shorts; prepare buys | **Partly** — "prepare" has no trigger |
| **T340** | 3+ reactions at the 50% line with each buyer push smaller and each seller push larger → prior low breaks toward next base | **Yes** |
| **T341** | Price between a lower-TF and higher-TF 50% line = neutral | **Partly** — no action attached |
| **T342** | A break of 50% counts only if the body clears the previous wick and holds for the next bar | **Yes** *(hold duration heard once)* |
| **T343** | Reversals/volume cluster at the 0/50/100 lines of the visible range | **Yes** |
| **T344** | Market follows the completed trend ~70% | **No** — "follow" undefined |

## Video 044 — Checking yesterday's calls; the "×3" validity ladder and the base cascade

He verifies the previous day's BTC/gold/NIFTY calls (price reached the predicted base), then reads today's candles at the 50% line (wicks vs bodies over ~3 hours), the stacked 5m/15m/1H/4H/Day bases, and layers on the V bounce. He states how long a candle, pattern and trend read stays valid on each timeframe, and how fast price travels when successive bases break. The last third is about training, psychology and why retail fear is the rich man's profit.

### T345 — VALIDITY LADDER: CANDLE = 3 BARS, PATTERN = 3 BARS OF THE NEXT TIMEFRAME, TREND = 3 BARS OF THE ONE AFTER

*"When we take a day chart, multiply it by three … the frame after day is week, multiply that by three — up to three weeks at most, that expiry, the day chart will show you clearly. If you take a 5-minute candle, three 5-minute candles is 15 minutes; if you do 5-minute candle analysis you can know how the market will be for the next 15 minutes."* (044 · 00:19:28 – 00:19:58)
*"If you analyse the boundary … the next bigger frame is 15 minutes, three 15 minutes is three-quarters of an hour to one hour."* (044 · 00:19:58)
*"Candle is candle-times-three; pattern is in the next frame; trend is in the frame after that. 1 hour — a candle is 3 hours; a 1-hour pattern, the next is 4 hour, 12 hours; a 1-hour trend, the next is day — the next three days. Analysing the 1-hour trend and analysing one day candle are the same thing."* (044 · 00:20:28 – 00:21:00)
*"Take a monthly candle — three candles is three months. A monthly pattern — after monthly is three months, three of those is nine months, so yearly. The monthly trend — three years."* (044 · 00:26:05)

Makes T92 numeric: a read on timeframe *t* is valid for 3 bars of *t* (candle), 3 bars of the next timeframe (pattern), or 3 bars of the timeframe after that (trend), on his ladder 5m→15m→1H→4H→Day→Week→Month→3M. His 5m boundary example (≈45–60 min) uses the pattern step.

### T346 — THE 5m/15m/1H STACK FORECASTS ONE TO THREE DAYS; RE-READ AFTER 3 HOURS

*"If you draw 5 minute, 15 minute, 1 hour — these three — you can know how the market is likely to be from at least one day to at most three days."* (044 · 00:32:48)
*"Short term means at most one day, at least three hours, gold is likely to reach this place. Whether it goes aggressively beyond … only if we give it about three hours again and analyse can we say the next thing."* (044 · 00:31:12)

Horizon attached to the short-term triad of T337. Earlier in the same video he says "the next one or two days" for the same stack (044 · 00:30:09) — the upper bound is *(unclear)*.

### T347 — HOLD AT THE 50% LINE WITH WICKS ONLY = BUYER CONTROL; TARGET THE OVERALL 50%

*"As soon as it touched this 50% line, only the wick went; the body could not go … As soon as this candle touched, one, two, three — for three hours the market sustained, settled here. For more than three hours the buyer held the market here."* (044 · 00:06:11 – 00:06:41)
*"If body goes beyond the body that already came, the market will certainly go to the 50% of this overall high."* (044 · 00:07:12)
*"Below, it can only put wicks; the lower wick belongs to the buyer … for more than three hours it could not break this body and put a body. So the market is in the buyer's control, settling. Automatically it can go to wherever this high's 50% is."* (044 · 00:24:03)

When price reaches a base/50% line and, for ~3 hours (on the 1H), only wicks — never bodies — close beyond the extreme body, the side defending the line holds control; the expected move is to the 50% of the overall range. Applies T100 with a duration.

### T348 — BASE CASCADE ACROSS TIMEFRAMES

*"1 hour's base is here, 15 minutes' base is here, 5 minute's base is here. So where will the next base go? If it breaks below, it will go to the 4-hour boundary."* (044 · 00:11:48)
*"To see where the next boundary is, you must upgrade and draw in 4 hour. To look closer and closer, downgrade and draw; to see where it can go further and further, upgrade."* (044 · 00:09:13 – 00:09:43)
*"Below this, it must not put a body outside — it may put a wick. The lower wick belongs to the buyer. If it puts a body, the market will surely go to the next source."* (044 · 00:11:48)

Target selection: when a body closes beyond the current timeframe's base, the next target is the next-higher timeframe's base (5m→15m→1H→4H→Day). A wick beyond does not trigger it (T100).

### T349 — EACH BROKEN BASE MULTIPLIES THE SPEED (×2, ×3, ×4, ×5)

*"It will go at least up to about 50% of that base, and then come very aggressively. Second, when it breaks the 5-minute base and goes to the 15 minute, there will be twice the speed. Breaking the 15 minute's and going in the 1 hour, three times the speed. Breaking the 1 hour's and going to the 4 hour, four times, five times the speed."* (044 · 00:22:01) *(heard once)*

Refines T74 (speed scales with the clock). "Speed" = distance per unit time *(unclear which unit)*.

### T350 — UNDER VALUE ON EVERY TIMEFRAME: A FURTHER DEEP FALL BEFORE A RETURN TO THE OVERALL 50% IS UNLIKELY

*"Because the market is below the 50% of day, 4 hour, 1 hour, 15 minute, 5 minute — all of them — it is strongly under value … buyer involvement will certainly be high … the probability that the market goes much further down without first bringing it at least to the 50% of the overall boundary and taking their profit out is low."* (044 · 00:13:50)

A falsifiable claim attached to T38's "under value on every timeframe".

### T351 — A NEW LAYER THAT CANNOT EXCEED THE PREVIOUS LAYER = STILL SELLER CONTROL

*"Here a green candle formed — single layer. Here a double layer … here a triple layer. Now it put the next layer; this layer could not go above the layer already put. It is still in the seller's control."* (044 · 00:14:21)

Refines T96: layers are compared by height; a later layer group that fails to exceed the earlier one's high leaves control with the seller.

### T352 — NEVER AVERAGE A FALLING MARKET WITHOUT A KNOWN BOUNDARY

*"If the market keeps going down in red candles out of control, can you take a flat entry everywhere? You can't … averaging a market without knowing a boundary is like catching a knife falling from above with your tongue — a terrible risk."* (044 · 00:16:24 – 00:16:55)
*"Don't look only at under value. Buying in under value is one rule, that's all. But is there demand in the under value? Is the rich man's involvement there? … Volume means the big rich man's involvement."* (044 · 00:17:25 – 00:17:57)

Corroborates T68 (buyer involvement required) and T78; adds that adds require a marked boundary.

### T353 — THREE STACKED 50% LINES + CONTRACTION = AGGRESSIVE BREAK EITHER WAY

*"Is the market above the three 50% lines, below the three, or inside the three — see these three things."* (044 · 00:33:20)
*"If it doesn't [close a body beyond], the market shrinks inside a boundary like this — it will blast and go up aggressively."* (044 · 00:33:50)
*"The seller must bring it more than the seller brought before … put at least one outside candle beyond this … The buyer's deadline is here, the seller's deadline is here; the market shrinks. Whether it breaks up or down it will break aggressively."* (044 · 00:34:50 – 00:35:21)

Corroborates T104 (O pattern) using the 5m/15m/1H 50% band as the range; adds that a side must beat its own previous push with an outside close to break it.

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
