## Video 076 — BTC: reading push by push, buying the sell trend, four 50% lines, horizon → timeframe

He reads a V push by push, counting red and green legs. He explains why every V has an A before it and draws both, which gives four 50% lines. He redraws each time price crosses a 50%. He matches the confirmation timeframe to the trade horizon, and moves down a timeframe only while the ultimate low holds. The last third is about capital, debt and borrowing.

### T565 — COUNT PUSHES: INDIVIDUAL VS OVERALL STRENGTH

*"More than the first, second; more than second, third; third to fourth; fourth to fifth; fifth to sixth — but seven couldn't [exceed] six. Only now are the sellers getting weak."* (076 · 00:12:52)
*"Individually they brought it further, but overall they couldn't. So inside, the seller is strong, but outside the seller couldn't stay strong."* (076 · 00:14:54)

Number each successive push. A side is strong while each push exceeds the previous one, and the first failure marks weakening. Check each push against the last one (individual) *and* against the largest earlier push (overall). This corroborates T91 and T103, and adds the individual/overall split.

### T566 — BUY THE SELL TREND, SELL THE BUY TREND

*"If it keeps going as a failed V pattern, the market is in the seller's control… if it goes up as failed A patterns, the market is in an uptrend. Don't buy because it's an uptrend… an uptrend means the market is heading toward over value — if you have something, sell it at a good price; otherwise just watch."* (076 · 00:17:58)
*"A sell trend means many people will sell and we should buy and enjoy."* (076 · 00:18:29)
*"'Sell trend' doesn't mean you should sell. In a sell trend you should buy. 'Buy trend' doesn't mean buy — in a buy trend you should sell."* (082 · 00:38:51)

A chain of failed V patterns (lower lows) means retail is selling, so prepare to buy. A chain of failed A patterns (higher highs) means over value, so exit or sell. He repeats this in 078 (00:25:20) and 080 (00:13:47). It is the trend-direction form of T38.

### T567 — READ THE CURRENT PATTERN WITH THE ONE BEFORE IT: FOUR 50% LINES

*"Without an A pattern, a V pattern can never form in the market — know this clearly."* (076 · 00:20:32)
*"Now we see four in the market… four green [50%] lines. The market is below all four — on the 4H it is strongly below. Only if the market goes above all four will the next thing happen."* (076 · 00:29:44–00:30:14)
*"The middle has a 50% here, a 50% here, a 50% here. If the market breaks these three 50%s and comes down, it will come right to this source."* (080 · 01:03:34)

Draw the 0/50/100 of both legs of the completed pattern and both legs of the current pattern. The four 50% lines form a band. Direction is decided only when price closes beyond all of them. This extends T105.

### T568 — REDRAW EACH TIME PRICE CROSSES THE 50% OF THE LAST LEG

*"First draw for this. Has the market gone beyond its 50%? If it has, start drawing for this… Has it come below its 50%? If it has, draw for this again. Simple — keep drawing each time it crosses a 50%."* (076 · 00:35:49)

The fib is re-anchored to the newest leg whenever price crosses the current leg's 50%. This is a procedure that pairs with T36 and T556.

### T569 — MATCH THE CONFIRMATION TIMEFRAME TO THE HORIZON; INTRADAY: DIRECTION IN THE FIRST 2 HOURS

*"If you're doing a monthly-expiry future, you don't need to check whether the 5 minute or 15 minute crossed — the 4 hour crossing is enough… But if you're doing intraday, your expiry is one day, and that's only six hours. Within the first two hours you must know the market's direction; only then can you take the trade and execute it in the next four hours."* (076 · 00:38:24)

Pick the confirmation timeframe from how long the position must live (T92). For NIFTY intraday, direction is settled in the first 2 hours and trades are executed in the remaining ~4 hours. This relates to T93, which has the violent hours at 09:15–10:00.

### T570 — MOVE DOWN A TIMEFRAME ONLY WHILE THE ULTIMATE LOW HOLDS; IF IT BREAKS, MOVE UP

*"Read only when it comes below the low. Don't read the patterns before that — the ones in between are inside patterns."* (076 · 00:42:07–00:42:38)
*"Only as long as this low doesn't break can you downgrade and look smaller. If it breaks the low, go to the 4H and analyse."* (076 · 00:43:08)
*"You must mark the ultimate low, not the small little lows."* (076 · 00:43:40)

Mark the ultimate low of the current structure. Drill into smaller timeframes only while it holds. If it breaks, move up to the next timeframe and re-read. This corroborates T105 (ignore inside patterns).

### T571 — BORROW ONLY IF EACH RUPEE OF INTEREST RETURNS ~5

*"If I borrow at one rupee interest, I must be able to invest it in my business and see five rupees profit — only then, having paid one, I have ₹4 profit. If there's no way to repay it, never borrow; do what you can with your own money."* (076 · 00:56:28)

This refines T80, which requires ~3× the interest. Here the example is 5×. In 081 he uses 3× against the bank rate (T590).

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T565** | Strength = each push exceeds the prior; judge both vs the last push (individual) and vs the largest earlier push (overall) | **Yes** |
| **T566** | Chain of failed V (lower lows) → prepare to buy; chain of failed A (higher highs) → exit/sell | **Yes** |
| **T567** | Draw both legs of the prior and current pattern; direction only after price clears all four 50% lines | **Yes** |
| **T568** | Re-anchor the fib to the newest leg whenever price crosses the current leg's 50% | **Yes** — a procedure |
| **T569** | Confirmation timeframe matches horizon; NIFTY intraday direction set in the first 2 h, executed in the last ~4 h | **Yes** |
| **T570** | Drill down only while the ultimate low holds; on a break, move up a timeframe | **Partly** — a procedure |
| **T571** | Borrow only if expected profit ≈ 5× the interest | **No** — a policy |

## Video 077 — Gold: accumulation by low-cutting legs, O-pattern retests, junctions, the wick/body progression

He calls the first item "new": buyer legs that repeatedly cut the prior buyer leg's low without taking its high mark accumulation, and he checks this against the volume indicator. He also covers:

- O-pattern boundaries being retested;
- the "junction" of an A pattern;
- the wick/body progression that decides a break;
- the 1m/5m/15m 50% zone as a day filter;
- the claim that 7 of 10 trades go against you first;
- a percentage split of a single candle.

### T572 — BUYER LEGS THAT CUT THE PRIOR LOW (NOT THE HIGH) = ACCUMULATION; CONFIRM WITH VOLUME

*"If they are only cutting the low — this is new, I haven't said this before, note it carefully — if they are only cutting the low, the buyer's accumulation is getting strong; many rich people are buying; a lot of volume is coming in on the buy side."* (077 · 00:07:39)
*"Mark the low and the high of the leg the buyer already took. The second time, within that low and high, check whether they cut the low. If they cut the low, big rich people are coming. If they can't even cut the low, volume isn't big — the market is 100% in the seller's control."* (077 · 00:11:44)
*"Buyer volume was 406 here, became 441. Seller volume was 524, and then it's only 465 — decreased. So the buyer's control is increasing, the seller's decreasing."* (077 · 00:12:14–00:12:44)

A buyer leg that starts below the prior buyer leg's starting low, while failing to exceed its high, is read as absorption, not weakness. Confirm it with rising buy-bar volume and falling sell-bar volume at those lows. The exact geometry is *(unclear)* from the captions. This is a candidate operational test for T68 and T72.

### T573 — AFTER AN O PATTERN THE BUYERS RETEST INSIDE IT; THE BREAK DOES NOT TRAVEL FAR

*"An O pattern — it breaks neither the head nor the tail of the previous pattern… a lot of candles inside."* (077 · 00:04:06)
*"Buyer involvement has become steady and strong here. So when it breaks this and forms a new pattern, the chances of the market going far again are very, very low. The buyer will keep retesting inside this boundary where it put the O pattern."* (077 · 00:04:37–00:05:07)

This **contradicts T104** ("an O pattern will break hard one way"). Here, an O pattern that holds buyer involvement leads to repeated retests of its own range, and the break is short. The two could be reconciled if T104 refers to shrinking ranges and this to a flat accumulation box *(unclear)*.

### T574 — JUNCTION RULE FOR AN A PATTERN

*"This is its start, this is the end; the edge where the A pattern begins — we call it the junction, because it's where what the buyer brought and what the seller brought meet."* (077 · 00:22:27)
*"It couldn't cross the junction — the second time too. And it didn't break the end either. So this time it must break the junction and go up. If it goes up, the chance of reaching 50% of the overall seller leg is high."* (077 · 00:22:58–00:23:28)
*"Body can go inside the junction; junction beyond junction is fine; but if a body goes beyond the junction, the story is over."* (077 · 00:46:20)

In an A pattern (start, junction, end): if price twice fails to cross the junction but also does not break the end, expect the junction to break next, targeting 50% of the overall seller leg. A body close beyond the junction is decisive.

### T575 — WICK/BODY PROGRESSION: WICK BEYOND WICK OK, BODY INTO WICK OK, BODY BEYOND WICK = BREAK

*"If a wick goes beyond the wick, next time what will they do? They'll bring a body into the wick. Once they bring a body beyond the wick, danger — it's over, it will break and go down. Until the body comes into the wick you needn't panic; even wick beyond wick is no problem."* (077 · 00:45:19)
*"Body can go into the wick, wick can go beyond wick, but if the body goes beyond the wick, the story is over."* (077 · 00:46:20)
*"A market going up goes swiftly: body beyond body, wick beyond wick… If it's going even faster, how to find out? The body keeps going beyond the previous wick, and the next body beyond the next wick."* (079 · 00:07:12)
*"Only if the body goes beyond this wick is that trend active… otherwise it is weak — however many candles it puts inside that area."* (080 · 00:28:34–00:29:04)
*"Even after the body goes beyond the wick, the next three candles must be growing candles, outside candles. If they're failed candles or inside candles, the market won't make it."* (080 · 00:30:36)

The sequence at the prior extreme candle is: wick beyond wick, then body inside the prior wick, then body beyond the prior wick. Only the last one is a break. After it, the next 3 candles must be outside candles; failed or inside candles cancel it. "Body beyond the previous wick on every bar" is the signature of a fast trend. This refines T100 and T101 into an ordered test.

### T576 — A DAY IS "UP" ONLY IF PRICE HOLDS ABOVE THE 1m/5m/15m 50% ZONE FOR ~8 HOURS

*"If the market has to stay up for the next 24 hours, it must maintain above the 50% of these three — 5 minute, 15 minute, 1 minute — for at least 8 hours without breaking below. Only then will it go further up today."* (077 · 00:34:42–00:35:13)
*"Maintain three 1-minute candles above it, and in the next three minutes not break below this 50% — then the probability of coming down again is very low."* (077 · 00:35:13–00:35:44)
*"Only if it maintains above these three green lines for the next four hours, 8:00 hours, is there a chance of the market being in an uptrend today."* (077 · 00:36:15)

For a 24-hour market (BTC), the day's bias is up only if price stays above the combined 1m/5m/15m 50% zone for 4–8 hours *(unclear which)*. The micro version is 3 one-minute closes above plus no break in the next 3 minutes.

### T577 — SEVEN OF TEN TRADES GO AGAINST YOU FIRST

*"Of ten trades you attend, seven will go to the negative side; only three go directly to profit. Seven go negative."* (077 · 00:41:47–00:42:17)
*"However technically you trade, seven of ten trades go unfavourable, against you. Three trades go to profit naturally."* (081 · 00:14:14–00:14:44)
*"Tighten technically → from seven losing we cut two-three: three becomes five… tighten with fundamentals and fund management → two more… the remaining three — trading flavours and mechanism, base entry and support entries."* (082 · 00:24:06–00:25:08)
*"Where we approach, eight or nine trades out of ten go to profit directly."* (082 · 00:10:53)

The baseline is 3 of 10 entries going straight to profit and 7 going adverse first. His layering claims 3 → 5 (technical) → 7 (fundamental + fund management) → 9 (mechanism). In 075 (00:54:18) he gives a different figure: technical alone = 10% win, 90% lose. This relates to T67 (80 wins per 100).

### T578 — PERCENTAGE SPLIT OF ONE CANDLE: SELLER % = UPPER WICK + RED BODY

*"The upper wick is about 25% of the candle, the body 50% — together 75% red and upper wick. Only 25% is the lower wick. So 75% of that candle is in the seller's control and 25% the buyer's."* (077 · 00:58:03)

Buyer share = lower wick + green body; seller share = upper wick + red body, each as a fraction of the candle's high–low. This quantifies T100.

### T579 — DO CANDLE ANALYSIS ONLY AT THE 50% OR AT A BOUNDARY

*"Whenever it comes to 50%, start candle analysis. Be careful there."* (077 · 00:42:17)
*"Don't sit and do it everywhere. Candle analysis is for the 50, or the boundary — 75–100, 25–50 — inside that area."* (079 · 00:31:09)

The candle-level tests (T540–T543, T575) are applied only when price is at a 50% line or inside a 25–50 / 75–100 boundary.

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T572** | Buyer legs that undercut the prior buyer leg's low without taking its high, with rising buy volume and falling sell volume, = accumulation | **Partly** — geometry unclear |
| **T573** | After an O pattern with buyer involvement, price retests inside it and the breakout does not travel far | **Yes** *(contradicts T104)* |
| **T574** | A pattern: 2 failures at the junction without breaking the end → junction breaks → target 50% of the seller leg | **Yes** |
| **T575** | Wick>wick and body-into-wick are not breaks; body beyond the prior wick is, then needs 3 outside (not inside/failed) candles | **Yes** |
| **T576** | Day bias is up only if price holds above the combined 1m/5m/15m 50% zone ~4–8 h | **Partly** — 4 vs 8 h unclear |
| **T577** | 3/10 entries go straight to profit, 7/10 go adverse first (baseline) | **Yes** — as an MAE statistic |
| **T578** | Candle control % = (lower wick + green body) vs (upper wick + red body) | **Yes** |
| **T579** | Apply candle tests only at the 50% line or inside a 25–50 / 75–100 boundary | **Partly** — a procedure |

## Video 078 — BTC review; how long a 5m read stays valid; re-entry after a breakout; small starting capital

He reviews the previous day's BTC calls and restates the trendline chain (see T555) and the 3-candle hold (T552). Most of the video is the rich-vs-retail narrative.

### T580 — HOW LONG A 5m READ STAYS VALID: CANDLE 15 MIN, PATTERN 1–3 H, TREND 4–8 H

*"A candle analysis on 5 minutes — analyse one 5-minute candle and you can find how the market will be for the next 15 minutes. Analyse one pattern on 5 minutes and you can see how the market will be for the next one to three hours. A trend on 5 minutes — how the market will be for the next four to eight hours."* (078 · 00:03:03–00:03:34)

This refines T92 with numbers for the 5m. It pairs with T554 for higher timeframes.

### T581 — A BREAKOUT THAT RETURNS INSIDE WITHIN 3 CANDLES GOES TO THE 50% LINE

*"When it goes to this boundary, it goes outside again; if it doesn't break this boundary and stays below — or even if it breaks — if within the next three candles it goes back, it will 100% come to this 50% line."* (078 · 00:01:03–00:01:31)

A push beyond a boundary that is back inside within 3 candles is a failed break. Price then travels to the range's 50%. This is the mirror of T552.

### T582 — START WITH MONEY YOU CAN AFFORD TO LOSE AND SCALE IT UP

*"'Small' means there is an amount you won't mind losing — for some it's 10 lakh, for some 10,000. Do it with that money, there's no hurry… make 10,000 into 20,000, then 50,000, then one lakh."* (078 · 00:53:54)

Even after training, begin live trading with an amount whose loss is tolerable and step it up. This is consistent with T46 (own capital only).

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T580** | A 5m read is valid ~15 min for a candle, 1–3 h for a pattern, 4–8 h for a trend | **Yes** |
| **T581** | A breakout back inside the boundary within 3 candles → price goes to that range's 50% | **Yes** |
| **T582** | Start live trading with expendable capital; scale 10k → 20k → 50k → 1L | **No** — a policy |

## Video 079 — BTC review on the 1m; fast versus slow candles

He mostly repeats the rules above (T552, T575, the four 50% lines of T567). He explains that the 1m is used because three candles confirm in three minutes, versus about an hour on the 15m (00:11:51). He restates the no-touch / touch / break sequence (T542, 00:35:42) and buying in a sell trend (T566, 00:23:29). The second half is motivational.

### T583 — FAST CANDLES DON'T GO FAR; SLOW SMALL CANDLES GO FAR

*"It will go fast, but it can't go fast for a long distance. But slow-moving candles — small small candles, watch them carefully — little by little they go a long distance, because there's no temptation there."* (079 · 00:30:09)
*"A fast candle is dangerous only if you chase it; for the rich man a fast candle is an opportunity."* (079 · 00:29:39–00:30:09)

This is a falsifiable claim about move persistence: legs made of large fast bars exhaust sooner than legs made of small bars. It fits the settle-after-climax idea in T544.

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T583** | Legs of large fast bars travel less far than legs of small slow bars | **Yes** |

## Video 080 — BTC after a week away: wide vs deep reading, greed↔fear symmetry, per-trade capital use

He sets out when to read wide and when to read deep. Wide means big timeframes, patterns and trend, used for "where will it go" and for investing. Deep means small timeframes and candles, used for "will it turn here" and for trading. He also covers:

- a symmetry claim between control of greed and control of fear;
- a heavily defended 25% line causing the 50% line to be skipped;
- how much of the account his trainees deploy per trade;
- buying as an asset versus selling as a contract.

### T584 — CONTROL OF GREED IS FOLLOWED BY AN EQUAL CONTROL OF FEAR

*"If there is control of fear in the market, 100% there will be control of greed too."* (080 · 00:04:36)
*"If there is a control of greed, there will be an equal control of fear. Know this well. As much night as there is, that much day."* (080 · 00:12:45)

A climax in one direction (T544) is paired with a comparable climax in the other. He states this twice in the video. It is falsifiable on size and timing.

### T585 — A 25% LINE DEFENDED MANY TIMES MEANS THE 50% IS SKIPPED → 75–100

*"Why didn't it respect [the 50]? Because the market already respected the 25 a lot. Sellers tried once, twice… a seventh time… couldn't even break it. So the buyer will break the distance they took and go consistently in control of greed… into the next zone, 75 to 100."* (080 · 00:35:40–00:36:11)

If a side makes many failed attempts (about 7) through the opposite side's 25% line without a body beyond its wick, expect the move to pass straight through the 50% into the 75–100 zone.

### T586 — PER-TRADE DEPLOYMENT IS AT MOST 15–20% OF CAPITAL

*"Someone from our training takes one lakh; at most 15,000 to 20,000 of it is used in the market. The remaining 80,000 just sits in hand — we park it in other income."* (080 · 00:18:22)
*"Not 15–20% for every trade — some trades use only 1,000–2,000, some 5,000–6,000; only some trade uses up to 15,000–20,000. Till today no one has used beyond 15–20."* (080 · 00:18:53–00:19:24)

This adds a per-trade ceiling to T6 (≤50% trading capital): peak deployment in any one campaign is ≤15–20% of the account, and typically 1–6%. He reports 5–17% returns over 30–40 trades on this basis (00:17:20–00:17:50).

### T587 — A BUY IS AN ASSET (LOSS CAPPED AT ZERO); A SELL IS A CONTRACT (LOSS UNLIMITED)

*"If you buy, your loss is at most — a stock at ₹100 can go down only to zero; the loss is limited. But a stock can go up from ₹100 to ₹1,000, 10,000 — for you [as a seller] the loss is unlimited. That's why the buy side is called an asset."* (080 · 00:15:48–00:16:18)

He treats short positions as contracts that exist only to protect a held asset. This is consistent with T47 and T79.

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T584** | A control-of-greed climax is followed by a comparable control-of-fear climax (and vice versa) | **Partly** — "equal" not defined |
| **T585** | ~7 failed attempts on the opposite 25% line → price skips the 50% and goes to 75–100 | **Yes** |
| **T586** | Peak deployment per trade ≤15–20% of the account (typically 1–6%) | **Yes** |
| **T587** | Longs are assets with capped loss; shorts are contracts with unlimited loss | **No** — a framing |

## Video 081 — BTC after 20 days: the six-line count, fuel-point timing, the bank benchmark

He reads monthly → 1m and introduces a count of 50% lines across three timeframes. He gives a fuel-point timing for the next day and restates that under value is not enough (a falling knife). The return hurdle is set against a bank deposit.

### T588 — SIX-LINE COUNT (4H / 1H / 15m, TWO 50% LINES EACH)

*"There should be six in total — we drew for three timeframes. Of the six, the market is standing above four lines, and it is going up toward the other two."* (081 · 01:02:00)
*"Above both of the 15-minute's price lines, so buy trend. Above one of the 1-hour's… above one of the 4-hour's… beyond four, heading to touch two."* (081 · 01:02:30)
*"Overall the buyer's involvement is higher… today's market trend is likely to be a buy trend."* (081 · 01:03:01)
*"There are two V patterns and one A pattern — the V is for the buyer, the A for the seller — so the buyer's control is greater; the market will still go up."* (082 · 00:51:34–00:52:04)

For each of 4H, 1H and 15m, draw the 50% of the completed leg and of the current leg (six lines). Count how many lie below price. Four or more below and price moving toward the rest = buy bias for the day. The 082 version counts patterns instead: the majority of V vs A across the three timeframes. Compare the weighted score in T559.

### T589 — FUEL POINT FIRST (4–8 H), THEN THE TREND (NEXT ~16 H)

*"The fuelling point of 5m, 15m and 1H is here. So today's market, in the first four to eight hours, will come to this place and then, after fuelling, go up for the next 16 hours."* (081 · 01:05:02)
*"It can turn without touching, turn after touching; it can put a wick beyond this, but it must not put a body beyond it. Even if a body, it must not maintain three candles. Otherwise today the market is 99.99% up."* (081 · 01:05:02–01:05:32)

For BTC's 24-hour session: price revisits the combined short-timeframe 50% "fuel point" in the first 4–8 hours, then trends for about 16 hours. The invalidation is a body beyond the fuel point held for 3 candles. *(Heard once.)*

### T590 — THE RETURN MUST BE ≥3× A BANK DEPOSIT

*"If you put one lakh in the bank you get about ₹600 a month; put the same lakh in here and you must earn at least ₹3,000 — only then is it worth coming to this business. In the bank the risk is very small; here the risk is about 30%."* (081 · 00:25:19)
*"At zero risk you earn 600; at 30% risk you must earn at least three times — 1,500–2,000 instead of 600; with brokerage and all, about 3,000. If you earn less than the bank, you are at a loss overall."* (081 · 00:25:49)

The monthly return on the whole account must be at least 3× (≈5× after costs) the bank rate. This pairs with T39/T41 (cost line) and T80.

### T591 — COINCIDING MONTHLY/WEEKLY/DAILY 50% LINES = CONTRACTION → BLAST

*"In the long-term timeframe the market is shrinking — you can see the 50% of all three is at one place… a particular price. The market is shrinking; when it shrinks it will blast one side."* (081 · 00:30:23–00:30:53)

When the 50% lines of M, W and D converge, the market is compressed and will break hard one way. This corroborates T104. It sits in tension with T553, where two coinciding 50% lines "are very hard to break".

He also restates, as corroboration, that under value alone is not a buy (00:20:48, "don't catch a falling knife with your tongue… see whether big money came"), which is T68. He repeats the claim that under value always goes to over value and back (00:42:42).

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T588** | Count the six 50% lines (4H/1H/15m × 2 legs); price above ≥4 and moving to the rest = buy bias for the day | **Yes** |
| **T589** | BTC: fuel point hit in the first 4–8 h, then ~16 h of trend; invalid if a body holds 3 candles beyond it | **Yes** *(heard once)* |
| **T590** | Monthly return must be ≥3× the bank rate (≈5× after costs) | **Partly** — a hurdle, not a signal |
| **T591** | Converging M/W/D 50% lines → compression → hard break one way | **Yes** *(tension with T553)* |

## Video 082 — BTC 1H→1m; the control-of-fear cascade; review of the 3→9 win ladder

He reviews the previous day's buy-bias call (T588), repeats buying the sell trend (T566) and the win-rate ladder (T577). He introduces base entry + support entries only by name (00:25:08; no numbers given). He describes a cascade of climaxes across timeframes. He mentions "ten questions you must answer before a trade" but never lists them (00:49:33).

### T592 — CONTROL-OF-FEAR CASCADE: 5m → 15m → 1H

*"If the market put three red candles on the 5 minute, it will put three red on the 15 minute and only then break that boundary and go to the next boundary. Likewise, if it put three red candles on the 15 minute, on the 1 hour it will put three big red candles, and then travel that boundary."* (082 · 00:35:46)

A 3-candle climax (T544) on one timeframe must be followed by a 3-candle climax on the next higher timeframe before the higher timeframe's boundary breaks. Without the higher-timeframe climax, expect a settle rather than a boundary break. This is testable and fits T23's 5m → 15m → 1H ladder.

| Rule | Statement | Testable as stated? |
|---|---|---|
| **T592** | A 3-bar climax on 5m must be followed by one on 15m (and then 1H) before that timeframe's boundary breaks | **Yes** |

---

## Most important and testable (my pick)

1. **T552 + T575 — break confirmation.** A break is a body close beyond the prior extreme wick, not a wick or a body inside the wick. It then needs three candles held beyond with outside, not inside/failed, bars. The return-through rate should be very low (he says 99.99%). This is the rule he applies in every video of the batch.
2. **T559 / T588 — multi-timeframe bias score.** Either the 70/50/30 weighted under/over-value score across a timeframe triplet, or the count of the six 50% lines (4H/1H/15m). Both give a daily directional bias that can be backtested directly.
3. **T554 + T580 — how long a read stays valid.** On the 5m: candle 15 min, pattern 1–3 h, trend 4–8 h. On the daily: candle 3–4 days, pattern ~3 weeks, boundary ~3 months. This turns T92 into concrete expiry times for signals.
